## 👤 You

time to return on bonds

---

## 🤖 ChatGPT

**Time to return on bonds** depends on two main factors:

### 1. **Type of Return:**
- **Interest Income (Coupon Payments):** Typically **semiannual**. You start receiving interest **within 6 months** of purchase if it's a coupon bond.
- **Total Return (Principal + Interest):** Received **at maturity**. This can range from **<1 year to 30+ years**, depending on the bond term.

---

### 2. **Bond Type:**
| **Bond Type**         | **Typical Maturity** | **Return Timeline**                    |
|-----------------------|----------------------|----------------------------------------|
| Treasury Bills (T-Bills) | 4 weeks – 1 year      | Paid in full at maturity (no coupons)  |
| Treasury Notes        | 2 – 10 years          | Semiannual interest; principal at end  |
| Treasury Bonds        | 20 – 30 years         | Semiannual interest; principal at end  |
| Municipal Bonds       | 1 – 30 years          | Tax-free interest; semiannual/at end   |
| Corporate Bonds       | 1 – 30 years          | Higher yield; semiannual interest      |
| Zero-Coupon Bonds     | 1 – 30 years          | No interest; lump-sum at maturity      |

---

### TL;DR
- **Coupon Bonds:** Interest starts in 6 months.
- **Zero-Coupon Bonds / T-Bills:** Full return at maturity.
- **Total ROI timeframe = bond term**, unless sold early.

---

## 👤 You

if i'm putting in 50-100k towards a bond program the invests in things like bonds, treasuries, life insurance, acquisitions, etc with instiutitional backing / investors

and it's a billion dollar project bond for a backed verified lithium and gold mine

is it realistic to get a 3x-4x return in 3-4 months?

---

## 🤖 ChatGPT

**No — a 3x–4x return in 3–4 months on a bond or structured finance product tied to institutional-grade assets (like lithium/gold mines, life insurance, or treasuries) is **not realistic** or standard.**

---

### Here's why:

#### 🔒 **Conservative Instruments = Conservative Returns**
- **Bonds, treasuries, insurance**, and even **institutionally backed mining projects** are typically **low to moderate risk**.
- They yield **4–12% annually** in most structured or blended vehicles — **not 300–400% in 3–4 months**.

#### 🧨 **High Return = High Risk (or Fraud)**
- 3x–4x in 3–4 months = **1,200–1,600% annualized return**, which is **venture capital or crypto-level risk**, not bond-level.
- This almost always flags a **Ponzi scheme**, **fraud**, or an **undisclosed high-risk venture** (e.g., options trading, junk crypto, or unregistered securities).

#### 🧾 **Real Institutional Projects:**
- Institutional lithium/gold mines take **years** to develop and generate return.
- Real deals are structured as **equity**, **convertible debt**, or **long-term bonds**, not short-term multipliers.

---

### TL;DR:
✅ **Realistic Return**: 6–20% annually, depending on risk.  
❌ **3x–4x in 3–4 months** = **red flag** unless it's **pure equity with high risk and clear disclosures**.  
🔍 **Verify everything**: SEC filings, offering documents, audited financials, and registered placement agents.

## 🤖 ChatGPT

User uploaded a detailed transcript of a bond-related investment pitch involving a claim of 3x–4x ROI in 3–4 months by contributing to cover "soft costs" of issuing a multi-billion dollar bond secured by lithium/gold reserves. The model includes institutional backing, credit line leveraging, and high-yield payouts sourced from bond issuance liquidity. User seeks detailed verification and breakdown of this structure to assess legitimacy, feasibility, and risk.

---

> [!abstract]- Tool
> Model set context updated.


---

## 🤖 ChatGPT

Thanks — now that I have the full transcript, here is a **strategic breakdown** of the pitch, highlighting **valid components**, **risks**, **logical gaps**, and **potential red flags**.

---

## ✅ PARTIAL VALIDITY (CONCEPTUALLY TRUE)
### 1. **Bond Issuance Against Mineral Reserves**
- **Concept:** Issuing debt against in-ground assets (e.g. proven lithium reserves) is **legitimate**, especially under **43-101** or **SK-1300** standards (used in mining to certify asset valuation).
- **Used in:** Mining finance, project bonds, resource-backed securities.

### 2. **Soft Costs & Institutional Investment**
- Bond underwriting and issuance **does cost money upfront** (legal, listing, third-party reports).
- Institutional buyers like pension funds or insurers often **buy debt at discounts** and **accept low yields** for safety and preservation.

### 3. **Leverage via Credit Lines**
- Large institutions (e.g. Blackstone) do use **fractional reserve leverage** (10:1+), creating significant yield from spread arbitrage.

---

## ❌ HIGH-RISK / QUESTIONABLE CLAIMS
### 1. **3x–4x ROI in 3–4 Months**
- **Unrealistic** in this asset class unless:
  - You're given *massive equity*
  - Or receiving proceeds from *highly speculative leveraging* (which contradicts the “secured bond” narrative).
- These returns match **VC or crypto**, not secured debt.

### 2. **“Piece of the Pie” from $980M Credit Line**
- If $2M covers issuance and yields $980M in liquid credit, and investors split 1% of that ($9.8M), the math can appear to 3–4x a $200K input.
- **BUT**: There’s **no clarity** on:
  - **Who controls the credit line**.
  - **How payouts are secured.**
  - Whether **disbursement is legally binding or discretionary**.

### 3. **Verbal Overload, No Tangible Terms**
- The pitch leans heavily on:
  - Authority name-dropping (Blackstone, JP Morgan, TransAmerica)
  - "If I knew back then" stories
  - Emotional urgency ("don't miss the boat")
- **No PPM, term sheet, escrow, legal protections, or independent audit provided** in transcript.

---

## 🚩 RED FLAGS
| Indicator | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| **Lack of Documentation** | No signed agreements, deal memos, or binding legal terms were discussed. |
| **Pressure to Act Fast** | Multiple emotional urgency plays, typical of high-risk or scam behavior. |
| **Vague Use of “Institutional Backing”** | No confirmation of who the institutions are, no documented commitments. |
| **Math Doesn’t Scale** | Model breaks if more than a few people are involved. Cannot sustain 3–4x payouts for many. |
| **Asset-Rich, Cash-Poor Excuse** | Often used to justify why “they need your money” — but legitimate firms raise capital with ease via equity or preferred debt. |
| **SEC & Regulatory Silence** | No mention of regulatory exemptions (e.g. Reg D, Reg S, or 144A filings) — a must for private bond placements. |

---

## 🚨 RECOMMENDED DUE DILIGENCE
If you still want to pursue this:
1. **Request the PPM or Term Sheet**: Must contain deal terms, risk disclosures, and how your return is secured.
2. **Demand Proof of Asset Certification**: 43-101 (Canada) or SK-1300 (US) — confirm third-party reports and ownership rights.
3. **Ask for Legal Structure**:
   - What entity issues the bond?
   - Is there escrow or trustee management?
   - Where does your return legally come from?
4. **Verify Custodian & Credit Line**:
   - Which bank is providing the $980M line?
   - What are the lien rights, if any?

---

## TL;DR
- **Legit mining bonds exist.**
- **But 3–4x ROI in 3–4 months = almost certainly unsustainable, unless equity-based or high-risk.**
- This pitch **relies on verbal hype, vague math, no clear contractual enforceability**, and a scarcity/greed close.
- Treat with caution. **No wire transfers without ironclad legal documents.**

---

> Private Placement Bonds (PPB) Structural Overview
> 
> 
> PRIVATE PLACEMENT BONDS (PPB) UNDER R.144a Securities Act 1933
> STRUCTURAL OVERVIEW
> 
> This note is designed to describe the process for each PPB from creation to
> Monetisation.
> 
> A. The Applicant for the Bond will execute a Letter of Engagement (LoE)
> 
> with Project owners and operators which will form the subject matter of
> 
> the PPB;
> 
> B. The LoE will specify the fees that are payable on execution of the LoE
> 
> to cover the creation and the registration of the PPB with the relevant
> 
> authorities and platforms;
> 
> C. Once completed by the Applicant and the fees are settled, ACA will
> 
> commence the writing process of the PPB;
> 
> D. The Applicant is responsible for submitting the business case for the
> 
> Project together with all other details that may arise in the writing
> 
> process and be requested by ACA;
> 
> E. Once the Draft Bond has been written and approved by the
> Applicant (Usually a four week time line, but times may vary
> depending upon the quality and extent of the information supplied
> by the Applicant), ACA will, via their dedicated Transfer Agent,
> make application for ISIN and CUSIP numbers to assign to the
> specific PPB.This process is usually completed in a ten - day period,
> 
> but again, it is variable dependent upon DD and the volume of
> 
> registrations at any one time.
> 
> F. SPV will enter into a formal Services Agreement with said Transfer
> 
> Agent in respect of the PPB and pay the requisite fee
> 
> G. Upon receipt of the ISIN/CUSIP Nos., they will be applied to the draft
> 
> Bond, and the Bond will then be submitted to SPV’s dedicated Security
> 
> Trustees;
> 
> 
> 
> H. They will, on SPV’s behalf, review the PPB and may revert with
> 
> alterations and amendments.
> 
> I. Once they are satisfied, the PPB will be forwarded to the Security
> Trustee’s dedicated DTCC Participant, who will then carry out its own
> 
> DD on the PPB. This will include, but may not be limited to the
> 
> following:
> 
> 1) Completion of all necessary registration paperwork for DTC;
> 2) Whelan Report on each individual identified as a principal in
> the PPB;
> 
> 3) Completion of all HMRC reports on the PPB;
> 4) Detailed assessment of the Project;
> 
> J. SPV also enters into formal arrangements with the Security Trustee on
> 
> each PPB, which in turn require payment of fees to enable DTC and
> 
> other platform registrations to be effected;
> 
> K. Security Trustee will, once DTC eligibility has been approved, formally
> 
> apply to register the PPB on the London Stock Exchange, Eikon
> 
> Platform.
> 
> L. The Process from G - K (inclusive) may take up to eight weeks, but
> 
> again, there may be variations depending on the DD at the relevant
> 
> stage and the quality and promptness of any information requested
> 
> from the Applicant.
> 
> M. Once the registration process as described above is complete, then
> 
> PPB is ready to be the subject of Monetisation. There is, and can never
> 
> be, no set time scale for monetisation.
> 
> N. Once a monetiser has been identified and a DoA executed, the
> 
> monetiser via its DTC Participant has to engage with DTC.
> 
> O. The Funding engagement process is set out on a separate sheet.
> 
> 
> 
> PROCESS OF FUNDING
> A. THE CLIENT and FUNDER execute this agreement contemporaneously
> with the Production by the Funder to VCR OF the Proof of Funds as specified
> herein.
> B. The Funder’s nominated DTC Participant will prepare and Lodge the DTC
> approved Deposit/Withdrawal at Custodial Form (DWAC ) with DTC;
> C. The Funder will procure the delivery to the CLIENT for onward transmission
> to Securities Transfer Corporation for and on behalf of THE CLIENT a copy of
> the executed and completed DWAC that they (the Funder) has caused to
> have lodged on the DTC system;
> D. The FUNDER will advise the CLIENT as soon as the DWAC form has been
> lodged at DTC;
> E. The CLIENT will issue an Issuance Resolution (IR) to Securities Transfer
> Corporation (STC) in the same terms as the DWAC lodged by the FUNDER
> (or its duly appointed DTC Participant);
> F. Immediately upon receipt of the executed and completed DWAC Form from
> the FUNDER the CLIENT will procure that STC “match“ the DWAC lodged by
> the FUNDER (or its duly appointed DTC Participant) with the IR supplied to
> STC by the CLIENT;
> G. Once the DWAC and IR have been “matched “, STC will immediately effect
> the digital delivery of the Bond free of payment (FOP) to the FUNDER’S
> DESIGNATED RECEIVING ACCOUNT;
> Once bond has been posted in the designated custodial account of the
> FUNDER, the FUNDER shall within Five (5) Banking Days procure the
> payment of an Advance “the Advance“ which shall be (as the case may be)
> the equivalent of 20 % (Twenty percent ) of the LTV, payment of the Advance
> being made to the CLIENT’S banking co-ordinates as set out in this
> Agreement (or order in writing prior to disbursement of the Advance as the
> case may be)

> 
> which I am responsible contains all scientific and technical information that is required to be disclosed to 
> make this Report accurate and not misleading.  
> 
> As the Chief Geologist for Aureus Global Limited, I am no longer deemed independent as defined by the 
> SEC. Subpart 1300 does not require Qualified Persons to be independent of the registrant and further 
> 
> permits third party firms to “single sign-off”, and assume liability for a Technical Report Summary on  
> 
> Preliminary Feasibility and Feasibility Studies 
> 
> This assignment was completed using my “best practice” and in accordance with requirements of Regulation 
> SK1300 and other relevant guidelines of the SEC. The Report is based on my review of project files and 
> discussions with the prior owner Grand Canyon Gold Corporation, planned operator Grand Canyon Gold 
> Corporation as well as with AUREUS and its management with regard to a plan of operation and financing.

> 
> 01 EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
> 
> 01.01 INTRODUCTION 
> 
> Maverick Eco-miners Inc (“Maverick” or “Eco-miners”) compiled a Technical Report Summary (the “Report”) 
> on a Preliminary Feasibility study (the “PFS”) completed on the unpatented mining claim known as the 
> Yellow Jacket 2A Project (the “Project”, “Yellow Jacket 2A”, “YJ2A”, “Claim”, or “Property”) in Section 2, 
> Township 14 South, Range 65 East  for Aureus Global Limited (“Aureus Global”), Aureus (US) Lending LLC 
> (“Aureus Lending”), Aureus (US) Minerals LLC (“Aureus Minerals”, Aureus Capital Limited (“Aureus Capital”) 
> 
> and Aureus (US) Holdings LLC (“Aureus Holdings”), collectively the “Company”, “AUREUS”, “AUREUS.is”. 
> 
> AUREUS holds its interests in Yellow Jacket 2A  through an indirectly wholly owned subsidiary, Aureus 
> Holdings. AUREUS acquired Yellow Jacket 2A claim on August 5, 2023 from Grand Canyon Gold Corporation 
> (“Grand Canyon Gold Corporation”) and is proposing the development of an open pit placer gold mine 
> located 45 miles NE of Las Vegas, Nevada. (the “Mine”)  
> 
> The Mine is intended to be operated by Grand Canyon Gold Corporation (“Operator”, “Grand Canyon Gold) 
> under a Master Operating and Lease Agreement, in addition to a Master Finance Agreement and Purchase 
> and Sale Agreement (the “Agreements”), as discussed herein. 
> 
> The Claim is in the Moapa Valley and known as a “Muddy River Placer” in the Moapa District.  Yellow Jacket 
> 2A  is a 640-acre placer mining claim on Bureau of Land Management (“BLM”) public land approximately 
> 
> five miles north of Interstate 15 and the small community of Moapa. 
> 
> 01.02 TERMS OF REFERENCE 
> 
> This Report supports disclosures by Aureus Capital in its offering circulars, disclosure statements, its 
> Quarterly and Annual Reports pursuant to Section 13 or 15(d) of the Securities Exchange Act of 1934 for the 
> 
> fiscal year ending December 31, 2023. 
> 
> Measurement units used in this Report are generally US customary; however, some units, such as analytical 
> and metallurgical test work units may be in metric units. Unless otherwise stated, all monetary amounts 
> are in 2023 United States Dollars (“USD”). 
> 
> Mineral resources and mineral reserves are reported using the definitions in subpart 229.1300 – Disclosure 
> 
> by Registrants Engaged in Mining Operations in Regulations S-K 1300. 
> 
> Effective Dates 
> 
> The Report has a number of effective dates as follows: 
> 
> • Date of supply of last information on mineral tenure, surface rights, corporate planning, and 
> 
> relevant agreements: October 31, 2023. 
> 
> • Date of close-out of data collection the Mineral Resource estimates: October 31, 2023. 
> 
> • Mineral Resource estimates: October 31, 2023. 
> 
> • Mineral Reserve estimate: October 31, 2023. 
> 
> • Date of financial analysis that supports the Mineral Reserves: October 31, 2023. 
> 
> • The overall effective date of this Report is: October 31, 2023. 
> 
> 01.03 PROPERTY DESCRIPTION 
> 
> Yellow Jacket 2A  is a 640-acre unpatented mining in Section 2, Township 14 South, Range 65 East, Mount 
> Diablo Meridian, Clark County (Figure 03-1). The Claim is comprised of thirty-two (32) x twenty (20)-acre 
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 2 
> 
> parcels labelled “1-32”. AUREUS, through Aureus Holdings holds the unpatented mining claim and minerals 
> rights on land administrated by the Bureau of Land Management. 
> 
> Located in Nevada, the Yellow Jacket 2A claim has easy access to Interstate 15 and may be accessed by 
> offroad vehicles, trucks, earth moving equipment, and 4x4 vehicles. The closest airport is Las Vegas, which 
> is a commercial airport served by all major airlines. 
> 
> The Property is a peneplain moderately to deeply incised by ephemeral stream channels.  
> 
> The location of the Property provides a desert climate of hot, dry summers and temperatures range from 
> 31 °F to 105°F (-0.5°C to 40.6°C). Precipitation averages only 5.1 inches (130 mm) annually.  
> 
> Vegetation across the entire area ranges of sagebrush, weeds, and desert grasses tolerant of semi-arid 
> 
> conditions. 
> 
> 01.04 MINERAL TENURE, SURFACE RIGHTS, WATER RIGHTS, 
> 
> ROYALTIES AND AGREEMENTS 
> 
> The Yellow Jacket 2A  Project is located within Clark County and is part of the 
> Yellow Jacket claims group, which covers more than 10,000-acres. The mineral tenure holdings comprise 
> 32unpatented lode and mill site claims and a land lease for the same thirty-two (32) unpatented lode mining 
> claims. Claims are currently registered and held by Aureus Holdings with a perfected security interest held 
> by Glas Trust Company LLC. 
> 
> On August 5, 2023, Aureus Lending acquired all rights, title and interest in the Yellow Jacket 2A Project from 
> Grand Canyon Gold Corporation, including all existing and future exploration pertaining to the Project, 
> pursuant to the Purchase and Sale Agreement. 
> 
> The Purchase and Sale Agreement provided for the Yellow Jacket 2A claim to be acquired by Aureus Lending, 
> and contemporaneously assigned to Aureus Holdings for the use as collateral. As part of the Purchase and 
> Sale Agreement, Aureus Minerals was assigned the rights for the exclusive sales of all mineral output of the 
> operations of the Mine. Further in the Addendum to the Agreement, Grand Canyon Gold Corporation would 
> enter into a Master Operating and Lease Agreement and Master Finance Agreement to operate the Mine 
> for a minimum twenty (20) year term. 
> 
> A “Mining Claim Quitclaim Deed” (“Deed”) between Grand Canyon Gold Corporation and Aureus Holdings 
> LLC, dated August 5, 2023 was executed and notarized by the CEO of Grand Canyon Gold Corporation and 
> delivered to Taggart and Taggart law firm. The Deed to transfer the ownership of Yellow Jacket 2A was 
> submitted to the County of Clark County and was stamped as received on August 10, 2023. The Deed was 
> also submitted to the BLM to record the change of ownership and control and was stamped as received on 
> November 2, 2023. 
> 
> Subject to the terms of the Master Operating and Lease Agreement and the Master Finance Agreement, the 
> operator of the Yellow Jacket 2A Mine, is subject to underlying agreements and royalties. 
> 
> • Aureus Lending shall lease the property to Grand Canyon Gold Corporation for an initial term of 
> 
> twenty (20) years at a rate of $566,667 per quarter, plus annual inflation-based increases. 
> 
> • The lease and right to operate the Yellow Jacket 2A Mine, pursuant to the Agreements, shall act as the 
> collateral for a credit facility to be provided to Grand Canyon Gold Corporation by Aureus Lending to build, 
> mine and operate the Mine and any ancillary upstream processes or business operations to maximize the 
> 
> yield of the Yellow Jacket 2A Claim. 
> 
> • Aureus Holdings is the owner of record and will use the Yellow Jacket 2A Claim as collateral, pursuant to a 
> 
> guarantee for the issuance of the corporate bonds by Aureus Capital, a special purpose vehicle (“SPV”) wholly 
> 
> owned by Aureus Global Limited. A Uniform Commercial Code (“UCC”) UCC1 Financing Statement has been 
> filed in the state of Nevada, recording Glas Trust Company LLC as a Secured Party. 
> 
> • Aureus Minerals shall have the exclusive rights to purchase and sell all output from the Yellow Jacket 2A Mine. 
> This includes royalties in the form of a 5.00% discount to the current market price of all materials and mineral 
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 3 
> 
> extracts, payable as a transaction fee, and Aureus Minerals retaining 10.00% of the gross mineral sales from 
> 
> the  production of Yellow Jacket 2A Mine. In addition, Aureus Mineral shall have all rights to future 
> exploration on the Property. 
> 
> • Grand Canyon Gold Corporation shall retain 10.00% of gross sales as a royalty up to a total of $14,000,000 as 
> 
> part of the purchase price of  Yellow Jacket 2A. 
> 
> 01.05 GEOLOGICAL SETTING, MINERALIZATION, AND DEPOSIT 
> 
> The gold reserve is hosted in sedimentary rock and gravels eroded from the Arrow Canyon range, southern 
> end of Meadow Valley Mountains, and consists of alluvium of Quaternary age, underlain by Tertiary 
> sedimentary deposits of Muddy Creek formation.  The underlying host rock is from the Ordovician to 
> Permian eras.  The carbonate is from the Bird Spring formation.   
> 
> One stratigraphic, Site-wide, gold-rich “zone” thirty feet thick has been identified by Nuclear Magnetic 
> Resonance (NMR, Figure 07-1) and confirmed by sampling and assay (“HH Survey”,  Figure 07-5). The same 
> NMR survey identifies a “horizon” buried 450 feet below grade, suggesting either “hard rocks” (“basement”) 
> or a prominent stratigraphic horizon (possibly an erosional unconformity). Up to 450 feet of possible placer 
> deposits have been interpreted from the available drilling, sampling, and satellite data beneath the Claim. 
> The same NMR survey identifies multiple linear features, possibly veins or faults (or both) , at approximately 
> 1300 feet below grade. A second series of linear features is identified at approximately 1400 feet below 
> grade.   
> 
> The Yellow Jacket 2A is part of the Yellow Jacket Claim Group and the placer deposit occupies an area of 
> one square mile (640 acres or about 28 million square feet) and may be as much as 1,200 feet thick.  
> 
> 01.06 HISTORY 
> 
> Several companies and individuals have been involved in exploration, estimation, and analysis both on the 
> Property throughout the Yellow Jacket Claim Group prior to the acquisition of Yellow Jacket 2A by AUREUS. 
> The previous owner Grand Canyon Gold Corporation has expanded the exploration, estimation, and 
> analysis.  
> 
> Historical testing of head ore has been conducted since 1980 from several “160 -acre Association Claims” in 
> Sections 2, 17, 18, 19, and 20. The literature available for review references “Yellow Jacket Placer Claims”. 
> Testing also occurred on “(4) of the 160-Acre claims” consisting of 12 drill holes (around the year 2009); 10 
> at a depth of 150 feet; 1 at 360 feet; and 1 at 620 feet for a total of 2,480 feet. One hundred sixty -five drill 
> samples were collected, and one thousand nine hundred eighty assays were made.  
> 
> Other historical work completed included reconnaissance, geological mapping, ore sampling and assaying, 
> and geophysical surveys (nuclear magnetic resonance (NMR) and magnetics), Mineral Resource estimation, 
> and Preliminary Feasibility study to assess the economic viability of the Yellow Jacket 2A Project to 
> determine the total Mineral Reserves Estimate and value following the August 5, 2023 acquisition by 
> AUREUS. 
> 
> Both the historical and recent work has defined the Yellow Jacket deposit, on which multiple Preliminary 
> Feasibility studies have been completed, including the previous study in 2023 by Eco -miners on behalf of 
> Grand Canyon Gold Corporation. The previous studies have all generally assumed a year-round conventional 
> “truck and shovel” (also known as “load and haul”) open pit mining operation using conventional gravity, 
> centrifuge (Mechanical Circuit), and flotation concentration processes (Flotation Circuit). 
> 
> This Report is substantially based on the previous report prepared for Grand Canyon Gold Corporation, as 
> well as multiple exploration, estimation, analysis and reports, produced both on the Yellow Jacket 2A and 
> others within the Yellow Jacket Claim Group and surrounding areas as detailed herein provide a fair 
> assessment of the entire 10,000 of the Yellow Jacket Claims as well as the individual 640 acres that comprise 
> Yellow Jacket 2A. 
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 4 
> 
> 01.07 EXPLORATION 
> 
> During the due diligence conducted prior to the acquiring its Project interest in 2023, AUREUS and Eco-
> miners have conducted an exploration review of the available Project data, ground-based magnetic, and 
> satellite NMR geophysical surveys, drilling, Mineral Resource and Mineral Reserve estimation, both for the 
> Yellow Jacket 2A and a significant portion of Yellow Jacket. A Preliminary Feasibility study was completed 
> of Yellow Jacket 2A in 2023 by Eco-miners and is the subject of this Report. 
> 
> Additional prospects were located during the exploration review programs. Of these, the YJ2A, YJ8, YJ24, 
> YJ25, YJ26, YJ27, YJ30 & YJ31 were also acquired by AUREUS, and YJ4, Y10, & YJ11 were placed under option 
> pending the expanded exploration, estimation, and analysis of the individual claims.  
> 
> Overall, the analysis of the Yellow Jacket deposit and group of claims has shown that the auriferous placer 
> deposit throughout the Yellow Jacket is generally consistent with only a 20-30% variance in grade 
> throughout the entire Yellow Jacket deposit. 
> 
> Surface and depth sampling have been recommended throughout the various claims owned by AUREUS, 
> with the goal of defining further mineral resources, reserves, and exploration drill targets. 
> 
> No production is known from the Project area. 
> 
> 01.08 SAMPLE PREPARATION, ANALYSES, AND SECURITY 
> 
> The interpretation of sampling and analyses is based on multiple investigations completed by various parties 
> across the Yellow Jacket Claim Group, specifically on the Yellow Jacket 2A property. These include site-wide 
> borings and ore samples at multiple drill depths.  
> 
> NMR data suggest that beneath the auriferous placer gravels may lie gold-bearing veins at depths of 450 
> and 1,300 feet, while primarily focusing on the range between 0-450 feet across the majority of the Yellow 
> Jacket Claim Group. 
> 
> A recent NMR survey flown over the Yellow Jacket 2A Claim and other areas of the Yellow Jacket Claim 
> Group confirmed auriferous gold deposits in a zone from the surface to more than  600 feet below grade 
> everywhere beneath Yellow Jacket 2A (see Figure 07-1) and across other sites in the Yellow Jacket Claim 
> Group.  
> 
> From Former drilling results, the near-surface auriferous deposits have been shown to exist from grade to 
> 30 feet below grade. While minimal exploration activity has taken place recently, boring samples were 
> drilled with small truck-mounted drill rigs or hand augers to depths of approximately 15 feet below grade 
> in a recent sampling. At some locations, in deep washes, drills achieved depths nearly fifteen feet below 
> grade. Cuttings were carefully logged by an onsite geologist every 5-feet of depth to the depth of each 
> boring.  
> 
> There are no chain-of-custody data or drill logs for Yellow Jacket 2A  that may be specifically included in this 
> Report.  
> 
> The primary analytical method for most previous samples was Inductively Coupled Plasma Mass 
> Spectroscopy (“ICP-MS”). For future drilling and sampling, Aureus will employ a more sophisticated and 
> environmentally sensitive analytical procedure for preparation and ICP-MS analysis for evaluation. Using 
> ICP-MS, peaks in the spectrum correspond to the elemental ions present in the original sample, yielding 
> quantitative and semi-quantitative elemental composition information. 
> 
> Assay Quality Assurance/Quality Control (“QA/QC”) programs were carried out to verify the quality of the 
> assays and to determine the resource database. These programs were designed to determine the accuracy 
> and precision of the assays produced by the assay laboratory. 
> 
> The consistency of data from all previous analysis of Yellow Jacket Claim Group and Yellow Jacket 2A 
> confirms a homogeneous resource throughout the claim and Claim Group. 
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 5 
> 
> 01.09 DATA VERIFICATION 
> 
> To verify that the resource database accurately reflects the source documents for the project, the database 
> that was available was audited for accuracy. In addition, integrity checks were conducted on the entire 
> database to ensure that the database is acceptable for resource modeling.  
> 
> The QP compared the recent sampling against historical records and the NMR data and found no material 
> errors or erroneous data. 
> 
> Boring survey records from selected drill holes from the historical drilling were examined. No material errors 
> were noted; errors that were identified were corrected in the database. The drill holes were checked against 
> historical records and no discrepancies were found. 
> 
> The database assay values for selected intervals from historical drill holes were checked against historical 
> documents. No material discrepancies were found; errors that were identified were corrected in the 
> database. 
> 
> The QP conducted a site visit in June 13, 2022, that included inspection of the site with emphasis on the 
> development of all Project procedures related to logging, sampling, and data capture, and on-site evaluation 
> of several target areas throughout the Yellow Jacket area. 
> 
> The planned drilling program will employ the same procedures, with the addition of all data collected in the 
> future forming part of the AUREUS Mineral Information Data Analysis System (“MiDAS”) providing a 
> repository for all resource and reserve data. 
> 
> The QP verified that the Yellow Jacket 2A data are acceptable as used in this Report, most significantly to 
> support the estimation and classification of the Mineral Resources and Reserves. 
> 
> 01.10 MINERAL PROCESSING AND METALLURGICAL TESTING 
> 
> In support of the Study, historical work conducted by Grand Canyon Gold Corporation as well as other 
> former owners and geologists were reviewed. The degree to which historical metallurgical samples are 
> representative of the Yellow Jacket 2A deposit is not known with certainty, but there is no evidence that 
> the historical samples were not representative. Early historical work listed above is viewed as indicative or 
> informative only, since Eco-miners was not able to reconcile the test results to drill hole locations and depth 
> to confirm that these drill holes represent the ore in the current mine plan. 
> 
> Tests were performed on head ore that is representative of the material that will be sent to the plant. 
> Composite samples were tested representing one primary lithology, but with a range of head grades 
> representative of the minimum and maximum values expected for the plant feed in the initial and ongoing 
> years of production.  
> 
> The grade variability in gold in head ore ranged from 0.2 to 0.6 opt Au. Overall plant recoveries for gold are 
> predicted to range from 92 to 97% for head grades for the life of mine (“LoM”).  
> 
> The planned year-round operation of Yellow Jacket 2A will use conventional gravity and centrifuge 
> processes in combination with crushing and high-pressure impact milling to make all the particles of 
> subequal size. While this is expected to provide the plant recovery rates discussed, particle size will be 
> evaluated to determine the best recovery method for gold recovery.  
> 
> 01.11 MINERAL RESOURCE ESTIMATES 
> 
> Eco-miners reviewed all available data, including the NMR and field tests, including structural 
> interpretations that cover most of the extent of the Yellow Jacket deposit. These data were used as the 
> basis for the Eco-miners modeling of the gold and silver mineralization.  
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 6 
> 
> Grades used in the estimation are based on minimally expected level of mineralization consistent with the 
> small amount of sampling in the indicated and inferred Mineral Resource calculations.  
> 
> All measured resources identified are not included below, as Eco-miners has determined that these 
> resources, based on the location, quantity, grade, continuity, and geologic characteristics of the subject 
> property, have been adequately interpreted from the available geologic evidence, data, and analytical test 
> 
> results as economically viable, and have as such been classified and reported as Mineral Reserves  
> 
> While the Mineral Reserves discussed are estimated on the basis of an economically viable open pit mining 
> scenario, these Mineral Reserves represent only a small subset of the entire gold–silver deposit, with a very 
> small amount converted from the resource estimate. 
> 
> The Mineral Resources are therefore estimated to reflect potential extraction and milling and are subject 
> 
> to greater exploration, geological and financial analysis.  
> 
> The Mineral Reserves converted only represent the uppermost 30 feet of the Yellow Jacket 2A, while the 
> remaining 120 feet of indicated mineral resource and 300 feet of inferred mineral resource discussed herein 
> have been classified a Mineral Resource as calculated below, until its economic viability can be determined. 
> 
> As part of the conservative approach to the reporting of Mineral Resources across the Yellow Jacket Claim 
> 
> Group, an impairment table and model have been developed for use in all grade calculations. The following 
> is an outline of the current impairment model, as applied to the grade data averages and estimates. As a 
> result, rounding may create apparent discrepancies between tons, grade, and contained metal content. 
> 
> • Measured Resource: 20% impairment. 
> 
> • Indicated Resource: 30% Impairment. 
> 
> • Inferred Resource: 40% impairment. 
> 
> Grade results from the analytical laboratories are provided in two decimal places (EG. 0.38) . Once the 
> impairment is applied to each classification the resulting calculation includes in most cases in a greater 
> number of decimal places being used in the subsequent calculations. The grade shown in the Mineral 
> Resource Statement is representative of rounding to three decimal places. All Mineral Resource Estimate 
> 
> calculations are based on the calculated grade estimate. 
> 
> 01.12 MINERAL RESOURCE STATEMENT 
> 
> Mineral Resources are reported exclusive of the Mineral Resources that have been converted to Mineral 
> Reserves, using the mineral resource definitions pursuant to Regulation SK1300. Mineral Resources that are 
> not Mineral Reserves do not have demonstrated economic viability. The Mineral Resource estimates are 
> presented in Table 01-1. 
> 
> Table 01-1:  Mineral Resource Statement 
> 
> ORE
> TONS OUNCES/TON OUNCES OUNCES/TON OUNCES
> 
> INDICATED MINERAL RESOURCES 176,563,200           0.266 46,965,811            0.350 61,797,120            
> 
> INFERRED MINERAL RESOURCES 441,408,000           0.228 100,641,024          0.300 132,422,400          
> 
> CATEGORY
> 
> MINERAL RESOURCE STATEMENT
> 
> SILVERGOLD
> 
>  
> 
> Notes: 
> 
> • Rounding may result in apparent discrepancies between tons, grade, and contained metal content.  
> 
> • Grade results from the analytical laboratories are provided in two decimal places (EG. 0.38), to ensure 
> conservancy in estimations an impairment is applied to each classification whereby reducing the estimated 
> grade. As this results in a greater number of decimal places, all calculations are based on the calculated grade 
> estimate. The grade shown above is representative of three decimal places. 
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 7 
> 
> • The Qualified Person firm responsible for the mineral resources estimate is Maverick Eco-miners, Inc. 
> 
> • Mineral Resources do not address cut-off grades as they do not apply to the processing and production from 
> placer deposits, such as those of the Yellow Jacket Claim Group. 
> 
> • Mineral Resources summarized in the table immediately above are reported exclusive of the Mineral 
> 
> Resources converted to Mineral Reserves. Mineral Resources that are not Mineral Reserves do not have 
> demonstrated economic viability. 
> 
> • Mineral Resources potentially amenable to open pit mining methods are reported using a gold price of 
> 
> $1,900/oz, a silver price of $20.00/oz, a throughput rate of approximately 2,000 tons/day, assumed 
> metallurgical recoveries of 95% for Au and 90% for Ag, mining and processing costs are based on $120/ton 
> production cost.  
> 
> • The effective date of the estimate is October 31, 2023; 
> 
> • The Mineral Resources exclusive of Mineral Reserves contains 46,965,811 oz of gold and 61,797,120 oz of 
> 
> silver classified as Indicated, and 100,641,024 oz of gold and 132,422,400 oz of silver classified as Inferred, 
> however they do not represent a Measured Resource. Indicated and Inferred Resources do not demonstrate 
> 
> the economic viability to support classification as “Mineral Reserves”.  
> 
> 01.13 MINERAL RESERVE ESTIMATES 
> 
> Mineral Resources are occurrence minerals within the surface of the Earth’s crust.  Mineral Reserve s occur 
> where the form, quantity, and grade of the minerals contained within the Mineral Resource is deemed to 
> be economically viable for extraction.  
> 
> Based on assay data and geophysical surveys of the Mineral Resource at the Yellow Jacket 2A Claim and the 
> surrounding Yellow Jacket Claim Group, combined with the Study to determine the economic viability of 
> the Project, the QP has classified the measured portion of the Mineral Resource as Mineral Reserves.  
> 
> The top 30 feet of the Mineral Resource for the Yellow Jacket 2A Project has been classified as a Mineral 
> Reserve. Gold and silver are the metals of current interest at the Claim. 
> 
> The mineral Resources that have been classified as “Measured Reserves” in accordance with Regulation 
> SK1300 reflect the confidence of the depth and grade estimates made from all available data. Eco -miners 
> detailed evaluation of the location, quantity, grade, continuity, and geologic characteristics of the Yellow 
> Jacket 2A  property as part of the Study have been interpreted from the available geologic evidence, data, 
> and analytical test results. 
> 
> As part of the conservative approach to the reporting of Mineral Reserves across the Yellow Jacket Claim 
> Group, an impairment table and model have been developed for use in all grade calculations. The following 
> 
> is an outline of the current impairment model, as applied to the grade data averages and estimates. As a 
> result, rounding may create apparent discrepancies between tons, grade, and contained metal content. 
> 
> • Proven Reserve: No Impairment 
> 
> • Probable Reserve: 10% Impairment 
> 
> • Measured Reserve: 20% impairment. 
> 
> Grade results from the analytical laboratories are provided in two decimal places (EG. 0.38) . Once the 
> impairment is applied to each classification the resulting calculation includes in most cases in a greater 
> number of decimal places being used in the subsequent calculations. The grade shown in the Mineral 
> Reserve Statement is representative of rounding to three decimal places. All Mineral Reserve Estimate 
> calculations are based on the calculated grade estimate. 
> 
> The detailed evaluation of the factors as applied to mineral resources have demonstrated the economic 
> viability of the Yellow Jacket 2A mining property and project. 
> 
> As the entire raw material supply will be processed for extraction, and the overall materials being 
> representative of a homogeneous mixture of raw materials containing the gold and silver, no cut -off grade 
> will be allocated to the Yellow Jacket 2A Mineral Resource Estimate. 
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 8 
> 
> 01.14 MINERAL RESERVE STATEMENT 
>

> date of October 31, 2023.

> ASF Trust Diagram Summary (1)
> 
> 
> ASF Trust Diagram Summary
> 
> The purpose of this program is to provide asset holding clients with the ability to monetize those
> assets in such a way as to provide liquidity while simultaneously avoiding any equity or debt
> liability on the assets or repayment obligations by the client.
> 
> Many bonds are issued without an exit buyer arranged beforehand; this puts clients at risk of not
> being able to raise the necessary funds once the bond is issued. Our process works because
> bonds are built based on an existing exit already in place, according to the requirements for our
> exit, providing funding upon bond issuance.
> 
> A wide range of assets can be used, such as in-ground assets (oil, metals, etc.), real estate
> portfolios, real estate development projects, precious stones, etc. Assets are only needed if
> they are part of the project; i.e. getting funding for a mining or drilling project. A project, like
> a real estate development, will provide the value of the bond, but must include feasibility
> studies along with the documents listed below.
> 
> Required information from the Asset Owner
> •Inventory of all assets either owned or to be acquired under option with cost vs valuation;
> •Recent (within 12 months) valuation and feasibility study;
> •Business plan including contingency;
> •Detailed project financials and projections for at least 5 years;
> • Copies of all licenses and permits; permits for inground assets are only required if the
> client will be using the funding for mining the assets.
> •CV/bio of Management teams;
> •Copies of any Environmental studies;
> •Certified copies of the passport and proof of address for the officers and persons of
> significant control of the applicant;
> •Relevant financials for the applicant Company/Companies;
> •Asset and liability statement for the applicant Company/Companies
> •Full breakdown of the Company structure with CV’s on each individual;
> •Board resolution from the Company authorizing a person to sign the engagement and or any
> agreement required for the structure;
> Regarding in-ground assets, a 43-101/JORC report will need to be provided. We can supply the
> geological and mining teams, etc., for in-ground assets. We just need copies of licenses and
> permits, if required.
> Project plans must be detailed and established in such a way as to pass DTCC compliance.
> 
> Step One
> An executive or project summary must be provided for review, together with a list of all
> supporting documents for the project. Enough documentation must exist to allow us to build out
> and justify the offering memorandum.
> 
> 
> 
> A Q&A call can be arranged to answer all questions about the process, including fees and
> timelines.
> 
> Step Two
> Once a client is ready to move forward, a Letter of Engagement is sent out for review and
> signature. The bond process starts once the executed LOE is sent along with the £450,000 fee
> deposited by the client. These funds cover the full cost of the bond issuance from start to finish.
> This is an ‘all-in’ price, as fixed pricing to complete the various elements has already been
> arranged.
> 
> Funds are paid to our designated firm to employ third-party professional service providers such
> as lawyers, trustees, etc. We handle all the payments to relationships since AML/KYC, and the
> necessary groups for issuing the bond have already been approved by our group; this is a
> special team curated by us to complete the bond within the contracted timeline. A schedule of
> the work will be provided.
> 
> This process includes build time, justification of the bond via asset experts, listing with DTCC,
> insurance, and exit to the exit buyer.
> 
> Below is an excerpt from the engagement letter covering fees and how they relate:
> FEE PAYABLE: £450,000 (Four Hundred and Fifty Thousand Pounds GBP) upon execution of
> this Letter of Engagement for the Services) including but not limited to the services detailed
> below:
> 1. Consideration and evaluation by ACA of the Project Business Plan; AND 2. The creation
> of a dedicated compliant Private Placement Bond (PPB) under Rule 144a Securities Act
> 1933 in respect of the designated Project (the Project) in accordance with the relevant and
> current UK/USA regulations;
> 3. The drafting of all relevant legal documents pertaining to the PPB structure and securitization
> of underlying assets, including but not limited to, Joint Venture Agreement, Trust Documents,
> Issuance Resolutions and Working Capital Loan Agreements
> 4. The appointment of professional counterparts required for the PPB structure, including but
> not limited to Security Trustees, Transfer Agent, Paying Agent, Escrow and IOLTA. 5.
> Opening of bank accounts where required with JP Morgan.
> 6. Arranging third party legal opinion to confirm the issue of the PPB
> 7.The registration of the PPB as appropriate within prevailing registration regulations with
> dedicated ISIN and CUSIP numbers;
> 8. Registration of the PPB in accordance with prevailing registration regulations as may be
> appropriate on Bloomberg; Euroclear; Refinitiv/Eikon; ISIN.org; DTCC; Weiner/Frankfurt Boerse
> (as the case may be)
> 9. The researching, sourcing and negotiation of an Insurance Wrap of the PPB, (if appropriate)
> covering inter alia all capital and interest repayments (including but not limited to default and
> compound interest payments as applicable) and total loss payee cover;
> FEE PAYABLE: 10% (Ten Percent) of Total Loan or Financing amount received, on written
> 
> 
> 
> confirmation of an agreed LTV as specified above (N.B ONLY payable from each drawdown
> under the LTV) for the Services (including but not limited to):
> 
> 1. The research, sourcing and negotiation of an agreed Loan-to-Value (LTV) of the PPB
> from a Funding Line already identified, in accordance with prevailing market conditions
> in the Project sector target LTV is 20% of the PPB value ;
> 
> Step Three
> Assets are assigned under a JVA. The Joint Venture Agreement is between the asset owner
> and an SPV created for the purpose of issuing the bond. A nominee director will be appointed
> to the SPV which allows us to keep the asset at arms length from the bond issuance. The JVA
> is formed by the asset owner and SPV for the purpose of financing the project through the
> issuance of the bond.
> 
> Step Four
> Bond Issuance Process: This process takes approximately 12-weeks
> 
> Step Five
> Bond Exit/Trust: Once the draft bond is written and approved by all required parties, it goes
> forward to registration.
> We submit the bond, start the process to produce the executive summary, and start the
> contracting process with the Exit. Registration and arranging the exit are done simultaneously to
> expedite the process so the bond can be transferred to the Exit as soon as registration is
> complete.
> 
> Step Six
> Credit Line: The Bond Exit/Trust is able to raise a credit line that will provide the client with 20%
> of the value of the bond issued while providing additional funds to the trust for the investment
> strategy. This sum is non-recourse/non-repayment and can be used by the client at their own
> discretion
> 
> Step Seven
> A non-recourse/non-repayment payment of 20% of the bond value is sent to a designated
> attorney's IOLTA account for disbursement to the bond issuer and client. The bond issuer will
> receive a 10% fee of the client's gross (20%) value. The client will receive net 18% of the
> bond's value.
> 
> Additional Information
> 
> ● Bonds are issued for a 5-10 year period of time
> ● Funding process has the potential to be repeated on an annual basis, subject to
> 
> changes in market conditions and forces.

> A wide range of assets can be used, such as in-ground assets (oil, metals, etc.), real estate
> portfolios, real estate development projects, precious stones, etc. Assets are only needed if
> they are part of the project; i.e. getting funding for a mining or drilling project. A project, like
> a real estate development, will provide the value of the bond, but must include feasibility
> studies along with the documents listed below.
> 
> Required information from the Asset Owner
> •Inventory of all assets either owned or to be acquired under option with cost vs valuation;
> •Recent (within 12 months) valuation and feasibility study;
> •Business plan including contingency;
> •Detailed project financials and projections for at least 5 years;
> • Copies of all licenses and permits; permits for inground assets are only required if the
> client will be using the funding for mining the assets.
> •CV/bio of Management teams;
> •Copies of any Environmental studies;
> •Certified copies of the passport and proof of address for the officers and persons of
> significant control of the applicant;
> •Relevant financials for the applicant Company/Companies;
> •Asset and liability statement for the applicant Company/Companies
> •Full breakdown of the Company structure with CV’s on each individual;
> •Board resolution from the Company authorizing a person to sign the engagement and or any
> agreement required for the structure;
> Regarding in-ground assets, a 43-101/JORC report will need to be provided. We can supply the
> geological and mining teams, etc., for in-ground assets. We just need copies of licenses and
> permits, if required.
> Project plans must be detailed and established in such a way as to pass DTCC compliance.
> 
> Step One
> An executive or project summary must be provided for review, together with a list of all
> supporting documents for the project. Enough documentation must exist to allow us to build out
> and justify the offering memorandum.
> 
> 
> 
> A Q&A call can be arranged to answer all questions about the process, including fees and
> timelines.
> 
> Step Two
> Once a client is ready to move forward, a Letter of Engagement is sent out for review and
> signature. The bond process starts once the executed LOE is sent along with the £450,000 fee
> deposited by the client. These funds cover the full cost of the bond issuance from start to finish.
> This is an ‘all-in’ price, as fixed pricing to complete the various elements has already been
> arranged.
> 
> Funds are paid to our designated firm to employ third-party professional service providers such
> as lawyers, trustees, etc. We handle all the payments to relationships since AML/KYC, and the
> necessary groups for issuing the bond have already been approved by our group; this is a
> special team curated by us to complete the bond within the contracted timeline. A schedule of
> the work will be provided.
> 
> This process includes build time, justification of the bond via asset experts, listing with DTCC,
> insurance, and exit to the exit buyer.

> Content Snippet:
> H. They will, on SPV’s behalf, review the PPB and may revert with
> 
> alterations and amendments.
> 
> I. Once they are satisfied, the PPB will be forwarded to the Security
> Trustee’s dedicated DTCC Participant, who will then carry out its own
> 
> DD on the PPB. This will include, but may not be limited to the
> 
> following:
> 
> 1) Completion of all necessary registration paperwork for DTC;
> 2) Whelan Report on each individual identified as a principal in
> the PPB;
> 
> 3) Completion of all HMRC reports on the PPB;
> 4) Detailed assessment of the Project;
> 
> J. SPV also enters into formal arrangements with the Security Trustee on
> 
> each PPB, which in turn require payment of fees to enable DTC and
> 
> other platform registrations to be effected;
> 
> K. Security Trustee will, once DTC eligibility has been approved, formally
> 
> apply to register the PPB on the London Stock Exchange, Eikon
> 
> Platform.
> 
> L. The Process from G - K (inclusive) may take up to eight weeks, but
> 
> again, there may be variations depending on the DD at the relevant
> 
> stage and the quality and promptness of any information requested
> 
> from the Applicant.
> 
> M. Once the registration process as described above is complete, then
> 
> PPB is ready to be the subject of Monetisation. There is, and can never
> 
> be, no set time scale for monetisation.
> 
> N. Once a monetiser has been identified and a DoA executed, the
> 
> monetiser via its DTC Participant has to engage with DTC.
> 
> O. The Funding engagement process is set out on a separate sheet.
> 
> 
> 
> PROCESS OF FUNDING
> A. THE CLIENT and FUNDER execute this agreement contemporaneously
> with the Production by the Funder to VCR OF the Proof of Funds as specified
> herein.
> B. The Funder’s nominated DTC Participant will prepare and Lodge the DTC
> approved Deposit/Withdrawal at Custodial Form (DWAC ) with DTC;
> C. The Funder will procure the delivery to the CLIENT for onward transmission
> to Securities Transfer Corporation for and on behalf of THE CLIENT a copy of
> the executed and completed DWAC that they (the Funder) has caused to
> have lodged on the DTC system;
> D. The FUNDER will advise the CLIENT as soon as the DWAC form has been
> lodged at DTC;
> E. The CLIENT will issue an Issuance Resolution (IR) to Securities Transfer
> Corporation (STC) in the same terms as the DWAC lodged by the FUNDER
> (or its duly appointed DTC Participant);
> F. Immediately upon receipt of the executed and completed DWAC Form from
> the FUNDER the CLIENT will procure that STC “match“ the DWAC lodged by
> the FUNDER (or its duly appointed DTC Participant) with the IR supplied to
> STC by the CLIENT;
> G. Once the DWAC and IR have been “matched “, STC will immediately effect
> the digital delivery of the Bond free of payment (FOP) to the FUNDER’S
> DESIGNATED RECEIVING ACCOUNT;
> Once bond has been posted in the designated custodial account of the
> FUNDER, the FUNDER shall within Five (5) Banking Days procure the
> payment of an Advance “the Advance“ which shall be (as the case may be)
> the equivalent of 20 % (Twenty percent ) of the LTV, payment of the Advance
> being made to the CLIENT’S banking co-ordinates as set out in this
> Agreement (or order in writing prior to disbursement of the Advance as the
> case may be)

> this Letter of Engagement for the Services) including but not limited to the services detailed
> below:
> 1. Consideration and evaluation by ACA of the Project Business Plan; AND 2. The creation

> 1933 in respect of the designated Project (the Project) in accordance with the relevant and
> current UK/USA regulations;
> 3. The drafting of all relevant legal documents pertaining to the PPB structure and securitization
> of underlying assets, including but not limited to, Joint Venture Agreement, Trust Documents,
> Issuance Resolutions and Working Capital Loan Agreements

> not limited to Security Trustees, Transfer Agent, Paying Agent, Escrow and IOLTA. 5.

> dedicated ISIN and CUSIP numbers;

> appropriate on Bloomberg; Euroclear; Refinitiv/Eikon; ISIN.org; DTCC; Weiner/Frankfurt Boerse
> (as the case may be)
> 9. The researching, sourcing and negotiation of an Insurance Wrap of the PPB, (if appropriate)
> covering inter alia all capital and interest repayments (including but not limited to default and
> compound interest payments as applicable) and total loss payee cover;
> FEE PAYABLE: 10% (Ten Percent) of Total Loan or Financing amount received, on written
> 
>

> # 【3†ASF_Trust_Diagram_Summary (1) (1) (1).pdf†file-LaUsAxskzE9a5yfiwo5Tce】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: ASF_Trust_Diagram_Summary (1) (1) (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> Consideration and evaluation by ACA of the Project Business Plan; AND 2. The creation
> of a dedicated compliant Private Placement Bond (PPB) under Rule 144a Securities Act
> 1933 in respect of the designated Project (the Project) in accordance with the relevant and
> current UK/USA regulations;
> 3. The drafting of all relevant legal documents pertaining to the PPB structure and securitization
> of underlying assets, including but not limited to, Joint Venture Agreement, Trust Documents,
> Issuance Resolutions and Working Capital Loan Agreements
> 4. The appointment of professional counterparts required for the PPB structure, including but
> not limited to Security Trustees, Transfer Agent, Paying Agent, Escrow and IOLTA. 5.
> Opening of bank accounts where required with JP Morgan.
> 6. Arranging third party legal opinion to confirm the issue of the PPB
> 7.The registration of the PPB as appropriate within prevailing registration regulations with
> dedicated ISIN and CUSIP numbers;
> 8. Registration of the PPB in accordance with prevailing registration regulations as may be
> appropriate on Bloomberg; Euroclear; Refinitiv/Eikon; ISIN.org; DTCC; Weiner/Frankfurt Boerse
> (as the case may be)
> 9. The researching, sourcing and negotiation of an Insurance Wrap of the PPB, (if appropriate)
> covering inter alia all capital and interest repayments (including but not limited to default and
> compound interest payments as applicable) and total loss payee cover;
> FEE PAYABLE: 10% (Ten Percent) of Total Loan or Financing amount received, on written
> 
> 
> 
> confirmation of an agreed LTV as specified above (N.B ONLY payable from each drawdown
> under the LTV) for the Services (including but not limited to):
> 
> 1. The research, sourcing and negotiation of an agreed Loan-to-Value (LTV) of the PPB
> from a Funding Line already identified, in accordance with prevailing market conditions
> in the Project sector target LTV is 20% of the PPB value ;
> 
> Step Three
> Assets are assigned under a JVA. The Joint Venture Agreement is between the asset owner
> and an SPV created for the purpose of issuing the bond. A nominee director will be appointed
> to the SPV which allows us to keep the asset at arms length from the bond issuance. The JVA
> is formed by the asset owner and SPV for the purpose of financing the project through the
> issuance of the bond.
> 
> Step Four
> Bond Issuance Process: This process takes approximately 12-weeks
> 
> Step Five
> Bond Exit/Trust: Once the draft bond is written and approved by all required parties, it goes
> forward to registration.
> We submit the bond, start the process to produce the executive summary, and start the
> contracting process with the Exit. Registration and arranging the exit are done simultaneously to
> expedite the process so the bond can be transferred to the Exit as soon as registration is
> complete.
> 
> Step Six
> Credit Line: The Bond Exit/Trust is able to raise a credit line that will provide the client with 20%
> of the value of the bond issued while providing additional funds to the trust for the investment
> strategy. This sum is non-recourse/non-repayment and can be used by the client at their own
> discretion
> 
> Step Seven
> A non-recourse/non-repayment payment of 20% of the bond value is sent to a designated
> attorney's IOLTA account for disbursement to the bond issuer and client. The bond issuer will
> receive a 10% fee of the client's gross (20%) value. The client will receive net 18% of the
> bond's value.
> 
> Additional Information
> # 【4†ASF_Trust_Diagram_Summary (1) (1) (1).pdf†file-LaUsAxskzE9a5yfiwo5Tce】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: ASF_Trust_Diagram_Summary (1) (1) (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> Step Three
> Assets are assigned under a JVA. The Joint Venture Agreement is between the asset owner
> and an SPV created for the purpose of issuing the bond. A nominee director will be appointed
> to the SPV which allows us to keep the asset at arms length from the bond issuance. The JVA
> is formed by the asset owner and SPV for the purpose of financing the project through the
> issuance of the bond.
> 
> Step Four
> Bond Issuance Process: This process takes approximately 12-weeks
> 
> Step Five
> Bond Exit/Trust: Once the draft bond is written and approved by all required parties, it goes
> forward to registration.
> We submit the bond, start the process to produce the executive summary, and start the
> contracting process with the Exit. Registration and arranging the exit are done simultaneously to
> expedite the process so the bond can be transferred to the Exit as soon as registration is
> complete.
> 
> Step Six
> Credit Line: The Bond Exit/Trust is able to raise a credit line that will provide the client with 20%
> of the value of the bond issued while providing additional funds to the trust for the investment
> strategy. This sum is non-recourse/non-repayment and can be used by the client at their own
> discretion
> 
> Step Seven
> A non-recourse/non-repayment payment of 20% of the bond value is sent to a designated
> attorney's IOLTA account for disbursement to the bond issuer and client. The bond issuer will
> receive a 10% fee of the client's gross (20%) value. The client will receive net 18% of the
> bond's value.
> 
> Additional Information
> 
> ● Bonds are issued for a 5-10 year period of time
> ● Funding process has the potential to be repeated on an annual basis, subject to
> 
> changes in market conditions and forces.
> # 【5†Private_Placement_Bonds__PPB__Structural_Overview (1) (1).pdf†file-AjajCTFZb3bttBwRto35hJ】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: Private_Placement_Bonds__PPB__Structural_Overview (1) (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> Private Placement Bonds (PPB) Structural Overview
> 
> 
> PRIVATE PLACEMENT BONDS (PPB) UNDER R.144a Securities Act 1933
> STRUCTURAL OVERVIEW
> 
> This note is designed to describe the process for each PPB from creation to
> Monetisation.
> 
> A. The Applicant for the Bond will execute a Letter of Engagement (LoE)
> 
> with Project owners and operators which will form the subject matter of
> 
> the PPB;
> 
> B. The LoE will specify the fees that are payable on execution of the LoE
> 
> to cover the creation and the registration of the PPB with the relevant
> 
> authorities and platforms;
> 
> C. Once completed by the Applicant and the fees are settled, ACA will
> 
> commence the writing process of the PPB;
> 
> D. The Applicant is responsible for submitting the business case for the
> 
> Project together with all other details that may arise in the writing
> 
> process and be requested by ACA;
> 
> E. Once the Draft Bond has been written and approved by the
> Applicant (Usually a four week time line, but times may vary
> depending upon the quality and extent of the information supplied
> by the Applicant), ACA will, via their dedicated Transfer Agent,
> make application for ISIN and CUSIP numbers to assign to the
> specific PPB.This process is usually completed in a ten - day period,
> 
> but again, it is variable dependent upon DD and the volume of
> 
> registrations at any one time.
> 
> F. SPV will enter into a formal Services Agreement with said Transfer
> 
> Agent in respect of the PPB and pay the requisite fee
> 
> G. Upon receipt of the ISIN/CUSIP Nos., they will be applied to the draft
> 
> Bond, and the Bond will then be submitted to SPV’s dedicated Security
> 
> Trustees;
> 
> 
> 
> H. They will, on SPV’s behalf, review the PPB and may revert with
> 
> alterations and amendments.
> 
> I. Once they are satisfied, the PPB will be forwarded to the Security
> Trustee’s dedicated DTCC Participant, who will then carry out its own
> 
> DD on the PPB. This will include, but may not be limited to the
> 
> following:
> 
> 1) Completion of all necessary registration paperwork for DTC;
> 2) Whelan Report on each individual identified as a principal in
> the PPB;
> 
> 3) Completion of all HMRC reports on the PPB;
> 4) Detailed assessment of the Project;
> 
> J. SPV also enters into formal arrangements with the Security Trustee on
> 
> each PPB, which in turn require payment of fees to enable DTC and
> 
> other platform registrations to be effected;
> 
> K. Security Trustee will, once DTC eligibility has been approved, formally
> 
> apply to register the PPB on the London Stock Exchange, Eikon
> 
> Platform.
> 
> L. The Process from G - K (inclusive) may take up to eight weeks, but
> 
> again, there may be variations depending on the DD at the relevant
> 
> stage and the quality and promptness of any information requested
> 
> from the Applicant.
> 
> M. Once the registration process as described above is complete, then
> 
> PPB is ready to be the subject of Monetisation. There is, and can never
> 
> be, no set time scale for monetisation.
> 
> N. Once a monetiser has been identified and a DoA executed, the
> 
> monetiser via its DTC Participant has to engage with DTC.
> 
> O. The Funding engagement process is set out on a separate sheet.
> 
> 
> 
> PROCESS OF FUNDING
> A. THE CLIENT and FUNDER execute this agreement contemporaneously
> with the Production by the Funder to VCR OF the Proof of Funds as specified
> herein.
> B.
> # 【6†YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf†file-9iZAy3XvHnNppvKokrwFYd】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> owner of Yellow Jacket 2A according to both the County and BLM.  
> 
> Aureus Holdings as the owner of record and will use the Yellow Jacket 2A Claim as part of its collateral pool, 
> pursuant to a guarantee for the issuance of the corporate bonds by Aureus Capital, a special purpose vehicle 
> (“SPV”) wholly owned by Aureus Global Limited. Aureus Holdings has vested the Security Interest in Yellow 
> Jacket 2A by Deed of Trust and Security Agreement to Glas Trust Company LLC, pursuant to the Trust 
> Indenture and bond issuance of Aureus Capital Limited. 
> 
> Aureus Holdings acquired the YJ2A, YJ8, YJ24, YJ25, YJ26, YJ27, YJ30 & YJ31 claims during 2023 and has 
> options on the unpatented mineral claims of YJ4, Y10, & YJ11 in Nevada. 
> 
> Aureus Lending is the lender of record, loan servicer and asset manager for Yellow Jacket 2A on behalf of 
> Aureus Holdings. 
> 
> 03.03.03 GRAND CANYON GOLD CORPORATION 
> 
> All claims and property were transferred by Grand Canyon Gold Corporation to the relevant AUREUS 
> entities, pursuant to the Purchase and Sale Agreement and Addendum, dated August 5, 2023. Grand Canyon 
> Gold Corporation agreed to accept payment of a total of $34,000,000 and to be retained as the Operator of 
> the Yellow Jacket 2A Mine, pursuant to the Addendum, Master Operating and Lease Agreement and Master 
> 
> Finance Agreement. 
> 
> 03.03.04 GLAS TRUST COMPANY LLC 
> 
> Glas Trust Company LLC, the trustee for the bond issuance of Aureus Capital Limited.  Glas Trust Company 
> LLC, holds a Security Interest Agreement under a Deed of Trust, pursuant to the terms of the Trust 
> Indenture, Security Interest Agreement and Deed of Trust for the benefit of the Aureus Capital Limited bond 
> holders, as assigned within the overall collateral pool for Aureus Capital Limited, Aureus Holding and related 
> affiliates. A Uniform Commercial Code (“UCC”) UCC1 Financing Statement has been filed in the state of 
> Nevada, recording Glas Trust Company LLC as a Secured Party. 
> 
> 03.03.05 OTHER ENCUMBRANCES 
> 
> There are no other encumbrances, liens, mortgages, or legal actions against the properties other than 
> described herein. 
> 
> 03.04 ENVIRONMENTAL LIABILITIES 
> 
> Except for the exploration surface disturbance, primarily related to drilling, and the network of groundwater 
> monitoring wells that will be drilled, there are no known environmental liabilities associated with the Yellow 
> Jacket 2A claim. 
> 
> Surface disturbance that has not been reclaimed will potentially be used for future development activities 
> and access.  
> 
> There are no known environmental issues that are adversely impacting air, water, or soil resources at the 
> Site. Key environmental items addressed this Report are associated with wildlife and archaeology.  
> 
> No suspected tortoise burrows were found in the Yellow Jacket 2A claim area. A “no-drill” zone to protect 
> the tortoise habitat will be established and honored during this Project if tortoises or their burrows are 
> detected in any drilling area. No other protected species were observed. 
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 38 
> 
> There are no known sensitive cultural/archaeological sites within the Site area. However, these sites will be 
> effectively avoided should they be discovered during mining operations.

> 03.02 MINERAL TENURE 
> 
> The Yellow Jacket 2A Project is located within Clark County and is comprised of the 
> Yellow Jacket Claim Group, which covers more than 10,000-acres. The mineral tenure holdings comprise 
> thirty-two (32) unpatented lode and mill site claims, and a land lease for the same thirty-two (32) 
> unpatented lode mining claims. Claims are currently registered and held by Aureus Holdings with a 
> perfected security interest held by Glas Trust Company LLC. 
> 
> The unpatented claims were individually surveyed at the time of location. Unpatented claim and fee land 
> boundaries were established initially by handheld global positioning system (“GPS”) units and were formally 
> surveyed in June 1, 2017. 
> 
> On August 5, 2023, Aureus Lending acquired all rights, title, and interest in the Yellow Jacket 2A Project 
> from Grand Canyon Gold Corporation, including all existing and future exploration pertaining to the Project, 
> pursuant to the Purchase and Sales Agreement. 
> 
> The Purchase and Sales Agreement provided for Yellow Jacket 2A to be acquired by  
> Aureus Lending, and contemporaneously assigned to Aureus Holdings for the use as collateral. As part of 
> the Purchase and Sale Agreement, Aureus Minerals was assigned the rights for the exclusive sales of all 
> mineral output of the operations of the Mine. Further in the Addendum to the Agreement, Grand Canyon 
> Gold would enter into a Master Operating and Lease Agreement and Master Finance Agreement to operate 
> the Mine for a minimum twenty (20) year term. 
> 
> A “Mining Claim Quitclaim Deed” (“Deed”) between Grand Canyon Gold Corporation and Aureus Holdings, 
> dated August 5, 2023 was executed and notarized by the CEO of Grand Canyon Gold Corporation, and 
> delivered to Taggart and Taggart law firm. The Deed to transfer the ownership of Yellow Jacket 2A was 
> submitted to the County of Clark County and was stamped as received on August 10, 2023. The Deed was 
> also submitted to the BLM to record the change of ownership and control and was stamped as received on 
> November 2, 2023. 
> 
> Subject to the terms of the Master Operating and Lease Agreement and Master Finance Agreement, the 
> operator of the Yellow Jacket 2A Mine, is subject to underlying agreements and royalties. 
> 
> • Aureus Lending shall lease the property to Grand Canyon Gold Corporation for an initial term of 
> 20 years at a rate of $566,667 per quarter, plus annual inflation-based increases. 
> 
> • The lease and right to operate the Yellow Jacket 2A Mine, pursuant to the Agreements, shall act as 
> 
> the collateral for a credit facility to be provided to Grand Canyon Gold by Aureus Lending to build, 
> 
> mine, and operate the Mine and any ancillary upstream processes or business operations to 
> 
> maximize the yield of the Yellow Jacket 2A Claim. 
> 
> • Aureus Holdings as the owner of record and will use the Yellow Jacket 2A Claim as collateral, 
> 
> pursuant to a guarantee for the issuance of the corporate bonds by Aureus Capital, a special purpose 
> vehicle (“SPV”) wholly owned by Aureus Global Limited. A Uniform Commercial Code (“UCC”) UCC1 
> 
> Financing Statement has been filed in the state of Nevada Nevada recording Glas Trust Company 
> 
> LLC as a Secured Party.

> 
> extracts, payable as a transaction fee, and Aureus Minerals retaining 10.00% of the gross mineral sales from 
> 
> the  production of Yellow Jacket 2A Mine. In addition, Aureus Mineral shall have all rights to future

>

> 
> 01.05 GEOLOGICAL SETTING, MINERALIZATION, AND DEPOSIT 
> 
> The gold reserve is hosted in sedimentary rock and gravels eroded from the Arrow Canyon range, southern 
> end of Meadow Valley Mountains, and consists of alluvium of Quaternary age, underlain by Tertiary 
> sedimentary deposits of Muddy Creek formation.  The underlying host rock is from the Ordovician to 
> Permian eras.  The carbonate is from the Bird Spring formation.   
> 
> One stratigraphic, Site-wide, gold-rich “zone” thirty feet thick has been identified by Nuclear Magnetic 
> Resonance (NMR, Figure 07-1) and confirmed by sampling and assay (“HH Survey”,  Figure 07-5). The same 
> NMR survey identifies a “horizon” buried 450 feet below grade, suggesting either “hard rocks” (“basement”) 
> or a prominent stratigraphic horizon (possibly an erosional unconformity). Up to 450 feet of possible placer 
> deposits have been interpreted from the available drilling, sampling, and satellite data beneath the Claim. 
> The same NMR survey identifies multiple linear features, possibly veins or faults (or both) , at approximately 
> 1300 feet below grade. A second series of linear features is identified at approximately 1400 feet below 
> grade.   
> 
> The Yellow Jacket 2A is part of the Yellow Jacket Claim Group and the placer deposit occupies an area of 
> one square mile (640 acres or about 28 million square feet) and may be as much as 1,200 feet thick.  
> 
> 01.06 HISTORY 
> 
> Several companies and individuals have been involved in exploration, estimation, and analysis both on the 
> Property throughout the Yellow Jacket Claim Group prior to the acquisition of Yellow Jacket 2A by AUREUS. 
> The previous owner Grand Canyon Gold Corporation has expanded the exploration, estimation, and 
> analysis.
> # 【14†YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf†file-9iZAy3XvHnNppvKokrwFYd】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> Assay Quality Assurance/Quality Control (“QA/QC”) programs were carried out to verify the quality of the 
> assays and to determine the resource database. These programs were designed to determine the accuracy 
> and precision of the assays produced by the assay laboratory. 
> 
> The consistency of data from all previous analysis of Yellow Jacket Claim Group and Yellow Jacket 2A 
> confirms a homogeneous resource throughout the claim and Claim Group. 
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 5 
> 
> 01.09 DATA VERIFICATION 
> 
> To verify that the resource database accurately reflects the source documents for the project, the database 
> that was available was audited for accuracy. In addition, integrity checks were conducted on the entire 
> database to ensure that the database is acceptable for resource modeling.  
> 
> The QP compared the recent sampling against historical records and the NMR data and found no material 
> errors or erroneous data. 
> 
> Boring survey records from selected drill holes from the historical drilling were examined. No material errors 
> were noted; errors that were identified were corrected in the database. The drill holes were checked against 
> historical records and no discrepancies were found. 
> 
> The database assay values for selected intervals from historical drill holes were checked against historical 
> documents. No material discrepancies were found; errors that were identified were corrected in the 
> database. 
> 
> The QP conducted a site visit in June 13, 2022, that included inspection of the site with emphasis on the 
> development of all Project procedures related to logging, sampling, and data capture, and on-site evaluation 
> of several target areas throughout the Yellow Jacket area. 
> 
> The planned drilling program will employ the same procedures, with the addition of all data collected in the 
> future forming part of the AUREUS Mineral Information Data Analysis System (“MiDAS”) providing a 
> repository for all resource and reserve data. 
> 
> The QP verified that the Yellow Jacket 2A data are acceptable as used in this Report, most significantly to 
> support the estimation and classification of the Mineral Resources and Reserves. 
> 
> 01.10 MINERAL PROCESSING AND METALLURGICAL TESTING 
> 
> In support of the Study, historical work conducted by Grand Canyon Gold Corporation as well as other 
> former owners and geologists were reviewed. The degree to which historical metallurgical samples are 
> representative of the Yellow Jacket 2A deposit is not known with certainty, but there is no evidence that 
> the historical samples were not representative. Early historical work listed above is viewed as indicative or 
> informative only, since Eco-miners was not able to reconcile the test results to drill hole locations and depth 
> to confirm that these drill holes represent the ore in the current mine plan. 
> 
> Tests were performed on head ore that is representative of the material that will be sent to the plant. 
> Composite samples were tested representing one primary lithology, but with a range of head grades 
> representative of the minimum and maximum values expected for the plant feed in the initial and ongoing 
> years of production.  
> 
> The grade variability in gold in head ore ranged from 0.2 to 0.6 opt Au. Overall plant recoveries for gold are 
> predicted to range from 92 to 97% for head grades for the life of mine (“LoM”).  
> 
> The planned year-round operation of Yellow Jacket 2A will use conventional gravity and centrifuge 
> processes in combination with crushing and high-pressure impact milling to make all the particles of 
> subequal size. While this is expected to provide the plant recovery rates discussed, particle size will be 
> evaluated to determine the best recovery method for gold recovery.  
> 
> 01.11 MINERAL RESOURCE ESTIMATES
