> Content source: Source.file
> Title: YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> Provided that actual construction, operation, management, and closure of the TSF do not differ materially 
> from the results and design parameters summarized in this Report, there are no significant risks and 
> uncertainties that could reasonably be expected to affect the reliability or confidence in the TSF design and 
> cost estimates. 
> 
> If actual activities related to the construction, management, operation, and closure of the TSF do differ 
> materially from the results summarized in this Report, then the reasonably foreseeable impacts of these 
> risks and uncertainties are most likely to be project delays and additional costs. However, any such delays 
> or additional costs may reasonably be expected to be managed in the ordinary course and should not impact 
> on the overall Project viability. 
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 122 
> 
> 22.10.05 WATER MANAGEMENT 
> 
> Contact and non-contact surface water will be routed around the plant site. Permanent channels were 
> designed on a 100-year, 24-hour storm event with 9 inches of freeboard, or 500-year, 24-hour storm event 
> without overtopping. Temporary channels were designed on a 25-year, 24-hour storm event with 9 inches 
> of freeboard, or 500-year, 24-hour storm event without overtopping. 
> 
> 22.10.06 WATER SUPPLY 
> 
> Water supply from the raw water production wells and mine dewatering is projected to be sufficient to 
> support the operational demands. Water demands are expected to vary seasonally.  
> 
> 22.11 ENVIRONMENTAL, PERMITTING AND SOCIAL 
> 
> CONSIDERATIONS 
> 
> No mining or processing permits are currently in place at the Yellow Jacket 2A property. A new permitting 
> process must be undertaken by the Operator. The Yellow Jacket 2A  facility is expected to be an EPA 
> designated "Zero Discharge Facility" and has never been cited by the EPA so permitting is not anticipated 
> to be a cumbersome process, though time-consuming.  
> 
> A list of Permits required is (in Table 17-2). State water rights for new wells will need to be acquired and 
> renewed annually by AUREUS or the Operator. The total water volume to be permitted is estimated to be 
> 258-acre-feet. A dam permit will be required to be maintained under the name of AUREUS and the Operator.  
> 
> Permits not obtained but needed for the type and scope of potential mining at Yellow Jacket 2A as outlined 
> in this Report will involve a number of Federal, State, and local regulatory authorities. The Project will 
> require the environmental permits covering the construction, operation, and closure of the envisioned mine 
> as discussed in Section 15. Further information on environmental studies, permitting, and social and 
> community impacts is discussed in Section 15. 
> 
> Except for the exploration surface disturbance, primarily related to drilling, and the groundwater 
> monitoring wells that will need to be created, there are no known environmental liabilities associated with 
> the Yellow Jacket 2A claim. 
> 
> Surface disturbance that has not been reclaimed will potentially be used for future development activities 
> and access.  
> 
> There are no known environmental issues that are adversely impacting air, water, or soil resources at the 
> Site. Key environmental items addressed this Report are associated with wildlife and archaeology.  
> 
> No suspected tortoise burrows were found in the Yellow Jacket 2A claim area. A “no-drill” zone to protect 
> the tortoise habitat will be established and honored during this Project if tortoises or their burrows are 
> detected in any drilling area. No other protected species were observed.
> # 【16†YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf†file-9iZAy3XvHnNppvKokrwFYd】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> To facilitate Project permitting and development for the Study and permitting programs, and to design a 
> sustainable project and reduce environmental risks, Aureus has adopted the following environmental 
> principles for the Project: 
> 
> • Confirm the presence of potential threatened and endangered or sensitive amphibians, wildlife, or 
> plant species at the site. 
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 98 
> 
> • Reduce the area of disturbance where possible by utilizing existing infrastructure and re-use of 
> 
> tailings as backfill. 
> 
> • Reduce environmental impacts such as emissions, noise and vibration, water consumption, etc. 
> 
> through operational controls such as limiting traffic to and from the site and the re-use of water 
> from the open pit and TSF. 
> 
> • Protect local surface and ground water quality and quantity by applying best management practices 
> 
> and evaluating and implementing new practices as they are identified. 
> 
> • Effectively manage all related mine waste including lining the TSF and use of tailings as backfill. 
> 
> • Reduce the carbon footprint for the Project by processing the gold concentrate on site. 
> 
> • Conduct environmental monitoring to ensure compliance with all applicable State, Federal, and 
> 
> local laws, regulations, and ordinances. 
> 
> • Transport all fuel to the mining operation according to accepted transport and spill prevention and 
> response standard operating procedures developed specifically for the Project. 
> 
> • Integrate proactive wildlife habitat mitigation and enhancement proposals with an environmentally 
> 
> responsible reclamation plan. 
> 
> • Provide adequate financial assurance for implementing an effective reclamation plan to ensure 
> 
> long-term protection and rehabilitation of the mine site. 
> 
> • Implement a responsible community and state-wide public affairs program to further open 
> 
> communications, maximize local job opportunities and involvement, and meet environmental 
> justice requirements for the Yellow Jacket 2A Mine Project. 
> 
> Collectively, these objectives or environmental principles will guide Project development. They will also 
> serve to reduce risk and enhance related Project opportunities. 
> 
> 17.09 QUALIFIED PERSON’S OPINION 
> 
> It is the QP’s opinion that the proposed future PoO and CPA to be submitted to the BLM will be adequate 
> for the successful permitting of the Project and meet State and Federal requirements. Aureus’ and Eco -
> miners’ engagement with the local, state, and federal regulatory agencies as well as the local community

## 🤖 ChatGPT

### 🔍 Bottom Line: Is This Model *Possible*?  
- **Yes** — it's **theoretically possible**.  
- **No** — it is **not probable**, **verifiable**, or **sound enough to invest in** *without substantial proof*.

---

## ✅ What *Checks Out* (Theoretically Possible)
| Element | Validity |
|--------|---------|
| **43-101 / SK-1300** | Legit geological standards for mineral reserve reporting. |
| **Rule 144A PPBs** | Real framework for private bonds in U.S. capital markets. |
| **Bond-backed Credit Lines** | Conceptually valid. Institutional buyers do issue lines against high-quality assets. |
| **IOLTA Disbursement** | Real legal escrow mechanisms used for settlements. |
| **SPV + JV with Asset Owner** | Common structure to separate liability and facilitate funding. |

---

## ❌ What *Does Not Check Out* (Critical Issues)
| Issue | Analysis |
|-------|----------|
| **3x–4x Return in 90–120 Days** | Improbable unless you're being given **significant equity** or **fraudulent leverage** is at play. |
| **“Soft Cost” Pay-to-Play** | No protections. No escrow. No enforceable return. Could easily vanish with no recourse. |
| **No Proof of Exit Buyer Payment** | You are being asked to front capital before *any institutional wire or binding agreement is shown*. |
| **Returns Based on “Leftover Slice” Theory** | Mathematically flimsy. 3x returns don’t scale across multiple investors unless the pool is strictly capped — which is unverifiable. |
| **No Prior Deal History or Receipts** | No redacted bank confirmations, DTC proof, prior settlement logs, or audited statements. |
| **Emotional Pressure & Storytelling** | The speaker relies heavily on urgency, ego-stroking, anecdotal success, and social proof (e.g., "this is how billionaires do it"). Classic high-risk pitch tactic. |

---

## 🎯 Final Verdict:  
> **Possible?** Yes.  
> **Probable?** No.  
> **Smart to Proceed Without Proof?** Absolutely not.  
> **Would Institutional PE or Family Office Touch This Without Docs?** Never.

---

## ⚖️ Recommended Action
Before you risk even $1:
- Get a **signed PPM** or legally binding term sheet.
- Demand a **proof of funds** or **escrowed capital agreement** from the exit buyer.
- Require **audit trail from a past disbursement**.
- Hire an **independent securities attorney** to review all materials.

| **Illusion of "Remaining Room"** | The “someone pulled out at 60%” story is **a common sales tactic** to create FOMO and urgency. |

> Content source: Source.file

> Content Snippet:
> ASF Trust Diagram Summary (1)
> 
> 
> ASF Trust Diagram Summary
> 
> The purpose of this program is to provide asset holding clients with the ability to monetize those
> assets in such a way as to provide liquidity while simultaneously avoiding any equity or debt
> liability on the assets or repayment obligations by the client.
> 
> Many bonds are issued without an exit buyer arranged beforehand; this puts clients at risk of not
> being able to raise the necessary funds once the bond is issued. Our process works because
> bonds are built based on an existing exit already in place, according to the requirements for our
> exit, providing funding upon bond issuance.
> 
> A wide range of assets can be used, such as in-ground assets (oil, metals, etc.), real estate
> portfolios, real estate development projects, precious stones, etc. Assets are only needed if
> they are part of the project; i.e. getting funding for a mining or drilling project. A project, like
> a real estate development, will provide the value of the bond, but must include feasibility
> studies along with the documents listed below.
> 
> Required information from the Asset Owner
> •Inventory of all assets either owned or to be acquired under option with cost vs valuation;
> •Recent (within 12 months) valuation and feasibility study;
> •Business plan including contingency;
> •Detailed project financials and projections for at least 5 years;
> • Copies of all licenses and permits; permits for inground assets are only required if the
> client will be using the funding for mining the assets.
> •CV/bio of Management teams;
> •Copies of any Environmental studies;
> •Certified copies of the passport and proof of address for the officers and persons of
> significant control of the applicant;
> •Relevant financials for the applicant Company/Companies;
> •Asset and liability statement for the applicant Company/Companies
> •Full breakdown of the Company structure with CV’s on each individual;
> •Board resolution from the Company authorizing a person to sign the engagement and or any
> agreement required for the structure;
> Regarding in-ground assets, a 43-101/JORC report will need to be provided. We can supply the
> geological and mining teams, etc., for in-ground assets. We just need copies of licenses and
> permits, if required.
> Project plans must be detailed and established in such a way as to pass DTCC compliance.
> 
> Step One
> An executive or project summary must be provided for review, together with a list of all
> supporting documents for the project. Enough documentation must exist to allow us to build out
> and justify the offering memorandum.
> 
> 
> 
> A Q&A call can be arranged to answer all questions about the process, including fees and
> timelines.
> 
> Step Two
> Once a client is ready to move forward, a Letter of Engagement is sent out for review and
> signature. The bond process starts once the executed LOE is sent along with the £450,000 fee
> deposited by the client. These funds cover the full cost of the bond issuance from start to finish.
> This is an ‘all-in’ price, as fixed pricing to complete the various elements has already been
> arranged.
> 
> Funds are paid to our designated firm to employ third-party professional service providers such
> as lawyers, trustees, etc. We handle all the payments to relationships since AML/KYC, and the
> necessary groups for issuing the bond have already been approved by our group; this is a
> special team curated by us to complete the bond within the contracted timeline. A schedule of
> the work will be provided.
> 
> This process includes build time, justification of the bond via asset experts, listing with DTCC,
> insurance, and exit to the exit buyer.

> 03.02 MINERAL TENURE 
> 
> The Yellow Jacket 2A Project is located within Clark County and is comprised of the 
> Yellow Jacket Claim Group, which covers more than 10,000-acres. The mineral tenure holdings comprise 
> thirty-two (32) unpatented lode and mill site claims, and a land lease for the same thirty-two (32) 
> unpatented lode mining claims. Claims are currently registered and held by Aureus Holdings with a 
> perfected security interest held by Glas Trust Company LLC. 
> 
> The unpatented claims were individually surveyed at the time of location. Unpatented claim and fee land 
> boundaries were established initially by handheld global positioning system (“GPS”) units and were formally 
> surveyed in June 1, 2017. 
> 
> On August 5, 2023, Aureus Lending acquired all rights, title, and interest in the Yellow Jacket 2A Project 
> from Grand Canyon Gold Corporation, including all existing and future exploration pertaining to the Project, 
> pursuant to the Purchase and Sales Agreement. 
> 
> The Purchase and Sales Agreement provided for Yellow Jacket 2A to be acquired by  
> Aureus Lending, and contemporaneously assigned to Aureus Holdings for the use as collateral. As part of 
> the Purchase and Sale Agreement, Aureus Minerals was assigned the rights for the exclusive sales of all 
> mineral output of the operations of the Mine. Further in the Addendum to the Agreement, Grand Canyon 
> Gold would enter into a Master Operating and Lease Agreement and Master Finance Agreement to operate 
> the Mine for a minimum twenty (20) year term. 
> 
> A “Mining Claim Quitclaim Deed” (“Deed”) between Grand Canyon Gold Corporation and Aureus Holdings, 
> dated August 5, 2023 was executed and notarized by the CEO of Grand Canyon Gold Corporation, and 
> delivered to Taggart and Taggart law firm. The Deed to transfer the ownership of Yellow Jacket 2A was 
> submitted to the County of Clark County and was stamped as received on August 10, 2023. The Deed was 
> also submitted to the BLM to record the change of ownership and control and was stamped as received on 
> November 2, 2023. 
> 
> Subject to the terms of the Master Operating and Lease Agreement and Master Finance Agreement, the 
> operator of the Yellow Jacket 2A Mine, is subject to underlying agreements and royalties. 
> 
> • Aureus Lending shall lease the property to Grand Canyon Gold Corporation for an initial term of 
> 20 years at a rate of $566,667 per quarter, plus annual inflation-based increases. 
> 
> • The lease and right to operate the Yellow Jacket 2A Mine, pursuant to the Agreements, shall act as 
> 
> the collateral for a credit facility to be provided to Grand Canyon Gold by Aureus Lending to build, 
> 
> mine, and operate the Mine and any ancillary upstream processes or business operations to 
> 
> maximize the yield of the Yellow Jacket 2A Claim. 
> 
> • Aureus Holdings as the owner of record and will use the Yellow Jacket 2A Claim as collateral, 
> 
> pursuant to a guarantee for the issuance of the corporate bonds by Aureus Capital, a special purpose 
> vehicle (“SPV”) wholly owned by Aureus Global Limited. A Uniform Commercial Code (“UCC”) UCC1 
> 
> Financing Statement has been filed in the state of Nevada Nevada recording Glas Trust Company 
> 
> LLC as a Secured Party.

> Step One
> An executive or project summary must be provided for review, together with a list of all
> supporting documents for the project. Enough documentation must exist to allow us to build out
> and justify the offering memorandum.
> 
> 
> 
> A Q&A call can be arranged to answer all questions about the process, including fees and
> timelines.
> 
> Step Two
> Once a client is ready to move forward, a Letter of Engagement is sent out for review and
> signature. The bond process starts once the executed LOE is sent along with the £450,000 fee
> deposited by the client. These funds cover the full cost of the bond issuance from start to finish.
> This is an ‘all-in’ price, as fixed pricing to complete the various elements has already been
> arranged.
> 
> Funds are paid to our designated firm to employ third-party professional service providers such
> as lawyers, trustees, etc. We handle all the payments to relationships since AML/KYC, and the
> necessary groups for issuing the bond have already been approved by our group; this is a
> special team curated by us to complete the bond within the contracted timeline. A schedule of
> the work will be provided.
> 
> This process includes build time, justification of the bond via asset experts, listing with DTCC,
> insurance, and exit to the exit buyer.
>

> 25.03 AGREEMENTS 
> 
> Information with respect to the agreement to acquire, operate and fund all items relating to Yellow Jacket

> Resource estimate in Section 11 and the Mineral Reserve estimate in Section 12. It is used in the capital and 
> 
> operating cost analysis in Section 18, and in the economic analysis in Section 19. 
> 
> 25.04 MARKETS 
> 
> Information relating to market studies/markets for product, market entry strategies, marketing and sales 
> contracts, product valuations, product specifications, refining and treatment charges, agency relationships, 
> material contracts (e.g. mining, concentrating, smelting, refining, transportation, handling, sales and 
> hedging, forward sales contracts, or arrangements) and contract status (in place, renewals). 
> 
> This information is used when presenting the Executive Summary in Section 01 and discussing the market, 
> commodity price and contract information in Section 16, and in the economic analysis in Section 19. It 
> supports the Mineral Resource estimate in Section 11 and in the Mineral Reserve estimate in Section 12. 
> 
> 25.05 LEGAL MATTERS 
> 
> Information relating to the corporate ownership interest, ownership of the Project area and any underlying 
> property agreements, mineral tenure, surface rights, water rights, royalties, encumbrances, violations, and 
> fines, permitting requirements, ability to maintain and renew permits.

> through the following documents:

> • Master Operating and Lease Agreement 
> 
> • Master Finance Agreement 
> 
> This information is used to present the Executive Summary in  Section 01, and the interpretation and 
> conclusions in Section 18. This information is used in discussing property ownership information in 
> Section 01 of the Report, the tailings facility design in Section 15, the permitting and closure discussions in 
> Section 17, and in support of the Economic Analysis in  Section 19. It also supports the Mineral Resource 
> estimate in Section 11 and the Mineral Reserve estimate in Section 12. 
> 
> 25.06 MINING INFORMATION DATA ANALYSIS  
> 
> This document contains data, tables, figures and other information that were produced using MiDAS - 
> Mineral Information Data Analysis System, a software system designed and developed for Aureus Global 
> Limited to interpret mining claim, mineral resource data and assessment of economic viability of a mining 
> operation. The information contained within this system has been provided by Eco Miners and Aureus 
> Global Limited, based on assumptions and detailed analysis of mining claims, mineral resources, mineral 
> reserves, economic estimates, and forecasting. All economic models are based on concepts developed 
> between Maverick Eco-miners Inc, Mark Slatten and Aureus Global Limited.

> # 【9†YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf†file-9iZAy3XvHnNppvKokrwFYd】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> Several companies and individuals have been involved in exploration, estimation, and analysis both on the 
> Property throughout the Yellow Jacket Claim Group prior to the acquisition of Yellow Jacket 2A by AUREUS. 
> The previous owner Grand Canyon Gold Corporation has expanded the exploration, estimation, and 
> analysis.  
> 
> Historical testing of head ore has been conducted since 1980 from several “160 -acre Association Claims” in 
> Sections 2, 17, 18, 19, and 20. The literature available for review references “Yellow Jacket Placer Claims”. 
> Testing also occurred on “(4) of the 160-Acre claims” consisting of 12 drill holes (around the year 2009); 10 
> at a depth of 150 feet; 1 at 360 feet; and 1 at 620 feet for a total of 2,480 feet. One hundred sixty -five drill 
> samples were collected, and one thousand nine hundred eighty assays were made.  
> 
> Other historical work completed included reconnaissance, geological mapping, ore sampling and assaying, 
> and geophysical surveys (nuclear magnetic resonance (NMR) and magnetics), Mineral Resource estimation, 
> and Preliminary Feasibility study to assess the economic viability of the Yellow Jacket 2A Project to 
> determine the total Mineral Reserves Estimate and value following the August 5, 2023 acquisition by 
> AUREUS. 
> 
> Both the historical and recent work has defined the Yellow Jacket deposit, on which multiple Preliminary 
> Feasibility studies have been completed, including the previous study in 2023 by Eco -miners on behalf of 
> Grand Canyon Gold Corporation. The previous studies have all generally assumed a year-round conventional 
> “truck and shovel” (also known as “load and haul”) open pit mining operation using conventional gravity, 
> centrifuge (Mechanical Circuit), and flotation concentration processes (Flotation Circuit). 
> 
> This Report is substantially based on the previous report prepared for Grand Canyon Gold Corporation, as 
> well as multiple exploration, estimation, analysis and reports, produced both on the Yellow Jacket 2A and 
> others within the Yellow Jacket Claim Group and surrounding areas as detailed herein provide a fair 
> assessment of the entire 10,000 of the Yellow Jacket Claims as well as the individual 640 acres that comprise 
> Yellow Jacket 2A. 
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 4 
> 
> 01.07 EXPLORATION 
> 
> During the due diligence conducted prior to the acquiring its Project interest in 2023, AUREUS and Eco-
> miners have conducted an exploration review of the available Project data, ground-based magnetic, and 
> satellite NMR geophysical surveys, drilling, Mineral Resource and Mineral Reserve estimation, both for the 
> Yellow Jacket 2A and a significant portion of Yellow Jacket. A Preliminary Feasibility study was completed 
> of Yellow Jacket 2A in 2023 by Eco-miners and is the subject of this Report. 
> 
> Additional prospects were located during the exploration review programs. Of these, the YJ2A, YJ8, YJ24, 
> YJ25, YJ26, YJ27, YJ30 & YJ31 were also acquired by AUREUS, and YJ4, Y10, & YJ11 were placed under option 
> pending the expanded exploration, estimation, and analysis of the individual claims.  
> 
> Overall, the analysis of the Yellow Jacket deposit and group of claims has shown that the auriferous placer 
> deposit throughout the Yellow Jacket is generally consistent with only a 20-30% variance in grade 
> throughout the entire Yellow Jacket deposit.
> # 【10†YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf†file-9iZAy3XvHnNppvKokrwFYd】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> 01.05 GEOLOGICAL SETTING, MINERALIZATION, AND DEPOSIT 
> 
> The gold reserve is hosted in sedimentary rock and gravels eroded from the Arrow Canyon range, southern 
> end of Meadow Valley Mountains, and consists of alluvium of Quaternary age, underlain by Tertiary 
> sedimentary deposits of Muddy Creek formation.  The underlying host rock is from the Ordovician to 
> Permian eras.  The carbonate is from the Bird Spring formation.   
> 
> One stratigraphic, Site-wide, gold-rich “zone” thirty feet thick has been identified by Nuclear Magnetic 
> Resonance (NMR, Figure 07-1) and confirmed by sampling and assay (“HH Survey”,  Figure 07-5). The same 
> NMR survey identifies a “horizon” buried 450 feet below grade, suggesting either “hard rocks” (“basement”) 
> or a prominent stratigraphic horizon (possibly an erosional unconformity). Up to 450 feet of possible placer 
> deposits have been interpreted from the available drilling, sampling, and satellite data beneath the Claim. 
> The same NMR survey identifies multiple linear features, possibly veins or faults (or both) , at approximately 
> 1300 feet below grade. A second series of linear features is identified at approximately 1400 feet below 
> grade.   
> 
> The Yellow Jacket 2A is part of the Yellow Jacket Claim Group and the placer deposit occupies an area of 
> one square mile (640 acres or about 28 million square feet) and may be as much as 1,200 feet thick.  
> 
> 01.06 HISTORY 
> 
> Several companies and individuals have been involved in exploration, estimation, and analysis both on the 
> Property throughout the Yellow Jacket Claim Group prior to the acquisition of Yellow Jacket 2A by AUREUS. 
> The previous owner Grand Canyon Gold Corporation has expanded the exploration, estimation, and 
> analysis.  
> 
> Historical testing of head ore has been conducted since 1980 from several “160 -acre Association Claims” in 
> Sections 2, 17, 18, 19, and 20. The literature available for review references “Yellow Jacket Placer Claims”. 
> Testing also occurred on “(4) of the 160-Acre claims” consisting of 12 drill holes (around the year 2009); 10 
> at a depth of 150 feet; 1 at 360 feet; and 1 at 620 feet for a total of 2,480 feet. One hundred sixty -five drill 
> samples were collected, and one thousand nine hundred eighty assays were made.  
> 
> Other historical work completed included reconnaissance, geological mapping, ore sampling and assaying, 
> and geophysical surveys (nuclear magnetic resonance (NMR) and magnetics), Mineral Resource estimation, 
> and Preliminary Feasibility study to assess the economic viability of the Yellow Jacket 2A Project to 
> determine the total Mineral Reserves Estimate and value following the August 5, 2023 acquisition by 
> AUREUS. 
> 
> Both the historical and recent work has defined the Yellow Jacket deposit, on which multiple Preliminary 
> Feasibility studies have been completed, including the previous study in 2023 by Eco -miners on behalf of 
> Grand Canyon Gold Corporation. The previous studies have all generally assumed a year-round conventional 
> “truck and shovel” (also known as “load and haul”) open pit mining operation using conventional gravity, 
> centrifuge (Mechanical Circuit), and flotation concentration processes (Flotation Circuit).

> Content source: Source.file
> Title: YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> AUREUS’s 100% ownership of the Yellow Jacket 2A is subject to the underlying agreements. 
> 
> As part of the Purchase and Sale Agreement and Addendum, dated August 5, 2023, Aureus Minerals shall 
> have the exclusive rights to purchase and sell all output from the Yellow Jacket 2A Mine. This includes 
> royalties in the form of a 5.00% discount to the current market price of all materials and mineral extracts, 
> payable as a transaction fee and Aureus Minerals retaining 10.00% of the gross mineral sales from the  
> production of Yellow Jacket 2A Mine. In addition, Aureus Mineral shall have all rights to future exploration 
> on the Property. 
> 
> Currently no water rights exist on Yellow Jacket 2A. 
> 
> To the extent known to Eco-Miners, there are no other significant factors and risks that may affect access, 
> title, or the right or ability to perform work on the Project that are not discussed in this Report.  
> 
> 22.03 GEOLOGY AND MINERALIZATION 
> 
> The Yellow Jacket 2A deposit is an example of an alluvial/colluvial deposit derived from the erosion of 
> interpreted low-sulfidation epithermal deposits in the Mormon Mountains (see below).  
> 
> Rocks and sediments surrounding the Moapa Valley range in age from Precambrian to Quaternary. 
> Metamorphic rocks of Precambrian age crop out along Black Ridge, Bunkerville Ridge, and Whitney Ridge 
> in the Virgin Mountains; along the eastern flank of the East Mormon Mountains; and in isolated patches in 
> the southern and western parts of the Mormon Mountains. They consist of metasedimentary gneiss and 
> schist and lesser amounts of marble associated with gneissic granite.  
> 
> Paleozoic sedimentary rocks are present in most of the ranges in the area and although complexly folded 
> and faulted in places, most outcrops display north-trending strikes and east or west dips. In the Mormon 
> and East Mormon Mountains, the Paleozoic rocks occur as thrust or detachment fault sheets or gravity 
> slides which have overridden Precambrian rocks. Triassic and Jurassic sedimentary rocks consisting of the 
> Chinle, and Moenkopi Formations and the Aztec Sandstone crop out along the north-trending Muddy 
> Mountains and the southern part of Whitney Ridge in the southern Virgin Mountains.  
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 118 
> 
> The understanding of the Yellow Jacket 2A deposit settings, lithologies, source mineralization, and the 
> geological, structural, and alteration controls on deposition is sufficient to support estimation of Mineral 
> Resources and Mineral Reserves. 
> 
> There is remaining exploration potential in the Project area. The gold populations (i.e., “Tiers ”, see 
> Section 11, Figure 11-3, this Report) in the Yellow Jacket 2A claims block have been developed with the goal 
> of defining further exploration drill targets to confirm additional Mineral Reserves.  
> 
> 22.04 EXPLORATION, DRILLING AND ANALYTICAL DATA 
> 
> COLLECTION IN SUPPORT OF MINERAL RESOURCE 
> 
> ESTIMATION 
> 
> The exploration programs completed to date are appropriate for alluvial and colluvial deposits (placer 
> deposits). Sampling methods are acceptable for Mineral Resource estimation. 
> 
> Sample preparation, analysis and security are generally performed in accordance with exploration best 
> practices and industry standards at the time the information was collected. 
> 
> The quantity and quality of the geological and geophysical survey data collected in the exploration and infill 
> drill programs are sufficient to support Mineral Resource estimation.

> Content source: Source.file
> Title: YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> 01.11 MINERAL RESOURCE ESTIMATES 
> 
> Eco-miners reviewed all available data, including the NMR and field tests, including structural 
> interpretations that cover most of the extent of the Yellow Jacket deposit. These data were used as the 
> basis for the Eco-miners modeling of the gold and silver mineralization.  
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 6 
> 
> Grades used in the estimation are based on minimally expected level of mineralization consistent with the 
> small amount of sampling in the indicated and inferred Mineral Resource calculations.  
> 
> All measured resources identified are not included below, as Eco-miners has determined that these 
> resources, based on the location, quantity, grade, continuity, and geologic characteristics of the subject 
> property, have been adequately interpreted from the available geologic evidence, data, and analytical test 
> 
> results as economically viable, and have as such been classified and reported as Mineral Reserves  
> 
> While the Mineral Reserves discussed are estimated on the basis of an economically viable open pit mining 
> scenario, these Mineral Reserves represent only a small subset of the entire gold–silver deposit, with a very 
> small amount converted from the resource estimate. 
> 
> The Mineral Resources are therefore estimated to reflect potential extraction and milling and are subject 
> 
> to greater exploration, geological and financial analysis.  
> 
> The Mineral Reserves converted only represent the uppermost 30 feet of the Yellow Jacket 2A, while the 
> remaining 120 feet of indicated mineral resource and 300 feet of inferred mineral resource discussed herein 
> have been classified a Mineral Resource as calculated below, until its economic viability can be determined. 
> 
> As part of the conservative approach to the reporting of Mineral Resources across the Yellow Jacket Claim 
> 
> Group, an impairment table and model have been developed for use in all grade calculations. The following 
> is an outline of the current impairment model, as applied to the grade data averages and estimates. As a 
> result, rounding may create apparent discrepancies between tons, grade, and contained metal content. 
> 
> • Measured Resource: 20% impairment. 
> 
> • Indicated Resource: 30% Impairment. 
> 
> • Inferred Resource: 40% impairment. 
> 
> Grade results from the analytical laboratories are provided in two decimal places (EG. 0.38) . Once the 
> impairment is applied to each classification the resulting calculation includes in most cases in a greater 
> number of decimal places being used in the subsequent calculations. The grade shown in the Mineral 
> Resource Statement is representative of rounding to three decimal places. All Mineral Resource Estimate 
> 
> calculations are based on the calculated grade estimate. 
> 
> 01.12 MINERAL RESOURCE STATEMENT 
> 
> Mineral Resources are reported exclusive of the Mineral Resources that have been converted to Mineral 
> Reserves, using the mineral resource definitions pursuant to Regulation SK1300. Mineral Resources that are 
> not Mineral Reserves do not have demonstrated economic viability. The Mineral Resource estimates are 
> presented in Table 01-1. 
>

> 01.13 MINERAL RESERVE ESTIMATES 
> 
> Mineral Resources are occurrence minerals within the surface of the Earth’s crust.  Mineral Reserve s occur 
> where the form, quantity, and grade of the minerals contained within the Mineral Resource is deemed to 
> be economically viable for extraction.  
> 
> Based on assay data and geophysical surveys of the Mineral Resource at the Yellow Jacket 2A Claim and the 
> surrounding Yellow Jacket Claim Group, combined with the Study to determine the economic viability of 
> the Project, the QP has classified the measured portion of the Mineral Resource as Mineral Reserves.  
> 
> The top 30 feet of the Mineral Resource for the Yellow Jacket 2A Project has been classified as a Mineral 
> Reserve. Gold and silver are the metals of current interest at the Claim. 
> 
> The mineral Resources that have been classified as “Measured Reserves” in accordance with Regulation 
> SK1300 reflect the confidence of the depth and grade estimates made from all available data. Eco -miners

> Jacket 2A  property as part of the Study have been interpreted from the available geologic evidence, data,

> # 【15†YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf†file-9iZAy3XvHnNppvKokrwFYd】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> Surface and depth sampling have been recommended throughout the various claims owned by AUREUS, 
> with the goal of defining further mineral resources, reserves, and exploration drill targets. 
> 
> No production is known from the Project area. 
> 
> 01.08 SAMPLE PREPARATION, ANALYSES, AND SECURITY 
> 
> The interpretation of sampling and analyses is based on multiple investigations completed by various parties 
> across the Yellow Jacket Claim Group, specifically on the Yellow Jacket 2A property. These include site-wide 
> borings and ore samples at multiple drill depths.  
> 
> NMR data suggest that beneath the auriferous placer gravels may lie gold-bearing veins at depths of 450 
> and 1,300 feet, while primarily focusing on the range between 0-450 feet across the majority of the Yellow 
> Jacket Claim Group. 
> 
> A recent NMR survey flown over the Yellow Jacket 2A Claim and other areas of the Yellow Jacket Claim 
> Group confirmed auriferous gold deposits in a zone from the surface to more than  600 feet below grade 
> everywhere beneath Yellow Jacket 2A (see Figure 07-1) and across other sites in the Yellow Jacket Claim 
> Group.  
> 
> From Former drilling results, the near-surface auriferous deposits have been shown to exist from grade to 
> 30 feet below grade. While minimal exploration activity has taken place recently, boring samples were 
> drilled with small truck-mounted drill rigs or hand augers to depths of approximately 15 feet below grade 
> in a recent sampling. At some locations, in deep washes, drills achieved depths nearly fifteen feet below 
> grade. Cuttings were carefully logged by an onsite geologist every 5-feet of depth to the depth of each 
> boring.  
> 
> There are no chain-of-custody data or drill logs for Yellow Jacket 2A  that may be specifically included in this 
> Report.  
> 
> The primary analytical method for most previous samples was Inductively Coupled Plasma Mass 
> Spectroscopy (“ICP-MS”). For future drilling and sampling, Aureus will employ a more sophisticated and 
> environmentally sensitive analytical procedure for preparation and ICP-MS analysis for evaluation. Using 
> ICP-MS, peaks in the spectrum correspond to the elemental ions present in the original sample, yielding 
> quantitative and semi-quantitative elemental composition information. 
> 
> Assay Quality Assurance/Quality Control (“QA/QC”) programs were carried out to verify the quality of the 
> assays and to determine the resource database. These programs were designed to determine the accuracy 
> and precision of the assays produced by the assay laboratory. 
> 
> The consistency of data from all previous analysis of Yellow Jacket Claim Group and Yellow Jacket 2A 
> confirms a homogeneous resource throughout the claim and Claim Group. 
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 5 
> 
> 01.09 DATA VERIFICATION 
> 
> To verify that the resource database accurately reflects the source documents for the project, the database 
> that was available was audited for accuracy. In addition, integrity checks were conducted on the entire 
> database to ensure that the database is acceptable for resource modeling.  
> 
> The QP compared the recent sampling against historical records and the NMR data and found no material 
> errors or erroneous data. 
> 
> Boring survey records from selected drill holes from the historical drilling were examined. No material errors 
> were noted; errors that were identified were corrected in the database. The drill holes were checked against 
> historical records and no discrepancies were found. 
> 
> The database assay values for selected intervals from historical drill holes were checked against historical 
> documents. No material discrepancies were found; errors that were identified were corrected in the 
> database.

> 02.06 INFORMATION SOURCES AND REFERENCES 
> 
> This Report is primarily based on the information collected by the Author and initially summarized into the 
> report entitled, “SK 1300 Preliminary Feasibility Study” for the Yellow Jacket 2A, Nevada, USA completed 
> earlier in 2023 and supporting memoranda and trade-off studies, as well as information from sources cited 
> in the references section of this Report (Sections 24 and 25). This Report is also based in part on internal 
> Company reports, documents, agreements, plus other maps, published government reports, and public

> Content source: Source.file
> Title: YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> Eco-Miners experienced no limitations with respect to data verification activities for the Yellow Jacket 2A. 
> In consideration of the information summarized in Sections 5 through 9 and 11 through 12 of the Report, 
> Eco-miners has verified that the Yellow Jacket 2A data are acceptable as used in this Report, Eco-miners has 
> verified that the Yellow Jacket 2A data are acceptable as used in this Report, most significantly to support 
> the estimation and classification of Mineral Resources.
> 
> 
> 
>  
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 57 
> 
> 10 MINERAL PROCESSING AND 
> 
> METALLURGICAL TESTING 
> 
> 10.01 INTRODUCTION 
> 
> The Yellow Jacket 2A deposit has been the subject of historical metallurgical test work programs or previous 
> studies as outlined herein. 
> 
> 10.02 RECENT TEST WORK PROGRAMS 
> 
> 10.02.01 RECENT STUDIES 
> 
> During recent studies, a test work program was focused on a gravity, leach, and adsorption flowsheet 
> comprising: 
> 
> • Primary grind and mill (120 to 600 µm). 
> 
> • Electromagnetic and neodymium magnetic separation. 
> 
> • Gravity gold recovery. 
> 
> • Rare Earth Element (“REE”) separation. 
> 
> • Precious Metals Group (“PMGs”) separation. 
> 
> • Adsorption in a leach circuit. 
> 
> During the Study, the leach flowsheet design (see Figure 14-1) will be modified to a simpler, lower capital 
> cost alternative comprising: 
> 
> • Primary grind (120 to 600 µm). 
> 
> • Centrifuge recoveries. 
> 
> • Hybrid leach–CIL circuit. 
> 
> • Amino acid leach circuit. 
> 
> 10.02.02 MINERALOGICAL ANALYSIS 
> 
> 10.02.02.01 ECO-MINERS 2019 
> 
> Mineralogical examinations of ore from Tiers 1, 2, and 3 (see Section 11) show that they are composed 
> predominately of medium- to fine-grained quartz. Pebble- to cobble-sized clasts may locally comprise up to 
> 50 percent of the ore, particularly in channels and paleo-channels.  
> 
> Findings included:  
> 
> • Gold and silver occur as free particles. 
> 
> • Gold sizes range from small flakes to “fine” gold (<30 µm). 
> 
> • Gold, if not liberated, may occur in association with light silicates. 
> 
> • Gold grains coarser than 30 µm may be recoverable by mechanical methods (e.g., centrifuge).  
> 
> • Clay may be a significant factor in gold recovery by mechanical means. 
> 
> 10.03 QUALIFIED PERSON’S OPINION ON DATA ADEQUACY 
> 
> In the QP’s opinion, the data adequacy, for the purposes of this PFS, is acceptable. 
> 
>  
> 
> 
> 
>  
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 58 
> 
> 11 MINERAL RESOURCE ESTIMATES 
> 
> Regulation SK1300 requires a registrant (such as Aureus Capital Limited) with material mining operations 
> to classify its mineral resources into inferred, indicated, and measured mineral resources, in order of 
> increasing confidence based on the level of underlying geological evidence. The rules define each class of 
> mineral resources. This classification requirement is consistent with the classification scheme under the 
> Committee for Mineral Reserves International Reporting Standards (“CRIRSCO”) standards.

> ASF Trust Diagram Summary (1)
> 
> 
> ASF Trust Diagram Summary
> 
> The purpose of this program is to provide asset holding clients with the ability to monetize those
> assets in such a way as to provide liquidity while simultaneously avoiding any equity or debt
> liability on the assets or repayment obligations by the client.
> 
> Many bonds are issued without an exit buyer arranged beforehand; this puts clients at risk of not
> being able to raise the necessary funds once the bond is issued. Our process works because
> bonds are built based on an existing exit already in place, according to the requirements for our
> exit, providing funding upon bond issuance.
> 
> A wide range of assets can be used, such as in-ground assets (oil, metals, etc.), real estate
> portfolios, real estate development projects, precious stones, etc. Assets are only needed if
> they are part of the project; i.e. getting funding for a mining or drilling project. A project, like
> a real estate development, will provide the value of the bond, but must include feasibility
> studies along with the documents listed below.
> 
> Required information from the Asset Owner
> •Inventory of all assets either owned or to be acquired under option with cost vs valuation;
> •Recent (within 12 months) valuation and feasibility study;
> •Business plan including contingency;
> •Detailed project financials and projections for at least 5 years;
> • Copies of all licenses and permits; permits for inground assets are only required if the
> client will be using the funding for mining the assets.
> •CV/bio of Management teams;
> •Copies of any Environmental studies;
> •Certified copies of the passport and proof of address for the officers and persons of
> significant control of the applicant;
> •Relevant financials for the applicant Company/Companies;
> •Asset and liability statement for the applicant Company/Companies
> •Full breakdown of the Company structure with CV’s on each individual;
> •Board resolution from the Company authorizing a person to sign the engagement and or any
> agreement required for the structure;
> Regarding in-ground assets, a 43-101/JORC report will need to be provided. We can supply the
> geological and mining teams, etc., for in-ground assets. We just need copies of licenses and
> permits, if required.
> Project plans must be detailed and established in such a way as to pass DTCC compliance.
> 
> Step One
> An executive or project summary must be provided for review, together with a list of all
> supporting documents for the project. Enough documentation must exist to allow us to build out
> and justify the offering memorandum.
> 
> 
> 
> A Q&A call can be arranged to answer all questions about the process, including fees and
> timelines.
> 
> Step Two
> Once a client is ready to move forward, a Letter of Engagement is sent out for review and
> signature. The bond process starts once the executed LOE is sent along with the £450,000 fee
> deposited by the client. These funds cover the full cost of the bond issuance from start to finish.
> This is an ‘all-in’ price, as fixed pricing to complete the various elements has already been
> arranged.
> 
> Funds are paid to our designated firm to employ third-party professional service providers such
> as lawyers, trustees, etc. We handle all the payments to relationships since AML/KYC, and the
> necessary groups for issuing the bond have already been approved by our group; this is a
> special team curated by us to complete the bond within the contracted timeline. A schedule of
> the work will be provided.
> 
> This process includes build time, justification of the bond via asset experts, listing with DTCC,
> insurance, and exit to the exit buyer.
> # 【1†Private_Placement_Bonds__PPB__Structural_Overview (1) (1).pdf†file-AjajCTFZb3bttBwRto35hJ】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: Private_Placement_Bonds__PPB__Structural_Overview (1) (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> Private Placement Bonds (PPB) Structural Overview
> 
> 
> PRIVATE PLACEMENT BONDS (PPB) UNDER R.144a Securities Act 1933
> STRUCTURAL OVERVIEW
> 
> This note is designed to describe the process for each PPB from creation to
> Monetisation.
> 
> A. The Applicant for the Bond will execute a Letter of Engagement (LoE)
> 
> with Project owners and operators which will form the subject matter of
> 
> the PPB;
> 
> B. The LoE will specify the fees that are payable on execution of the LoE
> 
> to cover the creation and the registration of the PPB with the relevant
> 
> authorities and platforms;
> 
> C. Once completed by the Applicant and the fees are settled, ACA will
> 
> commence the writing process of the PPB;
> 
> D. The Applicant is responsible for submitting the business case for the
> 
> Project together with all other details that may arise in the writing
> 
> process and be requested by ACA;
> 
> E. Once the Draft Bond has been written and approved by the
> Applicant (Usually a four week time line, but times may vary
> depending upon the quality and extent of the information supplied
> by the Applicant), ACA will, via their dedicated Transfer Agent,
> make application for ISIN and CUSIP numbers to assign to the
> specific PPB.This process is usually completed in a ten - day period,
> 
> but again, it is variable dependent upon DD and the volume of
> 
> registrations at any one time.
> 
> F. SPV will enter into a formal Services Agreement with said Transfer
> 
> Agent in respect of the PPB and pay the requisite fee
> 
> G. Upon receipt of the ISIN/CUSIP Nos., they will be applied to the draft
> 
> Bond, and the Bond will then be submitted to SPV’s dedicated Security
> 
> Trustees;
> 
> 
> 
> H. They will, on SPV’s behalf, review the PPB and may revert with
> 
> alterations and amendments.
> 
> I. Once they are satisfied, the PPB will be forwarded to the Security
> Trustee’s dedicated DTCC Participant, who will then carry out its own
> 
> DD on the PPB. This will include, but may not be limited to the
> 
> following:
> 
> 1) Completion of all necessary registration paperwork for DTC;

> We can supply the
> geological and mining teams, etc., for in-ground assets. We just need copies of licenses and
> permits, if required.
> Project plans must be detailed and established in such a way as to pass DTCC compliance.
> 
> Step One
> An executive or project summary must be provided for review, together with a list of all
> supporting documents for the project. Enough documentation must exist to allow us to build out
> and justify the offering memorandum.
> 
> 
> 
> A Q&A call can be arranged to answer all questions about the process, including fees and
> timelines.
> 
> Step Two
> Once a client is ready to move forward, a Letter of Engagement is sent out for review and
> signature. The bond process starts once the executed LOE is sent along with the £450,000 fee
> deposited by the client. These funds cover the full cost of the bond issuance from start to finish.
> This is an ‘all-in’ price, as fixed pricing to complete the various elements has already been
> arranged.
> 
> Funds are paid to our designated firm to employ third-party professional service providers such
> as lawyers, trustees, etc. We handle all the payments to relationships since AML/KYC, and the
> necessary groups for issuing the bond have already been approved by our group; this is a
> special team curated by us to complete the bond within the contracted timeline. A schedule of
> the work will be provided.
> 
> This process includes build time, justification of the bond via asset experts, listing with DTCC,
> insurance, and exit to the exit buyer.
> 
> Below is an excerpt from the engagement letter covering fees and how they relate:
> FEE PAYABLE: £450,000 (Four Hundred and Fifty Thousand Pounds GBP) upon execution of
> this Letter of Engagement for the Services) including but not limited to the services detailed
> below:
> 1. Consideration and evaluation by ACA of the Project Business Plan; AND 2. The creation
> of a dedicated compliant Private Placement Bond (PPB) under Rule 144a Securities Act
> 1933 in respect of the designated Project (the Project) in accordance with the relevant and
> current UK/USA regulations;
> 3. The drafting of all relevant legal documents pertaining to the PPB structure and securitization
> of underlying assets, including but not limited to, Joint Venture Agreement, Trust Documents,
> Issuance Resolutions and Working Capital Loan Agreements
> 4. The appointment of professional counterparts required for the PPB structure, including but
> not limited to Security Trustees, Transfer Agent, Paying Agent, Escrow and IOLTA. 5.
> Opening of bank accounts where required with JP Morgan.
> 6. Arranging third party legal opinion to confirm the issue of the PPB
> 7.The registration of the PPB as appropriate within prevailing registration regulations with
> dedicated ISIN and CUSIP numbers;
> 8. Registration of the PPB in accordance with prevailing registration regulations as may be
> appropriate on Bloomberg; Euroclear; Refinitiv/Eikon; ISIN.org; DTCC; Weiner/Frankfurt Boerse
> (as the case may be)
> 9. The researching, sourcing and negotiation of an Insurance Wrap of the PPB, (if appropriate)
> covering inter alia all capital and interest repayments (including but not limited to default and
> compound interest payments as applicable) and total loss payee cover;
> FEE PAYABLE: 10% (Ten Percent) of Total Loan or Financing amount received, on written
> 
> 
> 
> confirmation of an agreed LTV as specified above (N.B ONLY payable from each drawdown
> under the LTV) for the Services (including but not limited to):
> # 【3†ASF_Trust_Diagram_Summary (1) (1) (1).pdf†file-LaUsAxskzE9a5yfiwo5Tce】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: ASF_Trust_Diagram_Summary (1) (1) (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> Step Three
> Assets are assigned under a JVA. The Joint Venture Agreement is between the asset owner
> and an SPV created for the purpose of issuing the bond. A nominee director will be appointed
> to the SPV which allows us to keep the asset at arms length from the bond issuance. The JVA
> is formed by the asset owner and SPV for the purpose of financing the project through the
> issuance of the bond.
> 
> Step Four
> Bond Issuance Process: This process takes approximately 12-weeks
> 
> Step Five
> Bond Exit/Trust: Once the draft bond is written and approved by all required parties, it goes
> forward to registration.
> We submit the bond, start the process to produce the executive summary, and start the
> contracting process with the Exit. Registration and arranging the exit are done simultaneously to
> expedite the process so the bond can be transferred to the Exit as soon as registration is
> complete.
> 
> Step Six
> Credit Line: The Bond Exit/Trust is able to raise a credit line that will provide the client with 20%
> of the value of the bond issued while providing additional funds to the trust for the investment
> strategy. This sum is non-recourse/non-repayment and can be used by the client at their own
> discretion
> 
> Step Seven
> A non-recourse/non-repayment payment of 20% of the bond value is sent to a designated
> attorney's IOLTA account for disbursement to the bond issuer and client. The bond issuer will
> receive a 10% fee of the client's gross (20%) value. The client will receive net 18% of the
> bond's value.
> 
> Additional Information
> 
> ● Bonds are issued for a 5-10 year period of time
> ● Funding process has the potential to be repeated on an annual basis, subject to
> 
> changes in market conditions and forces.

> The information contained in this TRS-SK1300 includes the majority of the contents of the initial draft report 
> 
> and is based on the information provided by Grand Canyon Gold Corporation. 
> 
> I have been retained by Aureus Global Limited and its affiliates as Chief Geologist to review and revise the 
> initial draft report and to complete the Mineral Resource and Mineral Reserve Estimates for Yellow Jacket 
> 2A in accordance with the standards required by the United States Securities and Exchange Commission 
> pursuant to Subpart 1300 of Regulation S-K, with the addition of information provided by Aureus Global 
> Limited and its affiliates as outlined herein. 
> 
> Further, my role with Aureus Global Limited is to provide critical knowledge garnered during the 
> engagement with Grand Canyon Gold Corporation as well as greater knowledge of the Yellow Jacket Claim 
> Group. 
> 
> I have not previously been involved in Yellow Jacket 2A, other than as outlined herein, with respect to the 
> 
> Preliminary Feasibility Study and production of the initial draft report and its subsequent review and 
> updating into this TRS-SK1300. 
> 
> I have not received, nor do I expect to receive, any interest, directly or indirectly, in the Yellow Jacket 2A 
> project.  
> 
> As of the date of this certificate, to the best of my knowledge, information, and belief, this TRS-SK1300 for 
> 
> which I am responsible contains all scientific and technical information that is required to be disclosed to 
> make this Report accurate and not misleading.  
> 
> As the Chief Geologist for Aureus Global Limited, I am no longer deemed independent as defined by the 
> SEC. Subpart 1300 does not require Qualified Persons to be independent of the registrant and further 
> 
> permits third party firms to “single sign-off”, and assume liability for a Technical Report Summary on  
> 
> Preliminary Feasibility and Feasibility Studies 
> 
> This assignment was completed using my “best practice” and in accordance with requirements of Regulation 
> SK1300 and other relevant guidelines of the SEC. The Report is based on my review of project files and 
> discussions with the prior owner Grand Canyon Gold Corporation, planned operator Grand Canyon Gold 
> Corporation as well as with AUREUS and its management with regard to a plan of operation and financing.  
> 
>

> PROCESS OF FUNDING
> A. THE CLIENT and FUNDER execute this agreement contemporaneously
> with the Production by the Funder to VCR OF the Proof of Funds as specified
> herein.
> B. The Funder’s nominated DTC Participant will prepare and Lodge the DTC
> approved Deposit/Withdrawal at Custodial Form (DWAC ) with DTC;
> C. The Funder will procure the delivery to the CLIENT for onward transmission
> to Securities Transfer Corporation for and on behalf of THE CLIENT a copy of
> the executed and completed DWAC that they (the Funder) has caused to
> have lodged on the DTC system;
> D. The FUNDER will advise the CLIENT as soon as the DWAC form has been
> lodged at DTC;
> E. The CLIENT will issue an Issuance Resolution (IR) to Securities Transfer
> Corporation (STC) in the same terms as the DWAC lodged by the FUNDER
> (or its duly appointed DTC Participant);
> F. Immediately upon receipt of the executed and completed DWAC Form from
> the FUNDER the CLIENT will procure that STC “match“ the DWAC lodged by
> the FUNDER (or its duly appointed DTC Participant) with the IR supplied to
> STC by the CLIENT;
> G. Once the DWAC and IR have been “matched “, STC will immediately effect
> the digital delivery of the Bond free of payment (FOP) to the FUNDER’S
> DESIGNATED RECEIVING ACCOUNT;
> Once bond has been posted in the designated custodial account of the
> FUNDER, the FUNDER shall within Five (5) Banking Days procure the
> payment of an Advance “the Advance“ which shall be (as the case may be)
> the equivalent of 20 % (Twenty percent ) of the LTV, payment of the Advance
> being made to the CLIENT’S banking co-ordinates as set out in this
> Agreement (or order in writing prior to disbursement of the Advance as the
> case may be)

> At AUREUS, we are here to lighten the load of the mining operator, 
> providing key project financing and access to capital needed 
> to turn a claim into a producing and economically  
> viable mining operation. 
> 
>  
> 
> For our institutional investors, our goal is to consistently delivery 
> gold backed fixed income investment products, providing ongoing 
> returns, profit participation and the security of gold.

> 
> IMPORTANT NOTICE 
> 
> The Qualified Person firm responsible for the mineral reserves estimate is Maverick Eco-miners, Inc.  
> 
> (“Eco-miners”, “QPF”) and includes Mark Slatten as its Chief Executive Officer and Qualified Person (“Author”). Eco-
> 
> miners consent to the filing of this report with the United States Securities and Exchange Commission (“SEC”), any 
> 
> stock exchange, or other regulatory authority, as required now or at any time in the future.  
> 
> This report was prepared as a technical report as defined by the SEC regulation, Item 102 of Regulation S-K under 
> 
> the Securities Act of 1933 and the Securities Exchange Act of 1934 (“Securities Acts”) and in Industry Guide 7, and 
> 
> the mining property disclosure requirements pursuant to subpart of Regulation S-K subpart 229.1300 – Disclosure 
> 
> by Registrants Engaged in Mining Operations (“Regulation S-K 1300”).  
> 
> This Regulation S-K 1300 Technical Report Summary on the Preliminary Feasibility Study (“TRS-SK1300”, “Technical 
> 
> Report Summary”) is for the mineral claim known as Yellow Jacket 2A. The quality of the information, conclusions, 
> 
> and estimates contained herein are consistent with the level of effort involved in the services, based on; i) 
> 
> information available at the time of preparation; ii) data supplied by outside sources; and iii) the assumptions, 
> 
> conditions, and qualifications set forth in this report.  
> 
> This Technical Report Summary is intended to be used only by Aureus Global Limited and its affiliates (“AUREUS” 
> 
> or “Company”), Eco-miners, Mark H. Slatten, and their respective service providers and institutional investors. Eco-
> 
> miners permit AUREUS to file this TRS-SK1300 as a technical report as defined by Regulations S-K 1300, and as 
> 
> required pursuant to securities laws relating to AUREUS, including specifically Aureus Capital Limited. Except for 
> 
> the purposes outlined herein, any other use of this report by any third party is not authorized. 
> 
> This TRS-SK1300 contains "forward-looking information" within the meaning of applicable United States securities 
> 
> laws. Forward-looking information includes, but is not limited to, statements related to activities, events, or 
> 
> developments that the Author or the Company expect or anticipate will or may occur in the future, including, 
> 
> without limitation, statements related to the Author’s or the Company’s economic analysis, Mineral Resource 
> 
> Estimate, Mineral Reserve Estimate, the permitting process, environmental assessments, business strategy, 
>

> All initial information was compiled into a generic form and a final draft summary report produced. 
> Subsequently, that report, key documents and other supporting datapoints have been consolidated into 
> this Regulation S-K 1300 Technical Report Summary on the Preliminary Feasibility Study (the “Study”) of 
> Yellow Jacket 2A. This significantly updated and expanded report conforms to United States Securities and 
> Exchange Commissions in accordance with subpart 229.1300 of the S-K 1300 Disclosure by Registrants 
> Engaged in Mining Operations reporting requirements for issuers. (the “Report”, “TRS-SK1300”). 
> 
> This effort was commissioned and managed by Mark Slatten MS, PG, QP, the CEO and Principal Geologist 
> for Eco-miners, Chief Geologist for AUREUS, with the assistance of administrative personnel.  
> 
> The purpose of this Report is to present the results of a comprehensive evaluation of the economic viability 
> of the Yellow Jacket 2A project.  The claim is on property owned by the Bureau of Land Management 
> (“BLM”). 
> 
> AUREUS owns the Yellow Jacket 2A unpatented mining claim through its wholly owned subsidiary, Aureus 
> (US) Holdings LLC. (“Aureus Holdings”). 
> 
> 02.02 TERMS OF REFERENCE 
> 
> This Report supports disclosures by AUREUS in its Annual Report pursuant to Section 13 or 15(d) of the 
> securities exchange act of 1934 for the fiscal year ended December 31, 2023. 
> 
> Measurement units used in this Report are generally US customary; however, some units, such as analytical 
> and metallurgical test work units may be in metric units. Unless otherwise stated, all monetary amounts 
> are in 2023 United States Dollars (USD). 
> 
> Mineral Resources and Mineral Reserves are reported in accordance with subpart 229.1300 of the S-K 1300 
> reporting requirements. 
> 
> 02.03 QUALIFIED PERSONS 
> 
> The Qualified Person firm responsible for the Study and the preparation of this Technical Report Summary, 
> including the mineral reserves estimate and mineral resource estimate is Maverick Eco -Miners, Inc. (“Eco-
> Miners”, “QPF”) including Mark Slatten as Chief Executive Officer and Qualified Person(“Author”).  
> 
> Aureus Global Limited contributed to the preparation of this Technical Report Summary through detailed 
> reporting of the acquisition and planned operation of the Yellow Jacket 2A claim.  
> 
>  
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 25 
> 
> Tables and other reported information contained in this Report have been produced using AUREUS’ 
> proprietary Mineral Information Data Analysis System (“MiDAS”). All models and data preparation are based 
> on models and information developed by Eco-miners during their Preliminary Feasibility Study. 
> 
> 02.04 SITE VISITS AND SCOPE OF PERSONAL INSPECTION 
> 
> The study was initiated with the Site visit in June 13, 2022 by Mark Slatten MS, PG, QP. The intent of the 
> Site visit was to familiarize the Author with Site conditions, collect data, review project scope, and outline 
> any additional data requirements. The visit included a tour of the claim.  
> 
> 02.05 EFFECTIVE DATES 
> 
> The Report has a number of effective dates as follows: 
> 
> • Date of supply of last information on mineral tenure, surface rights and agreements:  
> 
> October 31, 2023; 
> 
> • Date of close-out of database that supports the Mineral Resource estimates: October 31, 2023; 
> 
> • Mineral Resource estimates: October 31, 2023;

> 03.03 ROYALTIES AND ADDITIONAL ENCUMBRANCES 
> 
> 03.03.01 AUREUS MINERALS 
> 
> As part of the Purchase and Sale Agreement and Addendum, dated August 5, 2023, Aureus Minerals shall 
> have the exclusive rights to purchase and sell all output from the Yellow Jacket 2A Mine. This includes 
> 
> royalties in the form of a 5.00% discount to the current market price of all materials and mineral extracts, 
> payable as a transaction fee and Aureus Minerals retaining 10.00% of the gross mineral sales from the  
> production of Yellow Jacket 2A Mine. In addition, Aureus Mineral shall have all rights to future exploration 
> on the Property. 
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 37 
> 
> 03.03.02 AUREUS HOLDINGS 
> 
> Pursuant to the Purchase and Sale Agreement and Addendum, dated August 5, 2023. Aureus Lending, an 
> affiliate of Aureus Holdings acquired the unpatented mineral rights for Yellow Jacket 2A claim and assigned 
> its rights to Aureus Holdings for use as collateral in its ongoing operations. Aureus Holdings is registered 
> 
> owner of Yellow Jacket 2A according to both the County and BLM.  
> 
> Aureus Holdings as the owner of record and will use the Yellow Jacket 2A Claim as part of its collateral pool, 
> pursuant to a guarantee for the issuance of the corporate bonds by Aureus Capital, a special purpose vehicle 
> (“SPV”) wholly owned by Aureus Global Limited. Aureus Holdings has vested the Security Interest in Yellow 
> Jacket 2A by Deed of Trust and Security Agreement to Glas Trust Company LLC, pursuant to the Trust 
> Indenture and bond issuance of Aureus Capital Limited. 
> 
> Aureus Holdings acquired the YJ2A, YJ8, YJ24, YJ25, YJ26, YJ27, YJ30 & YJ31 claims during 2023 and has 
> options on the unpatented mineral claims of YJ4, Y10, & YJ11 in Nevada. 
> 
> Aureus Lending is the lender of record, loan servicer and asset manager for Yellow Jacket 2A on behalf of 
> Aureus Holdings. 
> 
> 03.03.03 GRAND CANYON GOLD CORPORATION 
> 
> All claims and property were transferred by Grand Canyon Gold Corporation to the relevant AUREUS 
> entities, pursuant to the Purchase and Sale Agreement and Addendum, dated August 5, 2023. Grand Canyon 
> Gold Corporation agreed to accept payment of a total of $34,000,000 and to be retained as the Operator of 
> the Yellow Jacket 2A Mine, pursuant to the Addendum, Master Operating and Lease Agreement and Master 
> 
> Finance Agreement. 
> 
> 03.03.04 GLAS TRUST COMPANY LLC

> 
> 03.03.04 GLAS TRUST COMPANY LLC 
> 
> Glas Trust Company LLC, the trustee for the bond issuance of Aureus Capital Limited.  Glas Trust Company 
> LLC, holds a Security Interest Agreement under a Deed of Trust, pursuant to the terms of the Trust 
> Indenture, Security Interest Agreement and Deed of Trust for the benefit of the Aureus Capital Limited bond 
> holders, as assigned within the overall collateral pool for Aureus Capital Limited, Aureus Holding and related 
> affiliates. A Uniform Commercial Code (“UCC”) UCC1 Financing Statement has been filed in the state of 
> Nevada, recording Glas Trust Company LLC as a Secured Party. 
> 
> 03.03.05 OTHER ENCUMBRANCES 
> 
> There are no other encumbrances, liens, mortgages, or legal actions against the properties other than 
> described herein. 
> 
> 03.04 ENVIRONMENTAL LIABILITIES 
> 
> Except for the exploration surface disturbance, primarily related to drilling, and the network of groundwater 
> monitoring wells that will be drilled, there are no known environmental liabilities associated with the Yellow 
> Jacket 2A claim. 
> 
> Surface disturbance that has not been reclaimed will potentially be used for future development activities 
> and access.  
> 
> There are no known environmental issues that are adversely impacting air, water, or soil resources at the 
> Site. Key environmental items addressed this Report are associated with wildlife and archaeology.  
> 
> No suspected tortoise burrows were found in the Yellow Jacket 2A claim area. A “no-drill” zone to protect 
> the tortoise habitat will be established and honored during this Project if tortoises or their burrows are 
> detected in any drilling area. No other protected species were observed. 
> 
> 
> 
> YELLOW JACKET 2A  OCTOBER 31, 2023 
> 
> REGULATION S-K 1300 TECHNICAL REPORT SUMMARY ON PRELIMINARY FEASIBILITY STUDY PAGE 38 
> 
> There are no known sensitive cultural/archaeological sites within the Site area. However, these sites will be 
> effectively avoided should they be discovered during mining operations.

> 02 INTRODUCTION 
> 
> 02.01 INTRODUCTION 
> 
> Maverick Eco-miners, Inc. (“Eco-miners”) was retained by Grand Canyon Gold Corporation in March 2023 
> to conduct a Preliminary Feasibility Study for the Yellow Jacket 2A Project near Moapa, NV (“YJ2A, 
> “Property”, “Site”, “Project”) for the purposes of determining the economic viability of the Project. The 
> resulting information and analysis were then to be complied into draft format.  
> 
> Following the subsequent acquisition by Aureus Global Limited (“Aureus Global”, “Company”) through its 
> subsidiary affiliate Aureus (US) Lending LLC (“Aureus Lending”), Mark Slatten as a Qualified Person and as 
> the Chief Geologist for AUREUS was tasked with reviewing the draft information previously prepared for 
> Grand Canyon Gold Corporation and updating the summary information to reflect the changes associated 
> of ownership and operating structure. 
> 
> All initial information was compiled into a generic form and a final draft summary report produced. 
> Subsequently, that report, key documents and other supporting datapoints have been consolidated into 
> this Regulation S-K 1300 Technical Report Summary on the Preliminary Feasibility Study (the “Study”) of 
> Yellow Jacket 2A. This significantly updated and expanded report conforms to United States Securities and 
> Exchange Commissions in accordance with subpart 229.1300 of the S-K 1300 Disclosure by Registrants 
> Engaged in Mining Operations reporting requirements for issuers. (the “Report”, “TRS-SK1300”). 
> 
> This effort was commissioned and managed by Mark Slatten MS, PG, QP, the CEO and Principal Geologist 
> for Eco-miners, Chief Geologist for AUREUS, with the assistance of administrative personnel.  
> 
> The purpose of this Report is to present the results of a comprehensive evaluation of the economic viability 
> of the Yellow Jacket 2A project.  The claim is on property owned by the Bureau of Land Management 
> (“BLM”). 
> 
> AUREUS owns the Yellow Jacket 2A unpatented mining claim through its wholly owned subsidiary, Aureus 
> (US) Holdings LLC. (“Aureus Holdings”). 
> 
> 02.02 TERMS OF REFERENCE 
> 
> This Report supports disclosures by AUREUS in its Annual Report pursuant to Section 13 or 15(d) of the 
> securities exchange act of 1934 for the fiscal year ended December 31, 2023. 
> 
> Measurement units used in this Report are generally US customary; however, some units, such as analytical 
> and metallurgical test work units may be in metric units. Unless otherwise stated, all monetary amounts 
> are in 2023 United States Dollars (USD).

### 🛠️ 2. **What “Qualified Person” Means (SEC)**
Under **SEC Regulation S-K 1300**, a **Qualified Person** must:
- Be a licensed geologist, engineer, or equivalent with **at least 5 years' experience**.
- Be in **good standing with a professional association**.
- Have experience with the **type of mineralization and deposit** in question.

Mark Slatten qualifies as a **QP** under this rule, making him **authorized to prepare and sign off** on technical reports submitted for regulatory or investment purposes in the U.S.

> 18.01.02 PROJECT EXECUTION 
> 
> The estimate was based on the traditional engineering, procurement, and construction management 
> (EPCM) approach where the EPCM contractor will oversee the delivery of the completed project from 
> detailed engineering and procurement to the transfer of a working facility. The EPCM contractor shall 
> engage and coordinate several subcontractors to complete all work within the given scopes. 
> 
> 18.01.03 ESTIMATE SUMMARY 
> 
> The estimate was derived from budgetary pricing for major items in the mechanical equipment list, 
> electrical equipment list and contractor work packages benchmarked against similar projects and scaled / 
> escalated accordingly. The estimates were based on a number of fundamental assumptions as indicated in 
> mining operations and processing discussions herein. The estimate included all associated infrastructure as 
> defined by the scope of work. 
> 
> The initial capital cost estimate of $96,776,000 is summarized in Table 18-1.

> Content source: Source.file
> Title: YJ2A TRS SK1300 FULL REPORT - FINAL 1.5 (1).pdf
> Content Snippet:
> produced resulting in $12,924,426,240 in undiscounted royalty payments over the LoM. 
> 
> In addition, Grand Canyon Gold Corporation shall retain 10.00% of gross sales as a royalty up to a total of 
> $14,000,000 as part of the purchase price of  Yellow Jacket 2A. 
> 
> 19.06 ECONOMIC ANALYSIS 
> 
> The results of the economic analyses discussed in this section represent forward-looking information 
> statements within the meaning of applicable securities laws relating to Aureus Capital Limited. The results 
> depend on inputs that are subject to a number of known and unknown risks, uncertainties, and other factors 
> that may cause actual results to differ materially from those presented herein. 
> 
> An economic model was developed to estimate annual pre-tax and post-tax cash flows and sensitivities of 
> the Project based on a 11.25% discount rate, run on a constant 2023 United States Dollars basis with no 
> inflation. 
> 
> All inputs to the economic analysis are at a minimum of a feasibility level of confidence, having an accuracy 
> level of ±25% and a contingency range not exceeding 10%, as defined in S-K 1300. 
> 
> The economic analysis was performed using the following assumptions: 
> 
> • The economic analysis contemplated in the Study uses metal prices that remain constant over the Project life

> • Development and construction period of up to three (3) years beginning January 2024. 
> 
> • All construction costs are capitalized. 
> 
> • All equipment leased from an Aureus affiliate. 
> 
> • Commercial production starting (effectively) between September 2026 and January 2027. 
> 
> • Life of Mine of 24 years. 
> 
> • Cost estimates in constant 2023 United States Dollars with no inflation or escalation. 
> 
> • All Capital and Operating costs funded by Aureus Lending providing a secured line of credit to the appointed 
> 
> operator. 
> 
> • Metal is assumed to be sold in the same year it is produced. 
> 
> • No contractual arrangements for refining currently exist. 
> 
> • Closure costs of $10,000,000. 
>

>

> 
> resulting in $12,924,426,240 in undiscounted royalty payments over the LoM.

> 16.03 METAL PRICING AND PROJECTIONS 
> 
> 16.03.01 ECONOMIC ANALYSIS 
> 
> The economic analysis contemplated in the Study uses metal prices that remain constant over the Project 
> life and are based on a gold price of $1,900/oz and a silver price of $20.00/oz based on average trading 
> prices for the month prior to the effective date of this report. All data is provided by Microsoft through a 
> data feed to the AUREUS MiDAS platform. No price inflation or escalation factors were utilized as 
> commodity prices can be volatile, and there is the potential for deviation from the forecast.  
> 
> Metal prices are defined daily by several commodity markets via contract trading. Some of these markets 
> are the London Metal Exchange (LME), the Commodity Exchange (COMEX), the New York Mercantile 
> Exchange (NYMEX), the Chicago Mercantile Exchange (CME), and the London Bullion Market Association 
> (LBMA). 
> 
> Some exchanges define prices on the spot, while others like the LBMA set prices based on offer and demand 
> in the morning (AM) and in the afternoon (PM). Prices are set each day, except weekends and holidays. The 
> gold and silver PM contract values are used to define future and expected gold prices. 
> 
> Given the volatility of the metal prices, industry uses medium term average gold and silver prices as the 
> basis for economic analysis. The tables below show the average prices calculated each month  or each year 
> based on the data feed provided by Microsoft. 
> 
>  
>

## 🤖 ChatGPT

Exactly — **doing multiple bond rounds** would align logically with:
