> 
> Business model
> 
> Since its incorporation, ALLC has continued to develop its mining claims through exploration drilling and geophysical surveying. ALLC has also continued to acquire additional mining claims relating to the Clayton-Ridge Lithium Project.
> 
> ALLC’s proposed  business model will be to further explore and develop the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project as per ALLC’s intended development  program,  which  includes (but is not limited to) :
> 
> the completion of a Bankable  Feasibility  Study  (“BFS”);  
> 
> further exploration of its mining claims;
> 
> commencement of baseline studies required  for  permitting;  
> 
> further development of a process flowsheet to extract lithium and other valuable minerals and 
> 
> the completion of  engineering  and design  for  a  5,000t  per  annum  lithium  carbonate  equivalent  (“LCE”)  mine  and processing plant. 
> 
> If  ALLC  is  satisfied  with  the  results  of  the  BFS,  then  Authium  will  continue  the  development  of  the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project through the completion of engineering and design of a 5,000t per annum LCE mine and processing plant.
> 
>  ALLC currently anticipates that, at completion of the engineering and design phase, the Company will potentially need to raise additional funding required to construct the proposed mine and processing plant. 
> 
> ALLC plans to develop a low cost, low impact, direct extraction lithium production facility to leverage its strategic location to supply the North American electric vehicle market. 
> 
> ALLC currently expects to be able to complete all of this work over the first two years of the Bond. 
> 
> Whilst ALLC is completing the necessary workstreams to be construction ready for the 5,000tpa LCE mine and  processing  plant,  it  will  also  be  investigating  and  progressing  workstreams  for  a  larger  mine. 
> 
> The purpose of this Bond is to raise the Funds necessary to commence mining operations in relation to the Claims in accordance with the Company Business Plan details of which are set out in the BCL-AL Data Room.  The confirmation of title to AL of the Lithium/Alumimium Claims it owns may be viewed in the BCL AL Data Room 
> 
> The Company’s business model  is  to  initially  develop  the  smaller  5,000tpa  LCE  mine  and  processing  plant, followed by a larger 25,000 tpa operation if appropriate at the time. The rationale for the two stages is the speed at which a smaller mine can be developed.
> 
> Key dependencies
> 
> The  key  dependencies  influencing  the  viability  of  Authium’s  business  model,  and  the  Proposed Transaction include (but are not limited to ):
> 
> •the   Company’s   ability   to   raise   the   Minimum   Subscription   amount   under   the  Bond;
> 
> •exploration success;
> 
> •commodity price volatility and exchange rate risks;
> 
> •maintaining title  to  the Clayton  Ridge  Lithium  Project  and  receiving  required  permitting  and approvals to conduct the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project;
> 
> •receiving favourable results on the Company’s proposed economic assessments to support the technical and economic  feasibility  of  lithium  direct  extraction  for  the  Clayton  Ridge  Lithium  Project  at  a commercial scale, including the proposed BFS taking into account the risks of the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project;
> 
> •receiving the funding from the Bond required to carry out the Company’s proposed business model;
> 
> •sufficient worldwide demand for lithium chemicals; and
> 
> •the market price of lithium remaining higher than the Company’s costs of any future production.
> 
> Key risk factors
> 
> Investors should  be  aware  that  if  the funding of the Bond is successful,  the  Company  will  be changing  the  nature  and  scale  of  its  activities,  which  will  expose  the  Company  to  various  risk  factors. 
> 
> These risks are both specific to the industry in which the Company operates, and also relate to the general business and economic environment in which the Company will operate. An investment in the Bond is not risk free (see passim) and Investors MUST consider the risk factors described below, together with information contained elsewhere in this Explanatory Statement. The key risks associated with Authium, and the Proposed Transaction are identified below, noting that the list should not be taken as an exhaustive list of the risk factors to which the Company and its potential Bond investors may be exposed. 
> 
> Risk Explanation
> 
> Speculative investment
> 
> This Private Placement Bond (PPB) should be considered speculative due to the nature of Authium’s business. There cannot be any assurance as to payment of coupons, dividends, return of capital or Risk Explanation of the market value of shares. In particular, the price at which an investor may be able to trade shares may be above or below the price paid for those shares. Potential investors must make their own assessment of the likely risks (see passim) and determine whether an investment in the Company/Bond is appropriate having regard to their own particular circumstances. Careful consideration should be given to all matters raised and the relative risk factors prior to considering an acquisition of Shares/Investment. Some of these risks can be mitigated by the use of appropriate safeguards and actions, but some are outside of the control of the Company and cannot be mitigated. 
> 
> The risks can broadly be classified as those general to investing in resource companies and risks that are specific to an investment in the Company as follows :
> 
> Exploration, geological and development risks
> 
> Mineral exploration and development are both a speculative and high-risk undertaking that may be impeded by circumstances and factors beyond the control of the Company. The Company is subject to customary risks associated with an exploration entity, such as the volatility of industrial mineral prices and exchange rates, exploration costs, native title and indigenous heritage issues and risks with respect to the holding of exploration tenure.
> 
> Funding
> 
> The funds raised under the Public Offer are considered sufficient to meet the immediate objectives of the Company. To support its ongoing operations and implementation of strategies, further funding may be required by the Company in the event that costs exceed estimates or revenues do not meet forecasts.
> 
> Commodity price and currency exchange risks
> 
> As the Company’s potential earnings will be largely derived from the sale of mineral commodities, the Company’s future revenues and cash flows will be impacted by changes in the prices and available markets of these commodities. Any substantial decline in the price of those commodities or increase in transport or distribution costs may have a material adverse effect on the Company and the value of the shares which comprise the PPB. Commodity prices fluctuate and are affected by numerous factors beyond the control of the Company. These factors include current and expected future supply and demand, forward selling by producers, production cost levels in major mineral producing centres as well as macroeconomic conditions such as inflation and interest rates.
> 
> Agents and contractors
> 
> The Directors are unable to predict the risk of financial failure or default or the insolvency of any of the contractors which will be used by the Company in any of its activities or other managerial failure by any of the other service providers used by the Company for any activity. Any default or insolvency is outside the Company’s control and may have an adverse effect on the Company’s operations.
> 
> Environmental risks
> 
> The Company’s activities are subject to the environmental laws inherent in the mining industry and those specific to Nevada, United States of America. The Company intends to conduct its activities in an environmentally responsible manner and in compliance with all applicable laws. However, the Company may be the subject of accidents or unforeseen circumstances that could subject the Company to extensive liability. In addition, environmental approvals may be required from relevant government or regulatory authorities before activities may be undertaken which are likely to impact the environment. Failure or delay in obtaining such approvals will prevent the Company from undertaking its planned activities. Further, the Company is unable to predict the impact of additional environmental laws and regulations that may be adopted in the future, including whether any such laws or regulations would materially increase the Company’s cost of doing business or affect its operations in any area.
> 
> 
> 
> 
> 
> Contract risk
> 
> The operations of the Company will require the involvement of a number of third parties, including suppliers, contractors and customers. With respect to these third parties, and despite applying best practice/s in terms of pre-contracting due diligence, the Directors are unable to completely avoid the risk of:
> 
> •financial failure or default by a participant in any joint venture to which the Company or its subsidiaries may become a party; and/or

>

> 
> The Clayton Ridge Lithium Project’s mineral deposit type is a lithium mineralised illite-smectite claystone within the  Clayton  Valley,  which  is  host  to  multiple  lithium  deposits.  The Clayton Ridge  Lithium  Project borders several mining claims owned by Cypress Development Corporation (TSXV: CYP), Noram Lithium Corporation (TSXV: NRM) and Albemarle Corporation (NYSE: ALB).The Clayton Ridge Lithium Project’s mineral deposit type is lithium mineralised clay, discovered within the interbedded  claystone  and  mudstone  formations  inside  of  the  subject  property.  These units occur as surface outcroppings in the north-eastern corner of the property and continue on strike in a southernly direction below a wedge of alluvial sand and gravel to a depth of roughly one hundred (100) feet below ground surface. 
> 
> The total thickness  of  the  lithium  mineralised  claystone  averages  three-hundred  and twenty (320) feet with an average grade of approximately one thousand and forty (1,040) ppm of lithium.
> 
> The Clayton Ridge Lithium Project encompasses approximately 729 lode claims, and 138 placer claims over 60km2. The Authium Subsidiaries have delineated a JORC Resource of 2.2Mt LCE (above 500ppm Li cut-off grade) and 80Mt of High Purity Aluminium  (Measured and Indicated category) based on 14 drill holes over three drilling campaigns. The ore body at Clayton Ridge is well understood and defined through previous drilling with homogenous mineralisation and a consistent mineralised horizon. Significant exploration exists in addition to the current Mineral Resource, with mineralisation open at depth and to the south.
> 
> [ Insert Plan from Drop Box]
> 
> 
> 
> 
> 
> Location Access 
> 
> The Clayton Ridge  Lithium  Project access  is  by  means  of  paved  roads  arriving  from  Tonopah,  the nearest support centre, on United States Highway 95 South for approximately 34km and turning west for 24km onto Silver Peak Road. 
> 
> There are gravel and dirt access roads from Silver Peak Road that service the power lines. The terrain consists of multiple deeply incised drainage gullies within the colluvial fan that originates from Split Mountain to the east and slopes overall toward the west. 
> 
> There is limited infrastructure on the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project. The local paved roads are well maintained, and there are several gravel roads, including a power line access road onto the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project. The nearest community to the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project is the unincorporated community of Silver Peak.  Much of the population is the temporary workforce for the Mineral Ridge gold mines and the Albemarle Corporation lithium brine operation.  The main commercial centre with readily available resources is Tonopah, located roughly 35 miles northeast of the  subject  property.
> 
> The community of Tonopah is a regional hub for mining, resource materials, and services, including hotels, a grocery store, restaurants, hardware stores, and government offices.
> 
> Authium exploration
> 
> Only a small portion of the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project has been drilled and explored, with the potential to extend current resources with further drilling and exploration. Additionally, there has been no previous mining production on the mining claims held by ALLC. That being said, to date, the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project has been developed by Authium through the drilling of fourteen (14) diamond drill holes during the period from 2018 to 2022. 
> 
> The Clayton Ridge Lithium Project has  an  initial  mineral  resource  estimate  of  two  million,  two  hundred  and  forty-eight  thousand, eight hundred and thirty-eight (2,248,838) metric tonnes of LCE at a cut-off grade of five hundred (500) ppm. 
> 
> Exploration drilling campaigns have also been conducted in part of the claimed area from October 2018 to April 2022 utilising a diamond drillhole (“DDH”) core drilling rig. The 2018 drilling campaign consisting of four (4) drillholes occurred in the southern portion of the subject property. 
> 
> The results of the first drilling campaign were exploratory in nature.
> 
> The 2019 drilling campaign consisted of four (4) drillholes just north of Silver Peak Road to test exploration targets generated from the surface sampling. Assay results from the drilled core confirmed elevated lithium concentrations near a mineralized outcrop discovered during surface sampling and reconnaissance.
> 
> In 2020, a drilling campaign consisting of seven (7) drillholes was designed to further develop the lithium mineralization south  of  Silver  Peak  Road  by  following  the  lithium-mineralized  trend  on  strike  from  the second drilling campaign. 
> 
> The third campaign drill program was paused, as the 2020 project’s targeted exploration volume goal of two hundred thousand (200,000) tons of LCE was met.
> 
> The second and third drilling campaigns consisted of fourteen (14) vertical drillholes with a total drilled footage of roughly nine thousand one hundred (9,100) feet. The borings were drilled to depths between roughly two hundred (200) and twelve hundred (1,200) feet and spaced at roughly one thousand (1,000) feet apart. Each drillhole produced a ninety-six (96) millimeter outside diameter sized core from which core samples were collected, split, and analysed. 
> 
> In early 2022, a resource expansion drilling project was executed by drilling at three locations. These borings were set at a 45-degree angle from vertical and meant to target  both  a  southerly  extension  of  the  lacustrine  claystone  deposits  and  a  geophysical anomaly showing to the south-east.
> 
> Exploration results
> 
> Geologic units possibly containing lithium hosted sedimentary clay identified with Hybrid electromagnetic geophysical surveys (“HSAMT”) conducted in the southern portion of the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project. Follow-up geochemistry  samples  of  outcropping  white  aphanitic  tuff  yielding  results  above  500ppm lithium have confirmed lithium mineralisation at surface.
> 
> 
> 
> 
> 
> Proposed activities at Clayton Ridge Lithium Project
> 
> Following Completion, the Company will change its activities to lithium exploration with a focus on the discovery of lithium deposits and the development of existing lithium deposits at its Clayton Ridge Lithium Project. The proposed plan of work for the next twenty-four (24) months following the Proposed Transaction will be to complete the following activities:
> 
> •Conduct  an   exploration   drilling   program   within   the   prospective   tenements.   To   date   a geophysical and surface sampling program has been conducted on this area;
> 
> •Process Development.  Authium has  previously  completed  extensive  work  on  a  sulphuric  acid leach  process.  This will be completed, and  work  commenced  on  an  alternative  hydrochloric acid process. A single process will be selected from this work; 
> 
> •Completion of a BFS during the final quarter of 2024. This is anticipated to include all the required study work inclusive of mining, process, permitting, marketing, cost and capex analysis;
> 
> •Baseline permitting work was started during 2023 and this will continue into 2024 to support the permitting  requirements  for  the  5,000tpa  LCE  mine  and  processing  plant  initially  and  then  the larger 25,000tpa operation if appropriate at the time; and
> 
> •At the completion of a successful  BFS,  a front-end engineering  and  design  program  will commence which, subject to a successful completion of the program, will place the Company in the position to commence construction of the smaller 5,000tpa LCE mine and processing plant. This proposed plan  of  work  represents  the  current  intention  at  the  date  of  this Offering Memorandum and  is subject to change.

> Project Overview
> 
> This project involves the development of a mining operation and a chemical conversion plant. The total projected cost encompasses both development expenses and capital expenditures. Development costs, including exploration, technology development, feasibility studies, permitting, environmental studies, and infrastructure development, are estimated at USD 157.53 million. The total capital expenditure for the mining operation and chemical conversion plant is projected to be USD 580 million. An additional USD 102 million has been allocated for contingencies. The combined total project cost, covering both development and capital expenditures, is USD 839.24 million. A detailed cost breakdown is provided below.

> Cost Estimation and Accuracy
> 
> The capital cost estimate is categorized as a Class 4 estimate according to the Association for the Advancement of Cost Engineering (AACE), with an accuracy range of -30% to +50%. This estimate is based on pricing data collected in Q4 2023 and Q1 2024, with foreign currency amounts converted to USD at current exchange rates. Costs related to raw water wells, pumps, and distribution are excluded from this estimate.
> 
> Source of Estimates
> 
> Cost estimates are based on the following sources:
> 
> •	Mining Operation: Costs are derived from contract mining assumptions and Phase 1 data from comparable projects.
> 
> •	Process Plant: Equipment costs are based on a mix of experience-based estimates (12%), adjusted quotes (53%), and new vendor budget prices (66%).
> 
> •	Chlor-Alkali Facilities: Costs are derived from current estimates for similar facilities.
> 
> •	Contingencies: A flat rate of 15% is applied due to the lack of a Quantitative Risk Assessment.
> 
> Cost Breakdown
> 
> The project’s cost structure includes:
> 
> •	Engineering Costs: Encompassing basic engineering, detailed engineering, and procurement, calculated as a percentage of the Total Installed Cost (TIC).
> 
> •	Total Direct Field Cost (TDC): Includes equipment and installation costs such as piping, instrumentation, control systems, electrical systems, civil works, and ground improvements.
> 
> •	Total Indirect Field Cost (IFC): Covers home office support, field construction supervision, start-up, commissioning, and temporary construction utilities.
> 
> •	Total Non-Field Cost (NFC): Encompasses contractor overhead, fees, taxes, permits, contingencies, insurance, guarantees, and financial costs.
> 
> Mine Construction Costs
> 
> Mine Construction Costs are calculated by adding the Total Installed Cost (TIC) to additional expenses such as owner’s costs (land acquisition, client team expenses, licenses, and pre-operation costs), pre-operation costs (commissioning, mobilization, staff selection, and training), initial consumables and spare parts, as well as market factors, EPC premiums, and contingencies.
> 
> This detailed financial summary provides a transparent and comprehensive overview of both Development Costs and Mine Construction Costs, ensuring clarity for stakeholders and potential bond investors.

>  Section Six – Corporate Governance
> 
> 
> 
> The Company’s main corporate governance policies and practices are outlined below:
> 
> 
> 
> Board of Directors
> 
> 
> 
> The Board is responsible for corporate governance of the Company. The Board develops strategies for the Company, reviews strategic objectives and monitors performance against those objectives. The goals of the corporate governance processes are to:
> 
> 
> 
> (a)	Maintain and increase member value;
> 
> 
> 
> (b)	Ensure a prudential and ethical basis for the Company’s conduct and activities; and
> 
> 
> 
> (c)	Ensure compliance with the Company’s legal and regulatory objectives.
> 
> 
> 
> Consistent with these goals, the Board assumes the following responsibilities:
> 
> 
> 
> (a)	Developing initiatives for profit and asset growth;
> 
> 
> 
> (b)	Reviewing the corporate, commercial and financial performance of the Company on a regular basis;
> 
> 
> 
> (c)	Acting on behalf of, and being accountable to, the members; and
> 
> 
> 
> (d)	Identifying business risks and implementing actions to manage those risks and corporate systems to assure quality.
> 
> 
> 
> The Company is committed to the circulation of relevant materials to Directors in a timely manner to facilitate Directors’ participation in the Board discussions on a fully informed basis.
> 
> 
> 
> Composition of the Board
> 
> 
> 
> Election of Board members is substantially the province of the members. The Company is committed to the following principles:
> 
> 
> 
> (a)	The Board is to comprise Directors with a blend of skills, experience and attributes appropriate for the Company and its business; and
> 
> 
> 
> (b)	The principal criterion for the appointment of new Directors is their ability to add value to the Company and its business.
> 
> 
> 
> Independent Professional Advice: Subject to the Board’s approval (not to be unreasonably withheld), the Directors, at the Company’s expense, may obtain independent professional advice on issues arising in the course of their duties.
> 
> 
> 
> External Audit: The Company is responsible for the appointment of the external auditors of the Company, and the board will from time to time review the scope, performance and fees of those external auditors.
> 
> Legal and Corporate Structure:  Authium Limited is a privately held limited liability company, formed by The Board of Directors of Authium Limited., a Company incorporated on 15 September 2021 in Sydney Australiaxxxxxxxxx in , with Registered Offices at Bay 6 Middlemiss Street, North Sydney, NSW 2060.

> Section Seven – Description of The Notes
> 
> 
> 
> The Notes shall be secured by means of an Insurance Performance Guarantee in favour of BCL over the Company for and on behalf of the Senior Secured Note Holders entitled under the provisions of and under this Bond over the Company. The Insurance shall remain in place until all Senior Secured Note Holders have been paid in full. Such a procedure will protect the Senior Secured Note Holders in the event of a liquidation or sale of the Company.
> 
> 
> 
> If the Company becomes insolvent or is liquidated, the automatic operation of the Working Capital Loan Agreement will serve to pay the Senior Secured Note Holders first before any other classes of creditors. These assets, which serve as collateral, would be made available to BCL to satisfy the obligations to the Senior Secured Note Holders by BCL. 
> 
> 
> 
> Annual interest of Eight per cent (8.0 %) on the Senior Secured Notes shall be paid on an annual basis on the last day of the calendar year following the issuance of the Bond. The principal sum of $950,000,000 ( Nine Hundred and Fifty Million USD) shall be paid on the maturity date. The Record Date shall be close of Business on the 15th day of the calendar month preceding each applicable Interest Payment Date ("Record Date") UNLESS the 15th day of the month falls on a weekend or Holiday then the Record Date will be on the next Business day. The Notes shall be issued in $10,000,000 increments comprising 10,000,000 x $1 Shares. The Notes are senior debt obligations of BCL and its successors and assigns. Any future parent or subsidiaries of BCL will by operation of law automatically fully and effectually indemnify the Senior Secured Note Holders with unconditional commitments of collateral perfection of the collateral and its attachment to the Notes.
> 
> Section Eight – Plan of Distribution
> 
> The Notes are being offered only to Qualified Institutional Buyers pursuant to the exemption of Rule 144A of the Securities and Exchange Act of 1933, as amended. This is a private sale of securities to institutions by BCL.
> 
> A Qualified Institutional Buyer (QIB) is a corporate entity that falls within the ‘accredited investor’ category, defined in SEC Rule 501 of Regulation D, being an entity that owns and invests, on a discretionary basis, at least $100 million in securities; for a Broker-Dealer the threshold is $10 million. QIBs encompass a wide range of entities, including banks, savings and loans associations, insurance companies, investment companies, employee benefit plans or entities owned entirely by accredited investors. Banks and S&L associations must also have a net worth of at least $25 million to satisfy the QIB criteria.
> 
> The Notes shall be deposited into and delivered through the Depository Transfer Company (DTC) system for electronic transfer only to QIBs. The holding period for the Notes is Ten Years, and therefore exceeds the restricted holding period of Rule 144. In the hands of QIBs, the Notes shall be freely transferable from one QIB to another. No other party, other than a QIB, may purchase or hold the Notes offered hereby.
> 
> BCL will deliver the Notes to QIB holders through its Transfer Agent, Securities Transfer Corporation, 2901 North Dallas Parkway, Suite 380, Plano, Texas 75093.
> 
> The Notes are being privately offered in reliance on an exemption from registration under the Securities Act of 1933 to comply with the resale exemption provided in Rule 144A under the Securities Act.
> 
> The minimum initial purchase of Notes is $10,000,000 (comprising 10,000,000 $1 Shares for each Note) and multiples thereof unless reduced or waived by BENEDICT CAPITAL LIMITED in its sole and absolute discretion. BENEDICT CAPITAL LIMITED may as the Issuer of the Bond vary the face value of the Bond and the Coupon attached thereto at its absolute discretion. Notes are transferable only in compliance with an exemption from registration under the Securities Act.
> 
> Rule 144A, provides a safe harbour from the registration requirements of the Securities Act of 1933 for private resales of minimum $500,000 of restricted securities to qualified institutional buyers (QIBs).
> 
> The Securities will be offered by the BCL on a ‘Best efforts’ basis. All funds shall be made payable to BCL. No commissions shall be paid to officers, shareholders, or employees of BCL for their sales effort. The offering period will commence on the date of this Offering Memorandum and continue, subject to the right of BCL to terminate this offering any time, until the earlier of (i) the sale of all of the Notes offered hereby, or (ii) the expiry of the Term of the Bond whichever is the sooner.
> 
> 
> 
> Securities Transfer Corporation, 2901 North Dallas Parkway, Suite 380, Plano, Texas 75093, a Limited Liability Company, incorporated in Texas, shall be the exclusive Transfer Agent for this offering. BENEDICT CAPITAL LIMITED has entered into a written agreement with the Transfer Agent regarding the terms and conditions of such arrangement.
> 
> There can be no assurance that this Offering qualifies for exemption from federal registration.
> 
> This Offering is being made in reliance upon the “private placement” exemption from registration provided by Section 4(2) of the Securities Act of 1933, as amended and Rule 144A promulgated pursuant thereto. Reliance on these exemptions does not, however, constitute a representation or guarantee that such exemptions are indeed available. If for any reason this Offering is deemed to be a public offering of the Company’s securities (and if no other exemption from registration is available) and the offered Notes and underlying securities are not registered or qualified with the applicable federal or state authorities, the sale of the offered Notes and underlying securities would be deemed to have been made in violation of the applicable laws requiring registration of the offered securities and the delivery of a prospectus. As a remedy in the event of such violation, each purchaser of the offered securities would have the right to rescind his or her purchase of the offered securities and to have his or her purchase price returned. If such a purchaser requests a return of his or her purchase price, funds might not be available for that purpose. In that event, liquidation of the Company might be required. Any refunds made would reduce funds available for our working capital needs. A significant number of requests for rescission would probably cause the Company to be without funds sufficient to respond to such requests or successfully to proceed with our activities. The Company does not currently plan but may use placement agents consistent with state and federal exemptions from registration. In certain circumstances payment to agents could result in the loss of an exemption and would give rise to all of the aforementioned risks.
> 
> BCL will send the following reports to each person who held Securities during the period covered by such report: All Note holders will be provided annually, no later than 180 days from the close of BCL’s fiscal year, with audited financial statements including a balance sheet and the related statements of income, retained earnings, and changes in financial position of BCL.
> 
> Senior Secured Note Holders will have reasonable access to the books and records of BCL. All such books and records will be maintained and prepared at the expense of BCL and will be maintained at the administration offices of BCL in the United Kingdom.
> 
> Section Nine – How to Subscribe
> 
> Persons wishing to subscribe for Notes must tender to BCL a completed Subscription Agreement together with the appropriate subscription payment.
> 
> All persons who purchase the Notes offered hereby will be required to execute a Subscription Agreement, the form of which is attached hereto in section eighteen which provides, in part, that a purchaser may not sell, transfer, pledge, or otherwise dispose of any of such securities in the absence of an effective registration statement covering such transaction under the Securities Act and applicable state securities laws, or the receipt by the Company of an opinion of counsel to the effect that registration is not required.
> 
> Section Ten – Interest Sinking Fund
> 
> BCL shall establish a segregated Bank Account at a major bank in the United Kingdom or other location approved by BCL for the Noteholders, and BCL shall deposit 8 % of the gross proceeds collected from this offering as an Interest Reserve. BCL shall pay the Note Holders their 8.0% interest coupon in cash, paid annually in arrears, to each Note Holder for the Term of the Bond
> 
> The Interest Sinking Fund shall remove the possibility of a default on the Notes by BCL, since the required amount of interest shall be held on Deposit in a segregated bank account solely for the benefit of the Senior Secured Note Holders.
> 
> Section Eleven – Risk Factors
> 
> Prospective Investors should consider carefully each of the risks described below, together with all the other information contained or incorporated by reference in the Offering Memorandum, before deciding to invest in the Notes.
> 
> Potential Investors should consider that an investment in this BCL Bond is highly speculative and should consult their professional advisers before deciding whether to invest in the Senior Secured Notes offered pursuant to this Prospectus.
> 
> Sovereign Risk
> 
> The Company’s most valuable assets are located in USA and as such are located in a stable country with well-established legal system and tax authority therefore the sovereign risk is considered as very safe and good marketable title to its assets is assured.
> 
> Contractual Risks
> 
> The Company will be party to certain agreements relating to the operation of each Project. The ability of the Company to achieve its objectives may depend on the performance by the other parties to these and other contracts the Company may enter into in the future. If a party defaults in the performance of its obligations it may be necessary for the Company to approach a court to seek a legal remedy. Legal action can be costly and there can be no guarantee that a legal remedy will be ultimately granted on the appropriate terms. Outcomes
> 
> which could affect the enforceability of contracts entered into by the Company. The company is incorporated in Delaware and Delaware law is regarded as very effective and enforceable upon defaulting parties.
> 
> Development Risks
> 
> The Project is deemed highly prospective and potential investors should understand these are high-risk undertakings. There can be no assurance that the development of the Project will result in the recovery of economically viable net profits. Even if an apparently viable Project is identified, as is the case in the Projects already identified in this Offering Memorandum there is no guarantee that any one or all of the Projects can be economically exploited. The overhead costs of the Company are based on certain assumptions with respect to the method and timing of the network construction and operation of the Project/s.
> 
> By their nature, these estimates and assumptions are subject to significant uncertainties and, accordingly, the actual costs may materially differ from these estimates and assumptions. Accordingly, no assurance can be given that the cost estimates and the underlying assumptions will be realised in practice, which may materially and adversely affect the Company’s viability.
> 
> Operations
> 
> The operations of the Company may be affected by various factors, including failure to locate or identify sufficiently viable Project/s, failure to achieve predicted returns, operational and technical difficulties encountered in all or any of the Projects 
> 
> Other than those matters contained mentioned and/or referred to in the Environmental Report below (as the case may be) there are no known environmental issues that are adversely impacting the Project 
> 
> While there are no wildlife or plants Threatened or Endangered (T&E) species within the Project there may be sensitive wildlife species or habitat that may exist and depending upon their location may warrant special mitigation.
> 
> There are no known sensitive cultural/archaeological sites within the project area.
> 
> Insurance
> 
> The Company intends to insure its operations in accordance with industry practice. However, in certain circumstances, The Company’s insurance may not be of a nature or level to provide adequate insurance cover. The occurrence of an event that is not covered or fully covered by insurance could have a material adverse effect on the business, financial condition and results of the Company. Insurance against all risks associated with the construction and operation of a fixed wireless and mobile telecommunications business is not always available.
> 
> General economic conditions, introduction of tax reform, new legislation, movements in interest and inflation rates and currency exchange rates may have an adverse effect on the Company’s business development and operation activities, as well as on BCL’s ability to fund those activities.

> 
> 
> ▪	Terrorism or other hostilities.
> 
> The market price of Projects can fall as well as rise and may be subject to varied and unpredictable influences on the market for the Projects in general and commodities in particular. Neither BCL; the Company; nor the Directors warrant the future performance of the Company or any return on an investment in the Company.
> 
>  Dividends
> 
> Any future determination as to the payment of dividends by the Company will be at the discretion of BCL by virtue of its Debenture and Working Capital Loan Agreement and the Directors and will depend on the availability of distributable earnings of the company.
> 
> Taxation
> 
> The purchase of bonds and the repayment of the bonds may have tax consequences for bondholders which will differ depending on the tax jurisdiction of each individual investor. All potential investors in the bonds are urged to obtain independent financial advice concerning their purchase of the bonds. To the maximum extent permitted by law, BCL and/or the Company, its officers and each of their respective advisors accept no liability and responsibility with respect to the taxation consequences of investors purchasing bonds from BCL.
> 
> Additional requirements for Capital
> 
> The Company’s capital requirements depend on numerous factors. Depending on BCL’s ability to generate income from its planned sale of the senior secured notes and subsequently from the Company’s planned operations, the Company may require additional or alternate sources of financing. If BCL and/or the Company are unable to obtain any alternative or additional financing as needed, it may be required to reduce the scope of the Company’s operations and scale back its activities as the case may be. There is however no guarantee that BCL and/or the Company will be able to secure any alternative or additional funding or be able to secure other funding on terms favourable to the Company.
> 
> Reliance on Key Personnel
> 
> The responsibility of overseeing the day-to-day operations and the strategic management of the Company depends substantially on its senior management and its key personnel. There can be no assurance given that there will be no detrimental impact on the Company if one or more of these employees cease their employment. However, the Company anticipates that such impact would if it occurred be of a short-term nature as it could recruit other suitable senior management over a period of time.
> 
> Investment Speculative
> 
> The above factors, those referred to in Section 8 of this Prospectus, and others not specifically referred to above or elsewhere in this Prospectus, may in the future materially affect the financial performance of the Company and the value of the Senior Secured Notes offered under this Prospectus. Therefore, the Senior Secured Notes to be issued pursuant to this Prospectus should be considered carefully and potential investors should consider that the investment is subject to risks described herein and treated as speculative and should consult their professional advisers before deciding whether to purchase the Senior Secured Notes.
>

> 
> 
> The Company’s ability to execute its operating strategy is highly dependent on the Company having access to capital. BCL believes the Company has assessed its long-term liquidity needs through this Senior Secured Note offering. However, the Company may need to implement the use of bank credit facilities, second lien term credit facilities, and other offerings should too few Senior Secured Notes be purchased. Following this offering, BCL may, but will not necessarily, examine the following alternative sources of capital:

>

>

>

> 
> 
> ▪	Joint venture financing; and
> 
> 
>

These factors include general economic and financial market conditions, our credit ratings, interest rates, market perceptions of us or the industry, our market value and operating performance. BCL may be unable to execute the Company’s operating strategy if BCL and/or the Company cannot obtain capital from these sources.

The Company and/or BCL may be forced to raise additional debt or equity proceeds to fund such expenditures. > 
>

>

> 
> 
> 
> The Company may in the future incur, from time to time, debt under credit facilities. Should the borrowing base limitation under the Company’s bank credit facility be re- determined the Company could be forced to repay a portion of the Company’s bank debt. The Company may not have sufficient funds to make such repayments. The Company’s ability to meet expenses and debt obligations will depend on future performance, which will be affected by financial, business, economic, regulatory and other factors.
> 
> 
> 
> The Company will not be able to control many of these factors, such as economic conditions and governmental regulation. The Company cannot be certain that its earnings will be sufficient to allow payment of its principal and interest on debts, including the Notes, and meet the Company’s other obligations. If the Company does not have enough money to service the Company debt, the Company may be required to refinance all or part of the Company existing debt, including the Notes, sell assets, borrow more money, or raise equity. The Company may not be able to refinance the Company debt, sell assets, borrow more money, or raise equity on terms acceptable to the Company, if at all.

> # 【0†BCL Authium LLC Bond Amended Second Draft IRSJ 04092024 (3).docx†file-UDzyS2aGpyQhhBRnZRnLuZ】

> Title: BCL Authium LLC Bond Amended Second Draft IRSJ 04092024 (3).docx
> Content Snippet:
> Authium LLC (Nevada LLC) (ALLC)
> 
> 
> 
>                                    AUTHIUM LLC ( Nevada Corporation) (ALLC)
> 
> PLEASE INSERT APPROPRIATE DETAILS EG  -DATE AND PLACE  OF FORMATION – REGISTERED OFFICE – EIN NO. BUSINESS LICENCE etc SHAREHOLDER DETAILS 
> 
> Business model
> 
> Since its incorporation, ALLC has continued to develop its mining claims through exploration drilling and geophysical surveying. ALLC has also continued to acquire additional mining claims relating to the Clayton-Ridge Lithium Project.
> 
> ALLC’s proposed  business model will be to further explore and develop the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project as per ALLC’s intended development  program,  which  includes (but is not limited to) :
> 
> the completion of a Bankable  Feasibility  Study  (“BFS”);  
> 
> further exploration of its mining claims;
> 
> commencement of baseline studies required  for  permitting;  
> 
> further development of a process flowsheet to extract lithium and other valuable minerals and 
> 
> the completion of  engineering  and design  for  a  5,000t  per  annum  lithium  carbonate  equivalent  (“LCE”)  mine  and processing plant. 
> 
> If  ALLC  is  satisfied  with  the  results  of  the  BFS,  then  Authium  will  continue  the  development  of  the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project through the completion of engineering and design of a 5,000t per annum LCE mine and processing plant.
> 
>  ALLC currently anticipates that, at completion of the engineering and design phase, the Company will potentially need to raise additional funding required to construct the proposed mine and processing plant. 
> 
> ALLC plans to develop a low cost, low impact, direct extraction lithium production facility to leverage its strategic location to supply the North American electric vehicle market. 
> 
> ALLC currently expects to be able to complete all of this work over the first two years of the Bond. 
> 
> Whilst ALLC is completing the necessary workstreams to be construction ready for the 5,000tpa LCE mine and  processing  plant,  it  will  also  be  investigating  and  progressing  workstreams  for  a  larger  mine. 
> 
> The purpose of this Bond is to raise the Funds necessary to commence mining operations in relation to the Claims in accordance with the Company Business Plan details of which are set out in the BCL-AL Data Room.  The confirmation of title to AL of the Lithium/Alumimium Claims it owns may be viewed in the BCL AL Data Room 
> 
> The Company’s business model  is  to  initially  develop  the  smaller  5,000tpa  LCE  mine  and  processing  plant, followed by a larger 25,000 tpa operation if appropriate at the time. The rationale for the two stages is the speed at which a smaller mine can be developed.
> 
> Key dependencies
> 
> The  key  dependencies  influencing  the  viability  of  Authium’s  business  model,  and  the  Proposed Transaction include (but are not limited to ):
> 
> •the   Company’s   ability   to   raise   the   Minimum   Subscription   amount   under   the  Bond;
> 
> •exploration success;
> 
> •commodity price volatility and exchange rate risks;
> 
> •maintaining title  to  the Clayton  Ridge  Lithium  Project  and  receiving  required  permitting  and approvals to conduct the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project;
> # 【1†BCL Authium LLC Bond Amended Second Draft IRSJ 04092024 (3).docx†file-UDzyS2aGpyQhhBRnZRnLuZ】

> Authium has forged agreements with partners for its Exploration,Design, Impact, Risk Management, Accounting & Auditing, and IT/Technology divisions, and they have deployed dedicated management teams focused on developing Authium's business interest in the Project.
> 
> 
> 
>  Section Six – Corporate Governance
> 
> 
> 
> The Company’s main corporate governance policies and practices are outlined below:
> 
> 
> 
> Board of Directors
> 
> 
> 
> The Board is responsible for corporate governance of the Company. The Board develops strategies for the Company, reviews strategic objectives and monitors performance against those objectives. The goals of the corporate governance processes are to:
> 
> 
> 
> (a)	Maintain and increase member value;
> 
> 
> 
> (b)	Ensure a prudential and ethical basis for the Company’s conduct and activities; and
> 
> 
> 
> (c)	Ensure compliance with the Company’s legal and regulatory objectives.
> 
> 
> 
> Consistent with these goals, the Board assumes the following responsibilities:
> 
> 
> 
> (a)	Developing initiatives for profit and asset growth;
> 
> 
> 
> (b)	Reviewing the corporate, commercial and financial performance of the Company on a regular basis;
> 
> 
> 
> (c)	Acting on behalf of, and being accountable to, the members; and
> 
> 
> 
> (d)	Identifying business risks and implementing actions to manage those risks and corporate systems to assure quality.
> 
> 
> 
> The Company is committed to the circulation of relevant materials to Directors in a timely manner to facilitate Directors’ participation in the Board discussions on a fully informed basis.
> 
> 
> 
> Composition of the Board
> 
> 
> 
> Election of Board members is substantially the province of the members. The Company is committed to the following principles:
> 
> 
> 
> (a)	The Board is to comprise Directors with a blend of skills, experience and attributes appropriate for the Company and its business; and
> 
> 
> 
> (b)	The principal criterion for the appointment of new Directors is their ability to add value to the Company and its business.
> 
> 
> 
> Independent Professional Advice: Subject to the Board’s approval (not to be unreasonably withheld), the Directors, at the Company’s expense, may obtain independent professional advice on issues arising in the course of their duties.
> 
> 
> 
> External Audit: The Company is responsible for the appointment of the external auditors of the Company, and the board will from time to time review the scope, performance and fees of those external auditors.
> 
> Legal and Corporate Structure:  Authium Limited is a privately held limited liability company, formed by The Board of Directors of Authium Limited., a Company incorporated on 15 September 2021 in Sydney Australiaxxxxxxxxx in , with Registered Offices at Bay 6 Middlemiss Street, North Sydney, NSW 2060. 
> 
>  
> 
> Section Seven – Description of The Notes
> 
> 
> 
> The Notes shall be secured by means of an Insurance Performance Guarantee in favour of BCL over the Company for and on behalf of the Senior Secured Note Holders entitled under the provisions of and under this Bond over the Company. The Insurance shall remain in place until all Senior Secured Note Holders have been paid in full. Such a procedure will protect the Senior Secured Note Holders in the event of a liquidation or sale of the Company.
> 
> 
> 
> If the Company becomes insolvent or is liquidated, the automatic operation of the Working Capital Loan Agreement will serve to pay the Senior Secured Note Holders first before any other classes of creditors. These assets, which serve as collateral, would be made available to BCL to satisfy the obligations to the Senior Secured Note Holders by BCL. 
> 
> 
> 
> Annual interest of Eight per cent (8.0 %) on the Senior Secured Notes shall be paid on an annual basis on the last day of the calendar year following the issuance of the Bond. The principal sum of $950,000,000 ( Nine Hundred and Fifty Million USD) shall be paid on the maturity date.

> BCL is offering only to Qualified Institutional Buyers (‘QIB’) up to $950,000,000 (Nine Hundred and Fifty Million USD) in aggregate principal amount 10 YEAR 8 %SENIOR SECURED NOTES (the ‘Notes’). The Notes, which may be acquired in minimum denominations of $10,000,000 (Ten million U.S. Dollars), comprising ten million Shares per Note at an OFFERING PRICE of $1 (one US Dollar) per Share, shall be secured by all of the assets of the Company’s Project Profiles as described mentioned or referred to in this Offering Memorandum, 
>

September 2024 and shall be due for repayment after TEN Years, together with 8% annual interest (payable annually in arrears) on the maturity date of 30th. September 2034.The Notes bear interest at 8 % per annum (payable annually in arrears) and such interest shall be calculated as of the issue date of 30th. September 2024 and paid to Note holders annually up to and including the maturity date of 30th. September 2034. The Notes (also referred to as the ‘Bonds’) offered in this Offering Memorandum will constitute a single class of debt securities with total gross proceeds of $950,000,000 (Nine Hundred and Fifty Million USD) and are offered pursuant to an exemption from registration pursuant to rule 144 A of the Securities and Exchange Act of 1933 as amended. BCL will apply to the Depository Trust Company (‘DTC’) to become DTC eligible and to be able to deliver the Notes electronically to DTC Participants.

> SECTION ONE – THE COMPANY
> 
> Authium LLC is a privately held limited liability company, formed by The Board of Directors of Authium LLC., a Company incorporated in New South Wales Australia, with Registered Offices at Bay 6 Middlemiss Street, Lavender Bay, North Sydney, NSW 2060
> 
> By virtue of a series of Corporate Events and Resolutions from December 19th 2018 to 30th December 2019 the Company eneterd into a Joinder Agreement with Link Road Inc. (LRI) a Nevada Corporation to enable the effective operation of the The Clayton Ridge Lithium Project.  Full details of all Agreeents and Resolutions are comprised in the BCL-ALLC Data Room.
> 
> 
> 
> SECTION TWO – Summary of Business Activities 
> 
> The following summary contains information about the Company’s business activities and BCL’s offering of the Secured Notes. It does not contain all of the information that a prospective investor should consider in making their investment decision. For a more complete understanding of the Company’s, business plan, and this Offering, prospective investors should read and carefully consider all of the information in the entire Offering Memorandum, as well as the supporting documents and due diligence materials as referenced herein (see Section Fourteen – Due Diligence Documents, page 32).
> 
> The Company: 
> 
> Authium LLC owns and operates the Authium Clayton Ridge Lithium Project, comprising approximately 929 unpatented mining claims (“Tenements”), encompassing the Clayton Ridge lithium claystone property located at  the  Clayton  Valley,  western-central  Nevada,  United  States  of  America  (“Clayton  Ridge  Lithium  Project”) 
> 
> The Company is focused and determined to raise capital by the monetization of properties comprised within this Bond as well the production of the aforementioned precious metals.
> 
> The Company believes in a minimal risk philosophy and therefore will approach funding their construction projects via a full underwriting review by our consultants in mining, new construction, and banking.
> 
> The Company's target future services will be inter alia in the field of mining.

> Section Seven – Description of The Notes
> 
> 
> 
> The Notes shall be secured by means of an Insurance Performance Guarantee in favour of BCL over the Company for and on behalf of the Senior Secured Note Holders entitled under the provisions of and under this Bond over the Company. The Insurance shall remain in place until all Senior Secured Note Holders have been paid in full. Such a procedure will protect the Senior Secured Note Holders in the event of a liquidation or sale of the Company.
> 
> 
> 
> If the Company becomes insolvent or is liquidated, the automatic operation of the Working Capital Loan Agreement will serve to pay the Senior Secured Note Holders first before any other classes of creditors. These assets, which serve as collateral, would be made available to BCL to satisfy the obligations to the Senior Secured Note Holders by BCL. 
> 
> 
> 
> Annual interest of Eight per cent (8.0 %) on the Senior Secured Notes shall be paid on an annual basis on the last day of the calendar year following the issuance of the Bond. The principal sum of $950,000,000 ( Nine Hundred and Fifty Million USD) shall be paid on the maturity date. The Record Date shall be close of Business on the 15th day of the calendar month preceding each applicable Interest Payment Date ("Record Date") UNLESS the 15th day of the month falls on a weekend or Holiday then the Record Date will be on the next Business day. The Notes shall be issued in $10,000,000 increments comprising 10,000,000 x $1 Shares. The Notes are senior debt obligations of BCL and its successors and assigns. Any future parent or subsidiaries of BCL will by operation of law automatically fully and effectually indemnify the Senior Secured Note Holders with unconditional commitments of collateral perfection of the collateral and its attachment to the Notes.
> 
> Section Eight – Plan of Distribution
> 
> The Notes are being offered only to Qualified Institutional Buyers pursuant to the exemption of Rule 144A of the Securities and Exchange Act of 1933, as amended. This is a private sale of securities to institutions by BCL.
> 
> A Qualified Institutional Buyer (QIB) is a corporate entity that falls within the ‘accredited investor’ category, defined in SEC Rule 501 of Regulation D, being an entity that owns and invests, on a discretionary basis, at least $100 million in securities; for a Broker-Dealer the threshold is $10 million. QIBs encompass a wide range of entities, including banks, savings and loans associations, insurance companies, investment companies, employee benefit plans or entities owned entirely by accredited investors. Banks and S&L associations must also have a net worth of at least $25 million to satisfy the QIB criteria.

BCL is a private limited company registered in England and Wales under Company Number 13981087 whose Corporate Information Schedule (CIS) is more particularly set out in Section Sixteen.
> 
> 
> 
> The Company is a Limited Liability Company and was formed as a tax efficient entity for its members to engage inter alia in any lawful business  
> 
> 
>

> 
> 
> 
> The Offering does not purport to be all-inclusive or necessarily contain all information that a prospective interested party may desire for purchasing the Secured Notes of the Company. In all cases, interested parties should conduct their own investigation of the Secured Notes and analysis of the Company and of the data set forth in this Offering Memorandum and the related due diligence documents in BCL’s OneDrive file sharing system.
> 
> 
> 
> In particular, interested parties should consider the advice of their financial, legal, accounting, tax and other business advisors and such other factors that they consider appropriate in investigating and analysing the Company and such data. By accepting this Offering Memorandum, the recipient agrees to keep confidential the information contained herein or made available in connection with any further investigation of the Company.
> 
> 
> 
> This Offering Memorandum should not be photocopied, reproduced, or distributed to others at any time without the prior written consent of BCL. By accepting the Offering Memorandum, the recipient also agrees to comply with the terms stated on this page, as they relate to the Offering Memorandum and other evaluation material and agrees to return the Offering Memorandum and evaluation materials to BCL should the recipient terminate its review of a possible transaction involving the Company or should BCL request the return of such materials.

> # 【6†BCL Authium LLC Bond Amended Second Draft IRSJ 04092024 (3).docx†file-UDzyS2aGpyQhhBRnZRnLuZ】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: BCL Authium LLC Bond Amended Second Draft IRSJ 04092024 (3).docx
> Content Snippet:
> Current Project: The Company owns the Mining Claims as set out in the BCL-AL Data Room
> 
> The Clayton Ridge Lithium Project is located in western Nevada in Esmerelda County’s Clayton Valley. The Project is located next to Abermarle’s  Silver  Peak  Mine,  being North  America’s  only  producing  lithium  mine,  and  is adjacent to several advanced lithium clay resources, including those owned by Century Lithium Corp (TSXV:CYP) and Noram Lithium Corp (TSXV:NRM).
> 
> Encompassing 729 lode claims and 138 placer claims over 60 square km. The Clayton Ridge Lithium Project has Mineral Resource estimates of 2.2Mt LCE and 80Mt High Purity Aluminium (both Measured and Indicated category) with drilling only represents a small portion of the highly prospective tenure. The estimates have been reported in accordance with the JORC Code (2012) guidelines in the Independent Geological Report located in the BCL-AL Data Room.
> 
> Location & Tenure
> 
> The Clayton Ridge project is in western-central Nevada in Esmerelda County’s Clayton Valley, approximately 48 km (30 miles) southwest of Tonopah, Nevada (see figure 3). The property is on the eastern edge of Clayton Valley, with Angel Island to the west and Split Mountain and Clayton Ridge to the east. The yellow rectangle in Figure 3 depicts the location of the area/volume that has been estimated.
> 
> Future project development 
> 
> Planned exploration and development activities 
> 
> Following the funding of the Bond these include (but are not limited to) :
> 
> •Undertaking resource extension drilling in the southern portion of the Project Area.
> 
> •Completion of a bankable feasibility study for the commissioning of a 5ktpa LCE plant.
> 
> •The establishment of a pilot plant and design and permitting activities required to undertake construction.
> 
> Authium LLC Technical Team
> 
> The Authium  technical  team  was  heavily  involved  in  the  development  of the  direct  lithium  extraction(DLE) technology at Rincon Lithium known as The Rincon Lithium Project –a large, lithium-brine project located in the heart of the  ‘lithium  triangle’  in  Salar  Del  Rincón,  Salta  Province,  Argentina.
> 
> Rincon had  along-life,  scalable  resource capable of producing battery-grade lithium carbonate from raw brine with a total Mineral Resource Estimate of 11.8Mt LCE. 
> 
> Authium LLC Incoming Managing Director Todd Vains was previously the Chief Operating Officer of Rincon Ltd and was responsible for its lithium, mining and smelting operations. Mr Vains worked closely with the engineering teams in the delivery of the Rincon DLE Demonstration Plant.
> 
>  Additionally, MrCameron Stanton (who is also joining Authium), was the co-inventor of Rincon lithium’s extraction process and was the former Technical Director  of  Rincon  and  led  the  engineering design and construction of the Commercial Demonstration Plant on the Rincon Salar. 
> 
> Rincon Limited sold the Rincon Lithium asset to Rio Tinto for $825 Million in 2021 post the successful operation of the Commercial Demonstration Plant.
> 
> 
> 
> Overview of Authium
> 
> General
> 
> Authium was incorporated on 14 September 2021 as an Australian proprietary company limited by shares (and later converted to a public company on 21 June 2022)   a Company limited by shares  for the purposes of lithium exploration and development at mining claims located in the Clayton Valley, western-central Nevada, United States of America.

> Project Overview
> 
> This project involves the development of a mining operation and a chemical conversion plant. The total projected cost encompasses both development expenses and capital expenditures. Development costs, including exploration, technology development, feasibility studies, permitting, environmental studies, and infrastructure development, are estimated at USD 157.53 million. The total capital expenditure for the mining operation and chemical conversion plant is projected to be USD 580 million. An additional USD 102 million has been allocated for contingencies. The combined total project cost, covering both development and capital expenditures, is USD 839.24 million. A detailed cost breakdown is provided below.

> # 【10†BCL Authium LLC Bond Amended Second Draft IRSJ 04092024 (3).docx†file-UDzyS2aGpyQhhBRnZRnLuZ】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: BCL Authium LLC Bond Amended Second Draft IRSJ 04092024 (3).docx
> Content Snippet:
> •The establishment of a pilot plant and design and permitting activities required to undertake construction.
> 
> Authium LLC Technical Team
> 
> The Authium  technical  team  was  heavily  involved  in  the  development  of the  direct  lithium  extraction(DLE) technology at Rincon Lithium known as The Rincon Lithium Project –a large, lithium-brine project located in the heart of the  ‘lithium  triangle’  in  Salar  Del  Rincón,  Salta  Province,  Argentina.
> 
> Rincon had  along-life,  scalable  resource capable of producing battery-grade lithium carbonate from raw brine with a total Mineral Resource Estimate of 11.8Mt LCE. 
> 
> Authium LLC Incoming Managing Director Todd Vains was previously the Chief Operating Officer of Rincon Ltd and was responsible for its lithium, mining and smelting operations. Mr Vains worked closely with the engineering teams in the delivery of the Rincon DLE Demonstration Plant.
> 
>  Additionally, MrCameron Stanton (who is also joining Authium), was the co-inventor of Rincon lithium’s extraction process and was the former Technical Director  of  Rincon  and  led  the  engineering design and construction of the Commercial Demonstration Plant on the Rincon Salar. 
> 
> Rincon Limited sold the Rincon Lithium asset to Rio Tinto for $825 Million in 2021 post the successful operation of the Commercial Demonstration Plant.
> 
> 
> 
> Overview of Authium
> 
> General
> 
> Authium was incorporated on 14 September 2021 as an Australian proprietary company limited by shares (and later converted to a public company on 21 June 2022)   a Company limited by shares  for the purposes of lithium exploration and development at mining claims located in the Clayton Valley, western-central Nevada, United States of America. 
> 
> The Clayton Ridge Lithium Project’s mineral deposit type is a lithium mineralised illite-smectite claystone within the Clayton Valley, which is host  to  multiple  lithium  deposits.  The Clayton Ridge Lithium Project borders several mining claims owned by Cypress Development Corporation (TSXV: CYP), Noram Lithium Corporation (TSXV: NRM) and Albemarle Corporation (NYSE: ALB).
> 
> The Clayton Ridge Lithium Project’s mineral deposit type is lithium mineralised clay, discovered within the interbedded claystone and mudstone formations inside of the subject property.  These units occur as surface outcroppings in the north-eastern corner of the property and continue on strike in a southernly direction below a wedge of alluvial sand and gravel to a depth of roughly one hundred (100) feet below ground surface.  The total thickness of the lithium mineralised  claystone  averages  three-hundred  and twenty (320) feet with an average grade of approximately one thousand and forty (1,040) ppm of lithium.
> 
> The  Clayton  Ridge  Lithium  Project  encompasses  approximately  seven  hundred  and  twenty-nine  (729) lode  claims,  and  138  placer  claims  over  60km2.  The  Authium  Subsidiaries  have  delineated  a  JORC Resource of 2.2Mt LCE (above 500ppm Li cut-off grade) and 80Mt of High Purity Aluminium (Measured and Indicated category) based on fourteen (14) drill holes over three drilling campaigns. 
> 
> The ore body at Clayton Ridge is well understood and  defined  through  previous  drilling  with  homogenous  mineralisation  and  a  consistent  mineralised horizon. Significant exploration exists in addition to the current Mineral Resource, with mineralisation open at depth and to the south.
> 
> Company Structure

> # 【12†BCL Authium LLC Bond Amended Second Draft IRSJ 04092024 (3).docx†file-UDzyS2aGpyQhhBRnZRnLuZ】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: BCL Authium LLC Bond Amended Second Draft IRSJ 04092024 (3).docx
> Content Snippet:
> ALLC plans to develop a low cost, low impact, direct extraction lithium production facility to leverage its strategic location to supply the North American electric vehicle market. 
> 
> ALLC currently expects to be able to complete all of this work over the first two years of the Bond. 
> 
> Whilst ALLC is completing the necessary workstreams to be construction ready for the 5,000tpa LCE mine and  processing  plant,  it  will  also  be  investigating  and  progressing  workstreams  for  a  larger  mine. 
> 
> The purpose of this Bond is to raise the Funds necessary to commence mining operations in relation to the Claims in accordance with the Company Business Plan details of which are set out in the BCL-AL Data Room.  The confirmation of title to AL of the Lithium/Alumimium Claims it owns may be viewed in the BCL AL Data Room 
> 
> The Company’s business model  is  to  initially  develop  the  smaller  5,000tpa  LCE  mine  and  processing  plant, followed by a larger 25,000 tpa operation if appropriate at the time. The rationale for the two stages is the speed at which a smaller mine can be developed.
> 
> Key dependencies
> 
> The  key  dependencies  influencing  the  viability  of  Authium’s  business  model,  and  the  Proposed Transaction include (but are not limited to ):
> 
> •the   Company’s   ability   to   raise   the   Minimum   Subscription   amount   under   the  Bond;
> 
> •exploration success;
> 
> •commodity price volatility and exchange rate risks;
> 
> •maintaining title  to  the Clayton  Ridge  Lithium  Project  and  receiving  required  permitting  and approvals to conduct the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project;
> 
> •receiving favourable results on the Company’s proposed economic assessments to support the technical and economic  feasibility  of  lithium  direct  extraction  for  the  Clayton  Ridge  Lithium  Project  at  a commercial scale, including the proposed BFS taking into account the risks of the Clayton Ridge Lithium Project;
> 
> •receiving the funding from the Bond required to carry out the Company’s proposed business model;
> 
> •sufficient worldwide demand for lithium chemicals; and
> 
> •the market price of lithium remaining higher than the Company’s costs of any future production.
> 
> Key risk factors
> 
> Investors should  be  aware  that  if  the funding of the Bond is successful,  the  Company  will  be changing  the  nature  and  scale  of  its  activities,  which  will  expose  the  Company  to  various  risk  factors. 
> 
> These risks are both specific to the industry in which the Company operates, and also relate to the general business and economic environment in which the Company will operate. An investment in the Bond is not risk free (see passim) and Investors MUST consider the risk factors described below, together with information contained elsewhere in this Explanatory Statement. The key risks associated with Authium, and the Proposed Transaction are identified below, noting that the list should not be taken as an exhaustive list of the risk factors to which the Company and its potential Bond investors may be exposed. 
> 
> Risk Explanation
> 
> Speculative investment
> 
> This Private Placement Bond (PPB) should be considered speculative due to the nature of Authium’s business. There cannot be any assurance as to payment of coupons, dividends, return of capital or Risk Explanation of the market value of shares. In particular, the price at which an investor may be able to trade shares may be above or below the price paid for those shares.
> # 【13†BCL Authium LLC Bond Amended Second Draft IRSJ 04092024 (3).docx†file-UDzyS2aGpyQhhBRnZRnLuZ】
> Content source: Source.file
> Title: BCL Authium LLC Bond Amended Second Draft IRSJ 04092024 (3).docx
> Content Snippet:
> Annual interest of Eight per cent (8.0 %) on the Senior Secured Notes shall be paid on an annual basis on the last day of the calendar year following the issuance of the Bond. The principal sum of $950,000,000 ( Nine Hundred and Fifty Million USD) shall be paid on the maturity date. The Record Date shall be close of Business on the 15th day of the calendar month preceding each applicable Interest Payment Date ("Record Date") UNLESS the 15th day of the month falls on a weekend or Holiday then the Record Date will be on the next Business day. The Notes shall be issued in $10,000,000 increments comprising 10,000,000 x $1 Shares. The Notes are senior debt obligations of BCL and its successors and assigns. Any future parent or subsidiaries of BCL will by operation of law automatically fully and effectually indemnify the Senior Secured Note Holders with unconditional commitments of collateral perfection of the collateral and its attachment to the Notes.
> 
> Section Eight – Plan of Distribution
> 
> The Notes are being offered only to Qualified Institutional Buyers pursuant to the exemption of Rule 144A of the Securities and Exchange Act of 1933, as amended. This is a private sale of securities to institutions by BCL.
> 
> A Qualified Institutional Buyer (QIB) is a corporate entity that falls within the ‘accredited investor’ category, defined in SEC Rule 501 of Regulation D, being an entity that owns and invests, on a discretionary basis, at least $100 million in securities; for a Broker-Dealer the threshold is $10 million. QIBs encompass a wide range of entities, including banks, savings and loans associations, insurance companies, investment companies, employee benefit plans or entities owned entirely by accredited investors. Banks and S&L associations must also have a net worth of at least $25 million to satisfy the QIB criteria.
> 
> The Notes shall be deposited into and delivered through the Depository Transfer Company (DTC) system for electronic transfer only to QIBs. The holding period for the Notes is Ten Years, and therefore exceeds the restricted holding period of Rule 144. In the hands of QIBs, the Notes shall be freely transferable from one QIB to another. No other party, other than a QIB, may purchase or hold the Notes offered hereby.
> 
> BCL will deliver the Notes to QIB holders through its Transfer Agent, Securities Transfer Corporation, 2901 North Dallas Parkway, Suite 380, Plano, Texas 75093.
> 
> The Notes are being privately offered in reliance on an exemption from registration under the Securities Act of 1933 to comply with the resale exemption provided in Rule 144A under the Securities Act.
> 
> The minimum initial purchase of Notes is $10,000,000 (comprising 10,000,000 $1 Shares for each Note) and multiples thereof unless reduced or waived by BENEDICT CAPITAL LIMITED in its sole and absolute discretion. BENEDICT CAPITAL LIMITED may as the Issuer of the Bond vary the face value of the Bond and the Coupon attached thereto at its absolute discretion. Notes are transferable only in compliance with an exemption from registration under the Securities Act.
> 
> Rule 144A, provides a safe harbour from the registration requirements of the Securities Act of 1933 for private resales of minimum $500,000 of restricted securities to qualified institutional buyers (QIBs).

> 
> The Securities will be offered by the BCL on a ‘Best efforts’ basis. All funds shall be made payable to BCL. No commissions shall be paid to officers, shareholders, or employees of BCL for their sales effort. The offering period will commence on the date of this Offering Memorandum and continue, subject to the right of BCL to terminate this offering any time, until the earlier of (i) the sale of all of the Notes offered hereby, or (ii) the expiry of the Term of the Bond whichever is the sooner.

> 
> 
> Securities Transfer Corporation, 2901 North Dallas Parkway, Suite 380, Plano, Texas 75093, a Limited Liability Company, incorporated in Texas, shall be the exclusive Transfer Agent for this offering. > There can be no assurance that this Offering qualifies for exemption from federal registration.
> 
> This Offering is being made in reliance upon the “private placement” exemption from registration provided by Section 4(2) of the Securities Act of 1933, as amended and Rule 144A promulgated pursuant thereto. Reliance on these exemptions does not, however, constitute a representation or guarantee that such exemptions are indeed available. If for any reason this Offering is deemed to be a public offering of the Company’s securities (and if no other exemption from registration is available) and the offered Notes and underlying securities are not registered or qualified with the applicable federal or state authorities, the sale of the offered Notes and underlying securities would be deemed to have been made in violation of the applicable laws requiring registration of the offered securities and the delivery of a prospectus. As a remedy in the event of such violation, each purchaser of the offered securities would have the right to rescind his or her purchase of the offered securities and to have his or her purchase price returned. If such a purchaser requests a return of his or her purchase price, funds might not be available for that purpose. In that event, liquidation of the Company might be required. Any refunds made would reduce funds available for our working capital needs. A significant number of requests for rescission would probably cause the Company to be without funds sufficient to respond to such requests or successfully to proceed with our activities.

In particular, the price at which an investor may be able to trade shares may be above or below the price paid for those shares. Potential investors must make their own assessment of the likely risks (see passim) and determine whether an investment in the Company/Bond is appropriate having regard to their own particular circumstances. Careful consideration should be given to all matters raised and the relative risk factors prior to considering an acquisition of Shares/Investment. Some of these risks can be mitigated by the use of appropriate safeguards and actions, but some are outside of the control of the Company and cannot be mitigated. 
> 
> The risks can broadly be classified as those general to investing in resource companies and risks that are specific to an investment in the Company as follows :
> 
> Exploration, geological and development risks
> 
> Mineral exploration and development are both a speculative and high-risk undertaking that may be impeded by circumstances and factors beyond the control of the Company. The Company is subject to customary risks associated with an exploration entity, such as the volatility of industrial mineral prices and exchange rates, exploration costs, native title and indigenous heritage issues and risks with respect to the holding of exploration tenure.
> 
> Funding
> 
> The funds raised under the Public Offer are considered sufficient to meet the immediate objectives of the Company. To support its ongoing operations and implementation of strategies, further funding may be required by the Company in the event that costs exceed estimates or revenues do not meet forecasts.
> 
> Commodity price and currency exchange risks
> 
> As the Company’s potential earnings will be largely derived from the sale of mineral commodities, the Company’s future revenues and cash flows will be impacted by changes in the prices and available markets of these commodities. Any substantial decline in the price of those commodities or increase in transport or distribution costs may have a material adverse effect on the Company and the value of the shares which comprise the PPB. Commodity prices fluctuate and are affected by numerous factors beyond the control of the Company. These factors include current and expected future supply and demand, forward selling by producers, production cost levels in major mineral producing centres as well as macroeconomic conditions such as inflation and interest rates.
> 
> Agents and contractors

> Source of Estimates
> 
> Cost estimates are based on the following sources:
> 
> •	Mining Operation: Costs are derived from contract mining assumptions and Phase 1 data from comparable projects.
> 
> •	Process Plant: Equipment costs are based on a mix of experience-based estimates (12%), adjusted quotes (53%), and new vendor budget prices (66%).
> 
> •	Chlor-Alkali Facilities: Costs are derived from current estimates for similar facilities.
> 
> •	Contingencies: A flat rate of 15% is applied due to the lack of a Quantitative Risk Assessment.
> 
> Cost Breakdown
> 
> The project’s cost structure includes:
> 
> •	Engineering Costs: Encompassing basic engineering, detailed engineering, and procurement, calculated as a percentage of the Total Installed Cost (TIC).
> 
> •	Total Direct Field Cost (TDC): Includes equipment and installation costs such as piping, instrumentation, control systems, electrical systems, civil works, and ground improvements.
> 
> •	Total Indirect Field Cost (IFC): Covers home office support, field construction supervision, start-up, commissioning, and temporary construction utilities.
> 
> •	Total Non-Field Cost (NFC): Encompasses contractor overhead, fees, taxes, permits, contingencies, insurance, guarantees, and financial costs.
> 
> Mine Construction Costs
> 
> Mine Construction Costs are calculated by adding the Total Installed Cost (TIC) to additional expenses such as owner’s costs (land acquisition, client team expenses, licenses, and pre-operation costs), pre-operation costs (commissioning, mobilization, staff selection, and training), initial consumables and spare parts, as well as market factors, EPC premiums, and contingencies.
> 
> This detailed financial summary provides a transparent and comprehensive overview of both Development Costs and Mine Construction Costs, ensuring clarity for stakeholders and potential bond investors.

## ✅ **Legit Elements:**

1. **SK1300 Technical Report** (Yellow Jacket 2A):

3. **Authium LLC Bond Document:**
